# Bugs

Known compiler defects: incorrect output, crashes, miscompiles, rejection of valid code, missing diagnostics for unsafe code.

For missing features and language design gaps see `TODO.md`.

Each entry's first line is a **[priority size]** tag plus a one-sentence summary; the full description follows on the next lines. Priority is one of:

- `IMM` -- users hit this in their first non-trivial program (or basic Python idioms);
- `HIGH` -- users hit this regularly in non-trivial programs / stdlib work;
- `MED` -- specific patterns or advanced features;
- `LOW` -- latent / quality / hardening, no current user impact.

Size is one of `small` (hours-1d), `medium` (1-3d), `large` (~week), `xlarge` (bigger).

Entries tagged `deferred: MIR` are gated on the THIR/MIR migration (see `docs/IR_DESIGN.md` Open Questions 9/11/13) and must NOT be patched in the current AST/sema model -- the clean fix lands in the IR. Do not select them for `/tpy-next` or `/tpy-fix-bug`.

## Compiler bugs
- **[MED] A str/bytes view tuple-unpack target first-declared in a FOR-loop body from an owned-temp source, read after the loop, dangles (UAF).** `for i in range(n): a, b = split2(s)` then `print(b)` hoists `b` to the outer scope as `std::string_view` aliasing the per-iteration `auto __tup = split2(...)` temp, which dies each iteration -- the post-loop read is use-after-free (no TPy diagnostic; reads freed memory). Pre-existing (compiles+dangles identically at the merge-base; the tuple-unpack-hoisting fix closed the if/else + match branch variants but not this loop-body one). The branch-predecl owned-promotion (`deduction.promote_predecl_view_targets`) is NOT applied at the for-loop hoist site (`sema/expressions.py:_promote_pending_loop_var`) because a blanket promotion there would also wrongly OWN the safe leaked-loop-var case (`for k, v in pairs: ...; use(k)`, where the iteration var aliases the live container, not a temp). Telling the two apart requires source-lifetime analysis: own the target only when its source is a per-iteration owned `__tup` temp (call/owned source, `not source_binds_by_ref`), keep the view when it aliases a container element that outlives the loop. This is the same precise-escape-analysis deferred to the IR migration (TODO.md). Reproducer: `/tmp/agents/tupuaf/forbody_str_after.py`. Surfaced by /tpy-review (safety-model, architecture-fit) of the tuple-unpack-hoisting fix. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[MED] A reference-type local hoisted across an if/else (or match) branch that aliases a BRANCH-LOCAL source dangles -- scalar form is a SILENT use-after-free, tuple-unpack form is a loud C++ build error.** When a reference-type (record/list/...) local is first-assigned inside both arms of a branch and read after it, codegen hoists it to the outer scope as a `T*` and assigns `name = &(source_element)` per arm. If the source is itself branch-local (declared inside the arm), the pointee dies at arm exit and the hoisted pointer dangles. Two faces:
  - **Scalar (silent UAF):** `if c: o1 = Box(1); a = o1 else: o2 = Box(2); a = o2` then `a.v = 99; print(a.v)` -> `Box* a; ... a = &(o1); ... a = &(o2);` with `o1`/`o2` branch-local. After the branch the pointee is freed; the post-branch read is a use-after-free with no diagnostic (reads freed stack -- "works" by luck). Repro: `/tmp/agents/tupuaf/ref_scalar_branch_local.py`.
  - **Tuple-unpack (loud error):** `if c: a, b = t else: a, b = t` (`t: tuple[Box, Box]`) then `a.v = ...` -- the two arms emit inconsistent forms: the is_new arm aliases (`a = &(unwrap_ref(tuple_elem_ref(std::get<0>(__tup))))`), the is_new=False arm takes the rvalue rebind-slot path (`a = &*(__slot = ...)`, `__slot` a `std::optional<Box>`) -> g++ "no known conversion from Box* to optional<Box>". The codegen asymmetry makes tuple fail to compile rather than silently dangle. Repros: `/tmp/agents/tupuaf/ref_branch_hoist.py` (durable source -- this one is actually safe to alias, see below), `/tmp/agents/tupuaf/ref_branch_local.py` (branch-local).
  
  **DURABLE-source version is correct and must stay:** when the source outlives the branch (a function-scope tuple/var, a param, the leaked for-loop var aliasing the live container), aliasing is right and matches CPython's shared mutation -- the scalar durable case already works (`/tmp/agents/tupuaf/ref_scalar_branch.py`), and the for-loop tuple case works (`/tmp/agents/tupuaf/ref_loopvar_after.py`). So the bug is specifically the BRANCH-LOCAL source; a blanket reject or blanket-own would break the durable case (owning a record also breaks the shared-mutation aliasing CPython has). **CPython note:** CPython survives the branch-local case because the binding keeps the object alive (refcounting); TPy's inline/stack records have no such keep-alive, so the branch-local target would need to *own* (move the element out of the dying source), while the durable target must *alias*. **Root cause:** reference-local hoist-across-branch aliases its source with no check that the source outlives the hoist scope. **Fix shape:** source-lifetime / escape analysis -- alias when the source outlives the target's hoist scope, own (move out) when it does not; this is the reference-type sibling of the str/bytes Option-B over-own item in TODO.md (same analysis, different remedy: str/bytes can conservatively own since a copy is observationally identical, reference types cannot). A conservative sema rejection of branch-local-sourced reference hoists is a possible interim but was deferred (it over-rejects the durable case and is inconsistent with the silently-compiling scalar form). Surfaced by /tpy-fix-bug of the (former) tuple-only S1; the scalar silent-UAF face was found during that analysis. Needs the escape-analysis work (IR) for the precise fix.
- **[MED] A variable first-assigned only inside a `try`/`except` body (on all paths) and read after the block is not pre-declared in the outer scope -- C++ build error. Affects SCALARS too.** `try: x = compute() except OSError: x = 0` then `print(x)` emits `int32_t x = compute()` *inside the try block* and `x = 0` in the catch, with no hoisted `int32_t x;` before the try -- so the post-block `print(x)` fails g++ `'x' was not declared in this scope`. Confirmed for scalar (`/tmp/agents/tupuaf/try_scalar.py`) and tuple-unpack (`/tmp/agents/tupuaf/try_hoist.py`) targets alike: the throw-tier try codegen does not consume `if_branch_decls` / `_emit_branch_decls` to pre-declare branch-first-assigned vars the way the `if`/`elif`/`match` paths do. Likely the same gap for `with` bodies (they share the predecl path, though the `with`-as binding itself works). Pre-existing and independent of the tuple-unpack-hoisting fix (that fix covers if/else + match + while; try/with hoisting was already broken for scalars). Workaround: pre-declare the variable before the `try` (`x = 0` then reassign in the body). No silent miscompile -- C++ build error only. Surfaced by /tpy-review + /tpy-ready of the tuple-unpack-hoisting fix (the de-gate is inert here -- try/with still fail identically). Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] Single-member union narrowing's post-fall-through extraction matches members by `==`, so a generic-alias or cross-module same-short-name member may not match.** `_narrows_to_union_member` (`tpyc/codegen_cpp/statements.py`, in `_gen_if`'s post-narrowing filter) checks `any(m == narrowed for m in union.members)` using `TpyType.__eq__`. If the narrowed fact and the declared member differ in a way `==` doesn't bridge (e.g. a generic-alias member like `list[int]`, or two cross-module records sharing a short name), the extraction is skipped and the original "variant has no member" g++ build error reappears -- a loud failure, not a silent miscompile. Not reproduced; the common nominal-record union case matches fine. Fix shape: match members by the same nominal/qname identity rule codegen uses elsewhere (`same_nominal_symbol_loose`) rather than raw `==`. Surfaced by /tpy-review of `fix-union-else-narrowing`. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] `os.strerror` uses non-thread-safe `std::strerror`.** `tpy::stdlib::os::strerror` (`runtime/cpp/src/stdlib/os_impl.cpp`) wraps `std::strerror`, which on some POSIX platforms returns a pointer into a shared static buffer -- a data race if `os.strerror` is called concurrently from multiple threads. glibc 2.32+ (the primary Linux target) is thread-safe, and CPython's `os.strerror` carries the same property, so this is latent hardening rather than an active bug. Fix shape: use `strerror_r` with a caller-supplied buffer. Surfaced by /tpy-review (runtime-cpp-correctness) of the os process/system batch. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] A module-global narrowed value-Optional (`str | None` / `bytes | None`) iterated inside a coroutine/generator for-loop is silently miscompiled.** The resumable for-source render `_for_src_access` (`tpyc/codegen_cpp/gen_async.py`) calls `_maybe_unwrap_narrowed_optional(expr, gen_expr(expr), is_indirect_name(expr))`; for a module global `is_indirect_name` is True, so the helper early-returns the raw access without the `(*v)` optional-deref, and the global-slot deref is not applied in the begin_end/iter_next strata either (only the `is_async` / `async for` branch handles `is_already_pointer_source`). The frame iterator is built from the wrong access -> wrong runtime result, no diagnostic. Narrow, untested combination (global + narrowed value-Optional + for-loop inside a coro/generator); a function-local param works (the common case, tested). Was a hard C++ build error before the narrowed-value-Optional for-loop fix, so this converts a loud failure into a silent one. Fix shape: apply the global-slot + optional deref in the resumable for-source strata, mirroring the `is_async` branch's `is_already_pointer_source` handling. Surfaced by /tpy-review (codegen-correctness) of the narrowed-Optional for-loop fix. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW] `os.path.realpath` can return a *relative* path when `std::filesystem::absolute()` fails.** `path_realpath` (`runtime/cpp/src/stdlib/os_impl.cpp`) does `abs = absolute(input, ec); if (ec) abs = input;` then `weakly_canonical(abs, ec)` with an `abs.lexically_normal()` fallback. If `absolute()` errors (a pathological cwd -- e.g. the cwd was removed), `abs` stays the un-absolutized relative input and the result can be relative, violating realpath's always-absolute guarantee (CPython joins cwd unconditionally). Rare; no TPy diagnostic. Fix shape: on `absolute()` failure, join `current_path()` manually rather than falling back to the raw input. Surfaced by /tpy-review (runtime-cpp-correctness) of os.path.realpath. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW] `os.path.expanduser` `~user` silently drops the home dir if the passwd entry exceeds the getpw buffer (ERANGE not retried).** `current_home`/`user_home` (`runtime/cpp/src/stdlib/os_impl.cpp`) size the `getpwuid_r`/`getpwnam_r` buffer from `sysconf(_SC_GETPW_R_SIZE_MAX)` (fallback 16384) and treat *any* non-zero return -- including `ERANGE` -- as "not found", returning `""` (-> expanduser leaves `~user` verbatim). A passwd entry larger than the buffer is misreported as a missing user rather than retried with a grown buffer. Practically unreachable (16384 covers real entries); robustness only. Fix shape: loop doubling the buffer on `ERANGE`. Surfaced by /tpy-review (runtime-cpp-correctness) of the pwd binding. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW] `os.environ` snapshot build reads `extern char** environ` without synchronization.** `environ_keys()` (`runtime/cpp/src/stdlib/os_impl.cpp`) walks `::environ`; a concurrent `setenv`/`unsetenv` (which may reallocate the array) is a data race per POSIX. NOT currently reachable -- the snapshot is built once at program startup, before any user thread runs -- so this is a latent hazard only if snapshot construction is ever moved off the startup path. No fix needed today; noted so a future change doesn't reintroduce a live race. Surfaced by /tpy-review (runtime-cpp-correctness).
- **[MED] An `@overload` method on a type imported from another module does not resolve at the call site (`No matching overload`).** A free function with `@overload` stubs resolves correctly across module boundaries, but the same overload set on a *method* does not: calling `inst.get(k)` where `inst`'s type (with an overloaded `get`) is defined in a different module fails with `No matching overload for 'get' with argument types (...)`, even for an arity/type that matches a stub exactly. Same class, same method, same call -- defined and called in *one* module resolves fine; split across modules fails. Confirmed with a 2-module repro (`/tmp/agents/osenv/pkg/{leaf,main}.py`: an `M` with `get(key)->str|None` / `get(key,default)->str` overloads, `inst=M()` in `leaf`, `inst.get("a")` in `main`). Likely the overload group (keyed by `id(first_impl)` in `analyzer.overload_groups`) is registered per-analyzing-module and not carried across the module registry for imported types, so the downstream module sees only one stub (or none) when resolving the call. Workaround: collapse the method to a single non-overloaded signature (e.g. `os.environ.get` is `get(key, default=None) -> str|None`, the `dict.get` shape, instead of the typeshed str / str|None overload pair). Surfaced building `os.environ` (the `_Environ.get` accessor, always called from a downstream user module). Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] The generic-type-instantiation ctor path (`expr.call_type` set) lacks the `_gen_union_arg` dispatch arm the bare-name ctor path has.** `tpyc/codegen_cpp/expressions.py`: the `call_type is not None` constructor loop (the `Stack[Int32](...)`-style typed instantiation path) runs the dynamic-protocol / covariant / protocol / optional-ptr / mutated-temp arms but, unlike the bare-name user-record path, does NOT call `_gen_union_arg`. A generic user-record ctor taking a union param (`SomeGeneric[...](union_arg)` where `__init__` has an `A | B` param needing storage/borrow-variant conversion) would fall to the plain `gen_call_arg` fallback and may emit wrong C++. Source asymmetry is real; **not reproduced** -- constructing a generic user record whose `__init__` takes a conversion-needing union param is awkward, and `Box[A|B](v)` routes through the cpp_template ctor path instead. Fix shape: make this path a third caller of `_gen_record_ctor_args` (the loop the bare-name and raise paths now share); the one genuine difference -- its `TpyArrayLiteral` arm passes `call_type` as the nested-brace hint instead of `ptype` -- can be threaded as an optional `hint_type` param on the helper. Surfaced surveying the raise-arg dispatch fix. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug` (confirm first).
- **[LOW small] The two type-to-C++ rendering paths use different cross-module identity rules, a latent re-collision seam.** `NominalType.to_cpp` keys `native_cpp_names` by short name (`self.name`) with a qname-keyed lookup only as a *preference*; codegen's `TypeResolver.type_to_cpp` (`tpyc/codegen_cpp/types.py`) is qname-aware via `imported_record_qualification_for_type`. `fix-cross-module-type-identity` made the qname path authoritative almost everywhere (and added qname keys to `native_cpp_names` so `to_cpp` disambiguates same-short-name records), but the two paths still look interchangeable while resolving identity differently. Any future emit site that reaches C++ via `to_cpp` for a type whose qname key was *not* registered (e.g. a record reachable only through a path that doesn't populate the qname key) would fall back to the short-name `native_cpp_names` entry and silently reintroduce the B114 same-name collision class. The specific sync point is record registration in `tpyc/codegen_cpp/generator.py` (the per-emit-module `native_cpp_names` population): a new record-registration path that sets the short-name key but forgets the canonical-qname key degrades silently, with no tripwire unless a same-short-name cross-module pair happens to exist in the program -- the most likely regression vector. No active miscompile today (the union/record/constructor paths are already qname-direct); this is a structural hazard. **Fix shape:** make `to_cpp` resolve the cross-module C++ name from the record's qname (mirroring `type_to_cpp` / `error_return_to_cpp`) rather than a short-name map with a qname fallback -- ideally collapsing the two paths. THIR is the natural home (see `docs/IR_DESIGN.md` Open Questions item 13). Surfaced by the `fix-cross-module-type-identity` retrospective. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] `is_return_exception` / `register_return_exception` still identify `ReturnException` types by bare short name: any user class sharing a registered exception's short name is treated as a `ReturnException`.** `register_return_exception` (`tpyc/typesys.py`) stores `record.name` and `is_return_exception` strips its (already-qualified) input back to a bare name before a compilation-global set lookup, so e.g. a user class named `StopIteration`, or a second `JsonError` colliding with `tplib.json`'s, is wrongly classified return-only. The *propagation*-matching half of this cluster (`error_return_matches` conflating two distinct same-short-name exceptions, the C++-build-error symptom) was fixed in `fix-error-return-identity` by comparing the full defining-module qnames. Keying the set by qname is **blocked on a re-export-canonicalization decision**: `StopIteration` is *defined* in `tpy._builtins._exceptions` but registered/looked-up as the public `builtins.StopIteration` (the `__next__` form at `methods.py` and the builtins-preference in `qualify_exception_name` both produce the public qname, while registering from the defining module would produce the private one), so naive qname-keying breaks `StopIteration` recognition (generators/iterators). **Fix shape:** decide whether the canonical identity of a re-exported builtin exception is its defining-module or its public (`builtins`) qname, make `qualify_exception_name` and the set-registration agree on that one form, then key `return_exception_names` (and `BUILTIN_RETURN_EXCEPTIONS`) by it; or replace the bare-name cache with a protocol-implementation check that consults the resolved record (the cache exists only to bridge the pre-`_exceptions`-compiled ordering). Audit finding B113 (residual half). Needs `/tpy-fix-bug` or `/tpy-add-feature` (the canonicalization decision).
- **[LOW small] The type-mismatch diagnostic renders two same-short-name types identically (`expected Tag, got Tag`) when they are distinct cross-module records.** A correct rejection (e.g. passing a `blue.Tag` where a `red.Tag` is expected, now that cross-module same-name records are kept distinct) prints `Type mismatch in argument 't': expected Tag, got Tag` because `str(NominalType)` renders only the short name. Correctness is fine -- the rejection is right -- but the message is unhelpful. **Fix shape:** when the two rendered type strings collide, qualify both with their module (`red.Tag` vs `blue.Tag`) in the `expected X, got Y` formatter(s) in `tpyc/sema/compatibility.py` (many sites; a shared pair-rendering helper). Related to the existing builtin-`BaseException` "expected T, got T" entry below (that one is a *false* rejection from a qname mismatch; this one is a *correct* rejection with a confusing label). Pinned (current behavior) by `tests/cases/imports/error_cross_module_dup_name_mismatch`. Surfaced by `fix-cross-module-type-identity`. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW] `os.stat` nanosecond timestamps overflow (signed UB) past year ~2262.** `tpy::stdlib::os::ts_ns` (`runtime/cpp/src/stdlib/os_impl.cpp`) computes `static_cast<int64_t>(tv_sec) * 1000000000LL + tv_nsec`; for `tv_sec` beyond ~9.2e9 (year 2262) the multiply overflows a signed `int64_t` -- UB the compiler may exploit. The float-seconds fields (`st_mtime` etc.) are unaffected. Benign in practice, but the multiply should saturate or use an unsigned intermediate. Surfaced by /tpy-review (runtime-cpp-correctness) of os stat. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] `float`/`Float32` arithmetic with `bool` is rejected (`3.0 + True`), though CPython treats `bool` as an `int`.** The float operators widen over the `AnyFixedInt` marker protocol (every fixed-width int extends it), but `bool` does not extend `AnyFixedInt`, so `float OP bool` finds no overload ("Invalid operand types"). Rejects-valid, not a miscompile; workaround is an explicit cast (`3.0 + Int32(True)` or `3.0 + int(b)`). Fix shape: either add a `bool` (or a shared `AnyFixedInt`-including-`bool`) overload to the float/Float32 arithmetic dunders, or treat `bool` as conforming where fixed-width ints do. Surfaced by /tpy-review (docs-sync) of the float-fixed-int-arith fix. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW] Subscript-assign / del on a class with `__setitem__`/`__delitem__` but no `__getitem__` is rejected at sema ("Cannot index type ... no `__getitem__`").** CPython allows a write-only / del-only mapping (`m[k] = v` / `del m[k]` need only `__setitem__`/`__delitem__`, not `__getitem__`), but TPy's subscript sema requires `__getitem__` even on the assign/del target path. Workaround: add a `__getitem__`. Surfaced fixing the write-subscript non-int-key dispatch (the inverse-test class needed a `__getitem__` it shouldn't have). Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] A `str` (view) value is not converted to owned at two owned sinks: reassigning a view-typed param, and a variadic `*args: str` collection element.** Both reject valid code with a C++ build error (no TPy diagnostic). (a) `def f(p: str): p = g()` where `g() -> str` emits `std::string p = __param_p;` -- a `std::string` local initialized from the `std::string_view` param, no implicit conversion. (b) Passing a `str` view (a param or a local view) as a variadic `*paths: str` argument emits `std::array<const std::string, N>{p}` -- the varargs pack collects owned `std::string`, and a view doesn't convert. Both are missing view->owned conversion sites, siblings of the `_view_source_to_owned` chokepoint that already covers container-insert / return; the reassigned-param-local target and the varargs-element slot should route through the same helper. Worked around in `lib/tpy/os/path.py` by not reassigning the param and by factoring a non-variadic `_join2` (so `abspath`/`join` avoid feeding a view into the varargs pack). Surfaced building the os.path filesystem tier. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[MED medium, deferred: MIR] A `match` capture of an `A | B` (union) subject fails the C++ build -- two distinct sub-causes (spiked).** The pointer-repr **Optional**-subject half was fixed in `fix-match-capture-indirect-subject` (the capture aliases the pointer directly); the union half remains and is **bigger than a codegen tweak**. (a) **Narrowed union**: `x: A | B = A(1); match x: case q:` -- the assignment narrows `x` to `A`, so the match analyzes as a *record-A* match (q typed `A*`), but `x`'s storage is the pointer-variant `std::variant<A*,B*>` -> `cannot convert std::variant<A*,B*>* to A*`. A narrowing-type-vs-storage-form mismatch. Reproducer `/tmp/agents/c3probe/mu.py`. (b) **Genuine union**: `def m(x: A | B): match x: case q:` -- `q` is hoisted in storage form (value-variant `std::variant<A,B>`) but the subject is borrow form (pointer-variant `std::variant<A*,B*>`) -> `no match for operator=`. This is the **borrow/storage-form duality for union captures** (the IR-gated class, IR_DESIGN OQ9): q must either be hoisted in borrow form (pointer-variant, aliasing the subject) or the bind must convert (`to_value_variant`). Reproducer `/tmp/agents/rev2/union_param.py`. The aliasing form for a union capture is a **design decision** (pointer-variant alias vs value-variant copy-out vs per-variant narrowing), so this is `/tpy-add-feature` (design) more than `/tpy-fix-bug`, and the form half is best sequenced with THIR. Workaround: narrow with `isinstance` per variant before mutating. Surfaced by /tpy-review of the audit-#11 work; root causes spiked.
- **[MED small] Mutating a plain reference-type param through a `match` capture fails the C++ build (param wrongly inferred `const`).** `def f(b: Box): match b: case q: q.v = 99` emits `void f(const Box& b)` -- mutation through the match-capture alias `q` is not propagated back to the matched param, so const inference marks `b` non-mutating; the capture then aliases a `const Box&` and the write fails (`invalid conversion from const Box*`, or `discards qualifiers` under a guard). Toolchain-caught, not silent. The non-generic sibling of the recursive-union case already tracked below; same root cause (mutation tracking must follow a match-arm capture alias back to the matched param/subject). A LOCAL subject works (the local is mutable); only a param subject degrades. Reproducer `/tmp/agents/rev2/param_const.py`. Surfaced writing guarded-capture coverage during the match-capture-indirect-subject fix. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] A walrus binding a reference-type lvalue, read as a comprehension/genexpr element, pushes a borrow pointer into the storage-form result container.** `result = [y for x in items if (y := x).v > 1]` (reference-type `x`) emits `__result.push_back(y)` where `y` is a `Node*` borrow but `__result` is `vector<Node>` -- a g++ type error, no TPy diagnostic. **Pre-existing** (fails identically before the audit-#11 work). The comprehension element read of a walrus-bound pointer-local is not deref'd/converted to storage form, unlike the list-*literal* element sink (`xs = [(y := p)]` compiles). Workaround: bind in a separate statement (`y = x` inside the body is not expressible in a comprehension; rewrite as an explicit loop with `.append`). Fix shape: route the comprehension/genexpr element read through `gen_expr_deref` (the borrow->storage conversion the literal-element sink applies). Surfaced by /tpy-review of the audit-#11 work. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] Generic constructor/function inference cannot bind a type parameter to a `readonly[T]` type.** When the only evidence points to a readonly binding -- an expected type of `Own[Rc[readonly[T]]]` and/or a `Ptr[readonly[T]]` argument against a `Ptr[T_param]` parameter -- inference produces no binding and raises `Cannot infer type arguments for '<Record>'`, even though `<Record>[readonly[T]]` is the obvious solution. Confirmed (DIAG) on the `@auto_readonly` const overload of `Rc.clone`: hint `Own[Rc[readonly[T]]]`, args `['Ptr[_RcCellBase]', 'Ptr[readonly[T]]']`, yet inference fails; the mutable overload (no readonly) infers fine. Root cause is the readonly-stripping in arg/seed handling (`_restore_readonly_arg` / `seed_subst_from_record_pattern` in `tpyc/sema/calls.py`) erasing readonly before the type param is bound, plus no path to bind a param *to* a readonly type. Worked around in `interior`-mutable fields by spelling the construction type explicitly (`Rc[auto_readonly[T]](...)`, transformed per-overload by `@auto_readonly`); if this inference gap is fixed, that explicit marker can be dropped in favor of plain `Rc(...)` inference. Surfaced building the interior-mutable / readonly-`Rc.clone` feature. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small, perf] `key in <readonly dict>` emits a linear `std::ranges::contains` scan instead of the O(1) `.contains` hash lookup.** A membership test on a `readonly[dict[K,V]]` (e.g. `key in self._data` inside a `@readonly`/auto-readonly method) lowers to `std::ranges::contains(this->_data, key)` -- O(n) over the keys -- rather than the dict's O(1) `__contains__`. Root: the membership sema path resolves `__contains__` via `get_record_for_type(right_type)` without unwrapping `ReadonlyType`, so the dict's `contains` is not found and it falls back to the generic iterable scan (the readonly-dict-reads fix made `in` *work* on a readonly dict, via the iterability path, but not via the O(1) accessor). Mutable-dict `in` is O(1); only the readonly receiver degrades. Correctness is fine; perf only. Fix: unwrap `ReadonlyType` at the membership `__contains__` lookup (one site). Surfaced by /tpy-review (codegen-correctness) of `collections.Counter`. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small, deferred: MIR] A `key=` lambda / `copy(key)` over a generic-element tuple list miscompiles for a REFERENCE-type key (value-type keys work).** In `collections.Counter.most_common`, `pairs.append((copy(k), self._data[k]))` building a `list[tuple[T, int]]` then `sorted(pairs, key=lambda kv: -kv[1])` emits `val_or_ptr_t<T>` (borrow form) for the tuple's `T` element where the vector stores `tuple<T, int>` (storage form) -- a C++ type mismatch for a reference-type `T`. Latent: all current `Hashable` types are value types (str/int/...), so Counter keys never trigger it today. Same borrow/storage tuple-form duality as the other OQ9 exhibits (and the deferred key-lambda generic-element entry). Surfaced by /tpy-review (codegen-correctness) of `collections.Counter`. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug` (IR-gated).
- **[LOW small] The `Hashable` C++ concept requires only `__hash__`, but membership (`std::ranges::contains` / `__contains__`) needs `==`.** A user type that satisfies TPy `Hashable` (defines `__hash__`) but not `Equatable` (no `__eq__`) compiles as a dict key / Counter key but fails the C++ build at the membership/contains site. Python's `Hashable` does not formally imply `__eq__` either, but a hashable-without-`__eq__` type is degenerate; the gap is that TPy accepts it as a key then fails late in C++ rather than rejecting at sema (dict key validation should require `Hashable` AND `Equatable`). Pre-existing protocol gap, exposed by `collections.Counter`'s `T: Hashable` `__contains__`. Surfaced by /tpy-review (codegen-correctness). Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[MED small] A generator method that iterates `for k in self._data` AND subscripts `self._data[k]` with the loop var emits ill-formed C++.** The frame-hoisted loop var binds `k = &(*((*__for_it_0))++)` as mutable `T*`, but iterating a dict yields `const T*` -> g++ "invalid conversion from `const std::string*` to `std::string*`". A yield-only generator (`for k in self._data: yield k`) compiles fine; adding any `self._data[k]` subscript inside the generator triggers it (the loop var must be bound `const T*` in the frame). Blocks the natural `collections.Counter.elements()` (`for k in self._data: while j < self._data[k]: yield k`). No TPy diagnostic; C++ build error only. Workaround: build an eager `list[T]` in a non-generator method instead of a lazy generator. Reproducer: `/tmp/agents/gen_dict_sub.py`. Surfaced building `collections.Counter`. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[MED small] Subscript-assignment through a FIELD-access target rejects a readonly-sourced key, while the same through a bare-name target works.** Inside a readonly method, `result._data[copy(k)] = self._data[k]` (where `k` iterates the readonly `self._data`, and `result` is a fresh local of a dict-wrapping class) fails sema with "Cannot pass readonly[T] as mutable T in dict key (expected T)"; the identical pattern with a bare `dict[T,int]` local target (`out[copy(k)] = self._data[k]`) compiles. So the subscript-assignment key check for a field-access target (`obj._data[key] = v`) does not unwrap/accept the key the way the bare-name-target path does (the readonly-dict read fix covered the READ subscript + bare-target paths, not the field-access assignment target). Blocks `collections.Counter`'s `+`/`-`/`&`/`|` operators (they build a fresh `result` Counter and write keys into `result._data`). No miscompile -- sema rejection. Workaround: route writes through a method whose receiver is mutable. Reproducers: `/tmp/agents/ctr_add.py` (fails) vs `/tmp/agents/iso27.py` (bare-target, works). Surfaced building `collections.Counter`. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[MED small] An `and`/`or` whose result type is `bool` but with a RECORD operand emits raw C++ `operator||`/`&&` and fails to build.** Two triggers: (a) two unrelated record types, `dogs[0] or cats[0]` (`Dog`/`Cat`, no common resolved type); (b) a record `or`/`and` a non-record, `a or 0` (`a: Box`) -- both fall to the bool-result branch and emit `a || 0` -> g++ `no match for 'operator||'` (`Box` has no `operator||`). The bool-result logical path emits the raw C++ operator instead of the truthiness-aware lowering `_gen_logical_value` uses for value-result records. No TPy diagnostic; C++ build error only. The value-result path (same-type / `__bool__` records returning the operand) is fine -- only the bool-result path with a record operand breaks. Pre-existing on master, independent of the borrow-type fix (the int-literal -> bool branch is unchanged). Should route the bool-result path through a truthiness conversion for record operands, or emit a clean diagnostic. Surfaced testing the and/or borrow-type fix. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] An `async def` returning `self` copies the receiver instead of borrowing it -- a `@nocopy` receiver fails the C++ build.** `async def __aenter__(self) -> Server: return self` emits `Server __tpy_async_ret = __self;` in the coro frame (by-value copy), so a `@nocopy` `Server` fails "use of deleted ... copy constructor". A *sync* method returning self (`def __enter__(self) -> socket: return self`) borrows correctly; the async return path lowers self by value. Blocks an async context manager `__aenter__` from returning self (CPython's returns the server), so `asyncio.Server.__aenter__` returns None and `async with server as s` is unsupported. Fix: async return of `self` (or any borrow of the receiver) must produce a borrow, not a copy. Workaround: return None and use the bare `async with server:` form. Toolchain-caught. Surfaced by /tpy-review of the asyncio start_server branch. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] A `Callable`/`std::function` argument passed into an `async def`/generator is copied into the coro frame instead of moved.** `_classify_params` (`tpyc/codegen_cpp/gen_async.py`) classifies a `Callable[[...], R]` param as `VALUE` (not `FN`), so `_factory_args_forwarded` forwards it by bare name (no `std::move`); the coro-frame ctor then copy-constructs the `std::function` from the factory local. `std::function` copy is well-defined but non-trivial (potential heap alloc), so e.g. `asyncio.start_server`'s `_accept_loop` copies its `cb` once per spawn instead of moving. Pre-existing (latent until a Callable param reached an async factory); not a safety issue. Fix: forward a last-use `Callable` factory arg with `std::move`. Surfaced by /tpy-review of the async-fn->Callable coercion branch. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] An async-fn ref whose param is a borrow (`T`) coerced to a callable hint whose param is `Own[T]` is accepted by sema but miscompiles -- C++ build error.** `async def handle(reader: StreamReader)` passed where `Callable[[Own[StreamReader]], Own[Cancellable[None]]]` is expected: the function-ref match unwraps both `Ref` and `Own` on param types, so `StreamReader` (borrow) is treated as compatible with `Own[StreamReader]`, but the synthesized coroutine-factory wrapper takes the hint's `Own` param as `StreamReader&&` and `std::move`s it into `handle`, whose actual param is the lvalue ref `StreamReader&` -> g++ "cannot bind non-const lvalue reference ... to an rvalue". The borrow form is also unsound here (the handler's frame would borrow the wrapper-lambda local, dropped when the lambda returns while the coroutine lives on). Fix: in the function-ref-to-callable match, require an `Own[T]` hint param to match an `Own[T]` (not borrow `T`) function param, with a clean sema diagnostic. Workaround: annotate the handler param `Own[T]`. Part of the async-fn->Callable coercion (`feat-async-fn-callable-coercion`); toolchain-caught. Surfaced by the `examples/net/stream_server.py` handler. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] A local assigned inside a `try` and moved after the `try` block is not recognized as last-use-movable -- a `@nocopy`/owned such local fails to move.** `try: conn, addr = await f() except OSError: break` then `Rc.new(conn)` after the try -> "@nocopy type 'socket' cannot be copied ... Only the original owner can be moved at its last use", even though `conn` is bound only on the non-exception path and read only after the try. The conservative "a value read on an exception path stays live across the whole try body" rule over-fires: it keeps a try-*assigned* local live across the try even when it is not read inside the try. Workaround: avoid the `try` (e.g. stop a loop via cancellation rather than catching the error), or move the binding+use entirely inside the try. Distinct from the await-tuple-unpack move (which works without the try). Surfaced building `asyncio.start_server` (catching listener-closed around `sock_accept`). Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] A generic (templated) `async def` awaited as a sub-coroutine loses its template args in the awaiter's frame slot -- C++ build error.** An `async def`/method that is generic OR takes an `Fn`/protocol-typed param (so its coro frame is a template, `__coro_g<F>`) and is `await`ed from another coro emits `std::optional<__coro_g> __sub_N;` in the awaiter frame (no `<F>`), then `__sub_N` is "not declared in this scope" at the poll site. The sub-coro frame-slot type omits the generic frame's template arguments. Reproducer: `async def spawn(factory: Fn[[Int32], Own[Cancellable[None]]], n): asyncio.create_task(factory(n)); await asyncio.sleep(0)` then `await spawn(worker, 7)`. Distinct from the non-generic case, which monomorphizes fine. Blocks the `Fn`-template target of the async-fn-as-coroutine-factory coercion (the `Callable` type-erased target works). Toolchain-caught, not silent. Surfaced probing higher-order async for `start_server`. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] An `Own[@dynamic protocol]` local in an `async def`/generator body is hoisted into the coro frame as abstract by-value storage instead of `unique_ptr` -- C++ build error.** `coro: Own[Cancellable[None]] = factory(n)` inside an `async def` promotes the local to a `tpy::frame_slot<Cancellable<monostate>>` field; the frame's move ctor then does `new Cancellable(...)` on the abstract base -> g++ `invalid new-expression of abstract class type`. An `Own[@dynamic]` value lowers to `std::unique_ptr<P>` everywhere else (params, returns, fields of records); only the coro-frame *local* promotion uses the bare wrapped type. Workaround: don't bind the intermediate -- pass the call inline (`create_task(factory(n))`), which is the common shape. Reachable since `Own[Cancellable[T]]` is user-writable (the implicit-stdlib Cancellable protocol); adjacent to BUGS entry on `await coro` for a protocol-typed local. Toolchain-caught, not silent. Surfaced probing higher-order async for `start_server`. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[MED small] A concrete container passed where a GENERIC recursive-alias instance (`Tree[Int32]`) is expected is accepted by sema but fails the C++ build -- no clean diagnostic.** `type Tree[T] = T | list[Tree[T]]; def depth(t: Tree[Int32]): ...; xs = [1, 2, 3]; depth(xs)` -> g++ `invalid initialization of reference of type 'const Tree<int>&' from 'std::array<int, 3>'`. The std-container guard in `compatibility.py` `_check_compat` only catches a `UnionType`-with-wrapper element difference, not its generic sibling `RecursiveAliasInstanceType`, so `_check_union_member_type_args` accepts the element-wise coercion and codegen can't emit it. The non-generic case is rejected cleanly (a concrete container into a `UnionType` wrapper hits a diagnostic that names the workarounds); the generic sibling falls through to the raw C++ error instead. Fix shape: extend the guard's element check to fire for `RecursiveAliasInstanceType` too (and ideally cover the cross-container `Array`->`list`-member route, which the same-name guard misses). Reproducer: `/tmp/agents/deepconv/genalias.py`. Surfaced surveying recursive-union argument forms. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[MED small] Mutating a recursive-union param's nested container through a `match` binding fails the C++ build (param wrongly inferred `const`) -- CPython mutates and propagates.** `type V = Int32 | list[V]; def mutates(x: V): match x: case list() as items: items.append(1)` emits `void mutates(const V& x)` -- mutation tracking does not follow the `case list() as items` binding back to the param, so const inference marks `x` non-mutating; the match binding `items` is then `const`, and `items.append(1)` is a g++ error (`passing const ... discards qualifiers`). CPython mutates the caller's list in place (`reads(a)` 2 -> `mutates(a)` -> `reads(a)` 3). Toolchain-caught, not silent. Reproducer: `/tmp/agents/deepconv/mutprobe.py`. Fix shape: mutation tracking must propagate a structural mutation of a match-arm capture alias back to the matched param (the alias is a borrow of the subject). Surfaced probing recursive-union param mutation. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] A list literal does not coerce to an `Iterable[T]` (generic protocol) parameter: `f(["a", "b"])` where the param is `Iterable[T]` fails the C++ build.** `c.add_all(["a", "b"])` where `add_all(self, items: Iterable[T])` errors `no matching function ... add_all(<brace-enclosed initializer list>)`. The literal isn't materialized into a concrete `list[T]` (then matched against `Iterable[T]`) at the call boundary. Workaround: bind to a local first (`xs: list[str] = [...]; c.add_all(xs)`). Sibling of the literal-into-`Optional[list]` gap below (collection-literal contextual coercion not firing for a wrapper/protocol target). Surfaced building `collections.Counter`. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[MED small] `dict.get(k, "<lit>")` with a str/bytes-literal default fails the C++ build: the literal default is not coerced to the value type `V`.** `d: dict[str, str]; d.get("k", "<none>")` -> `no matching function for call to dict_get_default(ordered_map<string,string>&, const char[2], const char[7])` -- the `"<none>"` default stays `const char*`/`const char[N]` instead of being coerced to `std::string` (the value type `V`) before the call, so template deduction fails. The named-local workaround (`fb: str = "<none>"; d.get(k, fb)`) compiles. Same family as the `d.get(k, [])` empty-collection-default entry (the `default: V` param's type doesn't flow into the literal). Surfaced fixing the dict-value narrowed-Optional bug. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] A collection literal does not coerce to an `Optional` collection param: `f(["a", "b"])` where the param is `list[str] | None` fails the C++ build.** `csv.DictReader(fp, ["name", "age"])` (param `fieldnames: Own[list[str]] | None = None`) emits a brace-init `{...}` that g++ can't convert to `std::optional<std::vector<std::string>>` ("no known conversion from <brace-enclosed initializer list>"). A plain `list[str]` param takes the literal fine (`DictWriter(out, ["name","age"])` works) -- only the `| None` wrapper breaks it. Workaround: bind the literal to a local first (`fn: list[str] = [...]; DictReader(fp, fn)`). Root: the list-literal materialization targets the bare element type, not the `optional<vector>` storage, so the brace-init is ambiguous. Likely the same family as the dict/set-literal contextual-coercion gap (a plain concrete target hits the peer-unify branch); here the target is `Optional[list]`. Fix: route a collection literal whose expected type is `Optional[C]` through the same materialize-into-`C` path, then lift to the optional. Surfaced building `csv.DictReader`. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[MED small] An `async def` returning a tuple built from named owned (`@nocopy`/`Own`) locals COPIES the elements instead of moving them -- C++ build error.** `async def f() -> tuple[Own[A], Own[B]]: ...; a = A(...); b = B(...); return (a, b)` emits, in the coro frame, `std::tuple<A, B>{(*a), (*b)}` from the `std::optional<A>`/`std::optional<B>` frame slots (lvalues) with no `std::move`, so g++ fails `use of deleted function A(const A&)` for a `@nocopy` element. The async-return tuple-literal path does not apply last-use move to its frame-slot element locals. Workaround (used in `asyncio.open_connection`): build the tuple from fresh rvalues inline (`return (A(...), B(...))`), which C++ move-constructs. Sync-function tuple returns and the single-element / rvalue cases work; only the async-return tuple of multiple *named* owned locals is broken. Same move-out-of-named-owned-local family as the named-source whole-tuple unpack entry below, different site (async return tuple construction). Toolchain-caught, not silent. Surfaced building the asyncio streams layer (`StreamReader`/`StreamWriter` returned from `open_connection`). Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] `KeyError` from a missing dict key carries the message `"KeyError"` instead of the key value -- `str(e)` is `"KeyError"`, CPython's is `"'absent'"`.** A missing-key read (`d["absent"]`) raises a `KeyError` whose *type* is correct (so `except KeyError` works), but `tpy::__getitem__` for `ordered_map` (`runtime/cpp/include/tpy/dunder.hpp` ~167) calls `raise_key_error("KeyError")` with a fixed string rather than the formatted key. CPython reprs the key (`KeyError: 'absent'`). One-sided: catching works, message-comparing diverges. Same shape as the documented `str(KeyError(k))` gap noted for the builtins dict surface. Fix: format the key into the message (gated on the key type being printable/repr-able). Pre-existing; surfaced by /tpy-review (cpython-parity) of the const-container subscript-read fix. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] A `str` local that crosses an `await`/`yield` suspension is promoted to a coro-frame field typed `std::string_view` (borrow form), not owning `std::string` -- it dangles if its source temp is gone by the time it's read.** `host, port = sock.getsockname()` then `await ...` then reading `host`: the frame field `host` is `std::string_view` assigned from `std::get<0>(__tup)` where `__tup` (the getsockname result) is destroyed at the unpack block's end, so the view dangles across the suspension. Latent today -- the M2 reactor test (`tests/cases/async/reactor_echo_accept_connect`) binds `host` but never reads it post-suspension, so there's no miscompile in shipped code; it only bites a program that reads such a `str` local after the suspension. Distinct from the `_SockConnect._addr` FIELD case, which correctly stores `std::string` (storage form) -- the gap is specifically the coro-frame promotion of a `str` *local*, which keeps borrow form. Likely adjacent to the just-merged view-lifetime dangling-holes work (audit #10) -- check whether that pass covers async-frame promotion before opening separate work. Fix shape: a `str`/view local promoted into a coro frame across a suspension must be stored owning (`std::string`), with the boundary copy applied at promotion -- the analog of the str/bytes *param* fix (`is_owned_in_coro_frame` -> `_CoroParamKind.OWNED_COPY` + the simple-generator owned init-capture), but at the local-hoist site (`local_slot` / the resumable-frame local promotion) rather than param classification. Surfaced by /tpy-review (codegen-correctness) of the asyncio M2 branch. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] `json.dumps((1, 2, 3))` (a tuple) is rejected** -- `tuple` is not a `JsonValue` member, so a tuple argument fails the union match. CPython serializes tuples as JSON arrays. Divergence: idiomatic `json.dumps((a, b))` works in CPython. Resolving it means either adding tuple handling to `JsonValue` / `json.dumps` (tuples are heterogeneous value types -- not a clean union member) or a dedicated tuple->array coercion in the json layer. Surfaced surveying json.dumps input forms. Likely `/tpy-add-feature`.
- **[LOW small] `csv.writer(..., doublequote=False)` silently emits malformed CSV for a field containing the quotechar.** With `doublequote=False` and no escapechar, a field that needs quoting and contains the quotechar is wrapped in quotes without escaping the inner quote (`lib/tpy/csv.py` `_format_row`), producing unparseable output; CPython raises `csv.Error("need to escape, but no escapechar set")`. Only on the non-default `doublequote=False` path. Fix needs a small surface decision first: TPy `csv` has no `csv.Error` type -- either add one (matching CPython's hierarchy) or raise `ValueError`. Surfaced by /tpy-review (cpython-parity) of csv v1. Needs `/tpy-add-feature` for the error-surface call, then a localized fix.
- **[LOW small] Module-qualified construction of a generic class fails: `mod.GenericClass(...)` reports "Module has no function 'GenericClass'".** `csv.DictReader(fp)` (a generic `class DictReader[W]` accessed via the module attribute) is rejected, while `from csv import DictReader; DictReader(fp)` resolves and deduces `W` correctly. Module-attribute access resolves functions and non-generic classes but not generic-class construction. Workaround: direct import. Surfaced building `csv.DictReader`. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] `sorted(d.items(), key=...)` (and other element-type inference) on a readonly dict fails: `items()` yields `readonly[value]` for a value-typed V.** `d.items()` on a `readonly[dict[K, int]]` is typed `dict_items[K, readonly[int]]`, so iterating yields `tuple[K, readonly[int]]`; passing it to `sorted(..., key=lambda kv: -kv[1])` makes overload/type-param inference conflict (`tuple[K, readonly[int]]` vs the lambda's `tuple[K, int]`). `readonly[int]` is pointless (a value V read from a dict view is a copy), but it is materialized eagerly by `@auto_readonly` on `items()`/`values()`. The other readonly-dict reads (`.get`, `in`, for-iteration, `d[k]` with a readonly key, readonly *reference*-value reads) now work; only `.items()`/`.values()` projecting readonly onto a VALUE element is unfixed. Workaround: build the pairs list manually (`for k in d: pairs.append((k, d[k]))`) then sort that. **Fix shape:** the collapse must be POSITION-AWARE -- `readonly[value-type]` collapses to the value type only in a value position (tuple/dict-view element), NOT under `Ptr`/`Span`/`SpanIter`/`Ref` where it is the const-pointee/element (a broad `substitute_type_params` collapse breaks `Ptr[readonly[T]]` / ReadOnlyPtr -- verified). Cleanest is making `auto_readonly` value-aware at substitution (resolve `AutoReadonlyType[V]` to bare V when V is value, `readonly[V]` when reference) rather than the eager `apply_auto_readonly`. Surfaced building `collections.Counter`'s `most_common`; deferred from the readonly-dict read-access fix. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[MED small] A generator that takes a protocol-typed parameter cannot be embedded (iterated) inside another resumable frame.** `for row in _parse_rows(fp, ...): ...` where `_parse_rows(fp: Readable) -> Iterator[...]` is itself a generator, placed inside another generator/async body, errors: "a generator with protocol-typed parameters cannot be embedded in a resumable frame yet; bind the elements first (e.g. `xs = list(...)`)". The loud diagnostic + the `list(...)` workaround are correct, but it forces eager materialization for any lazy generator-over-generator pipeline whose inner stage has a protocol param. A generator with a *concrete* (or `Ptr[R]`) param embeds fine. **Blocks lazy `csv.DictReader`** (and any pipeline of protocol-param generators). Fix shape: support protocol-typed params in nested resumable frames (the inner generator's frame field needs the protocol's borrow representation). Surfaced building `csv.DictReader`. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] A `for <var> in <list of str/bytes>` loop inside a resumable frame crashes with an internal error: `resumable-frame local '<var>' still has unresolved type Pending{Str,Bytes}Type after resolve_all`.** A `for name in xs:` over a `list[str]` (a `self.fieldnames` field or a `list[str]` param) -- or `for msg in [b"a", b"b"]:` over a `bytes`-literal list -- inside a generator/async body leaves the loop var with an unresolved `PendingStrType` / `PendingBytesType` in the resumable frame: a Pending resolution sink does not sync `current_ns` for the frame-hoisted loop var. Reproducers: `def g(items: list[str]) -> Iterator[str]: for it in items: yield it`; `async def c(): for m in [b"a\n", b"b\n"]: w.write(m); await w.drain()`. Affects any `Pending*` element type (str/bytes confirmed). Workaround: an index `while` loop, or unroll. Internal error, not a miscompile. Surfaced building csv (str) and the asyncio streams demo (bytes). Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] A `dict`/`set` literal with a coercible-but-not-equal element type rejects-valid instead of applying the runtime-checked `BigInt -> fixed-width int` coercion.** `d: dict[str, Int32] = {"k": make_big()}` / `s: set[Int32] = {make_big()}` (where `make_big() -> int`) is rejected at sema with `Type mismatch in variable 'd': expected Int32, got int`, even though the same `int` value coerces cleanly at scalar (`x: Int32 = make_big()`) and now list/tuple-element positions (the runtime-checked `to_fixed_check`). Root: the dict/set literal analyzers' per-element contextual-coercion branch only fires when the expected element type is a union/optional/Any/recursive-wrapper/covariant-generic shape; a plain concrete target (`Int32`) falls to the `else` peer-unify branch, so the literal's type becomes `dict[str, BigInt]` / `set[BigInt]`, which the strict container-assignment element check then rejects. Distinct from the list/tuple gap that this sibling fix closed -- those *silently miscompiled* (ill-formed C++); dict/set instead *reject-valid* with a clean error, so it is lower severity. Fix shape: widen the contextual branch to any known concrete target and route plain-target elements through the same materialize-narrowing-coercion path `_analyze_array_literal` now uses (`_materialize_narrowing_element_coercion`), verifying the strict element check still rejects user-record slicing. Surfaced fixing the list/tuple aggregate-element coercion (`tests/cases/int/int_to_fixed_in_aggregate`). Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[MED medium] A protocol whose method is typed with a *same-module* recursive type alias fails the C++ build -- the protocol's concept / `@dynamic` base + `Adapter`/`RefAdapter` are emitted before the recursive-alias wrapper struct is declared.** `type Tree[T] = T | list[Tree[T]]` plus `class Counter(Protocol): def count(self, t: Tree[int]) -> Int32: ...` (or the `@dynamic` form) in one module emits, in the header, a virtual `count(Tree<BigInt>&)` / a `Counter` concept referencing `Tree<...>` *before* the same-module `template<...> struct Tree` (or its forward decl) appears -- g++ reports `'Tree' has not been declared` (structural: `concept 'Counter' not in namespace scope`; `@dynamic`: `RefAdapter` pure-virtual `count` not overridden due to the broken signature). Affects both structural and `@dynamic` protocols, generic recursive alias confirmed (non-generic recursive union likely same root, unverified). **Works cross-module**: importing the alias (`from treelib import Tree`) brings the wrapper in complete via the included header, so the ordering is satisfied -- this is the workaround. Toolchain-caught (C++ build error), not silent. Fix shape: in `codegen_cpp/generator.py` emit ordering, the recursive-union wrapper forward-decl/definition for a *local* alias must precede protocol (concept / `@dynamic` base / adapter) emission, mirroring the guarantee the imported-header path already provides. Surfaced by /tpy-ready coverage-hardening probes for generic recursive aliases (the `@dynamic`-protocol-method coverage gap). Covered cross-module by `tests/cases/protocols/dyn_recursive_alias_method`; the same-module case is the gap. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] `TextFile`/`BinaryFile` reads do not surface a genuine I/O error (badbit) -- a read that fails on a device error returns "" / b"" instead of raising `OSError`.** `runtime/cpp/include/tpy/file.hpp`: the full-read path (`read()` / `read(size<0)`) drains via `ss << fs_.rdbuf()` / `istreambuf_iterator` and never checks `fs_.bad()`; the sized-read path (`read(size>=0)`) now preserves badbit across its short-read `clear()` but still doesn't *raise* on it -- a subsequent read just returns empty. CPython raises `OSError` on a real read error. Latent: badbit on a regular file is a hardware/FS fault, near-unreachable in tests; EOF (the common short-read cause) is correctly handled as empty. Fix shape: after each read, if `fs_.bad()` (not merely `eof`/`fail`), `raise_os_error(...)` -- consistently across both the full-read and sized-read paths. Surfaced by /tpy-review (codegen-correctness) of the io `read(size)` change.
- **[MED medium] A two-operand binary expression with side-effecting operands evaluates them right-to-left, diverging from CPython's left-to-right.** `a() + b()` (each appending to a shared list) runs `b()` then `a()` -- TPy emits the C++ expression `a() + b()` and g++ evaluates function-call arguments right-to-left (C++ leaves the order unspecified), so any observable side effect in the operands is sequenced backwards. The *result value* is correct (operator is applied to the right values); only the side-effect *order* diverges. Reproducer: `order=[]; def a(): order.append("a"); return "A"` + same for `b`; `a()+b()` gives `order==['b','a']` (CPython `['a','b']`). Notably the 3+-operand chain `a()+b()+c()` is correct (`['a','b','c']`) -- it routes through a left-to-right fold -- so the bug is specific to the single binary op. Applies to any binary operator (`+`/`-`/`*`/comparisons/etc.) whose two operands both carry side effects, not just str concat. No diagnostic; silent. Affects `<`/`==` operand order, `x[f()] = g()`-style too (unaudited). Fix shape: sequence binary-op operands left-to-right at emit (materialize the left operand into a temp before evaluating the right when either operand is non-trivial), mirroring the chained-comparison statement-expression path. Surfaced authoring the io `read(size)` tests (`fp.read(4) + "|" + fp.read()`). Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[MED] A *simple-peephole* generator that iterates an `Iterable[T]` parameter captures the parameter by reference into its escaping `make_generator` lambda -- a dangling reference (use-after-free) once the factory returns.** `def starmap(func, it): for pair in it: yield func(pair[0], pair[1])` (a single tail-yield over the `it` param -> the simple-generator peephole, not a resumable frame) emits `inline auto starmap(__F0&& func, T_it& it) { return ::tpy::make_generator<R>([func, &it, ...]() ... { ... ::tpy::__iter__(it) ... }); }` -- the lambda captures `&it` (the reference parameter) and the returned generator outlives `starmap`, so `it` dangles. Latent UAF: it happens to read correct values when the generator is consumed immediately in the same statement (the stack slot is not yet clobbered), so tests pass by luck. The *resumable* path is unaffected -- it owns the iterable via a `T_it&&` frame field (so generators with a `break`/`continue` or a non-tail yield, e.g. `takewhile`/`filterfalse`, are safe); only the simple peephole over an `Iterable` param is wrong. No diagnostic; silent UB. **Blocks `itertools.starmap`** (reverted from v1 for this reason). Fix shape: the simple-generator peephole must own the iterable param (capture by value -- which for a reference param copies the reference to the live source -- or move it into the closure), not capture `&param`, when the generator escapes (`make_generator` always escapes). Workaround: force the resumable path (a `break`/`continue` or non-tail yield). Surfaced by /tpy-review of itertools starmap. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[MED, deferred: MIR] A generator holding an `Optional[T]` local across iterations and yielding a tuple of (the narrowed previous value, the current loop var) miscompiles -- the previous-value tuple element silently reads the current loop var.** `def pairwise[T](it): prev: Optional[T] = None; for x in it: (if prev is not None: yield (prev, x)); prev = copy(x)` over `[1,2,3,4]` yields `(2,2),(3,3),(4,4)` instead of `(1,2),(2,3),(3,4)` -- the `prev` element of the yielded tuple reads `x`, not the stored `prev`. Reproduces in a plain `for a, b in pairwise(...)` loop (not a comprehension artifact), so the fault is in the generator's tuple-yield of a frame-held `Optional[T]` element alongside the loop var, not the consumer. No diagnostic; silent wrong output. The single-element `Optional[T]`-across-yield path (e.g. just `yield prev`) is untested here; the miscompile is observed for the tuple-of-(prev, x) shape. **Blocks `itertools.pairwise`** (and any windowed generator that carries a previous value). Workaround: none clean (the natural formulation miscompiles). Surfaced probing itertools v2 feasibility. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[MED small] A generator with a generic `Optional[T]` accumulator written across the loop fails the C++ build (`__slot_1 is not captured` / `std::optional<int>::optional(int*&)`).** `def accumulate[T](it, func): acc: Optional[T] = None; for x in it: (acc = copy(x) if acc is None else func(acc, x)); yield acc` -> g++ errors at the resumable-frame body: the `Optional[T]` accumulator frame slot is mis-emitted (a captured-temp `__slot_1` referenced out of scope, and an `optional<int>` constructed from `int*`). The narrowed `acc` (Optional -> T) assigned back into the Optional slot across the resumable frame is the trigger. No TPy diagnostic; C++ build error only. **Blocks `itertools.accumulate`** (the generic running-fold shape). Workaround: none in the generic generator form. Surfaced probing itertools v2 feasibility. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[MED small] An `@overload`-ed generator crashes the compiler with a bare `Internal error:`.** Declaring `@overload def rep[T](obj: T) -> Iterator[T]: ...` + `@overload def rep[T](obj: T, n: Int32) -> Iterator[T]: ...` with a single sentinel-default implementation crashes sema/codegen (empty-message internal error) when the function body contains `yield`. Non-generator overloaded functions work. **Blocks `itertools.repeat`'s CPython-shape separate `repeat(obj)` / `repeat(obj, n)` overloads** (v1 uses a single negative-`times` sentinel instead) **and the overloaded `islice(it, stop)` / `islice(it, start, stop[, step])` forms.** Workaround: a single signature with sentinel defaults. Surfaced probing itertools v2 feasibility. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[MED small] `d.get(k, [])` (empty-list / collection-literal default) fails to compile: the literal default arg never resolves its element type.** `d: dict[str, list[Int32]]; d.get("k", [])` -> `No matching overload for 'get' with argument types (str, PendingList[???, 0])` -- the `[]` stays a `PendingListType` (no element-type context flows from the `default: V` param to the literal), so overload resolution can't match `get(key, default: V)`. The named-local workaround (`fb: list[Int32] = []; d.get(k, fb)`) compiles. Same expected-type-into-nested-literal hole as the annotated dict-literal-of-list-literals case (audit cluster 14 family); the `default` param's `V` should hint the literal. Reproducer: `/tmp/agents/getb/absent_rvalue.py`. Blocks the rvalue-default path of the dict.get(k,default) aliasing work (Stage B). Surfaced reproducing that work.
- **[LOW small] `_is_const_indirect` may emit `const T*` for a mutable borrow when a `@readonly` `@native` record method returns a reference type.** Now that native `-> V` reference returns alias (the native-record exclusion was removed), `_is_const_indirect` (`tpyc/codegen_cpp/statements.py`) fires for `@readonly` + `call_returns_cpp_ref` and renders the bound local `const T*`; if such a method intentionally exposes a mutable `T&` and the caller wants a mutable `T*`, the borrow is silently over-constrained. No current stdlib case (the affected methods -- e.g. uninit `load` -- pick the non-const C++ overload on a non-const receiver, and `const T*` from a mutable `T&` is a safe narrowing). Latent trap for future native bindings. Surfaced by /tpy-review (codegen-correctness).
- **[LOW small] `@cpp_template` accessor methods on `@native` containers (`list`/`Span`/`Array`/`varargs.unchecked_get`) now alias for reference-type element instantiations, untested.** The native-record exclusion removal generalized aliasing to all native `-> V` reference returns, including `@cpp_template` `operator[]`-style accessors; the `unchecked_get` family is exercised only for value-type elements, so the reference-element aliasing path (`list[SomeRecord].unchecked_get(i)` bound and mutated through) has no regression test. Behavior is believed correct (operator[] returns `T&`); the gap is coverage. Surfaced by /tpy-review (codegen-correctness).
- **[LOW small] Free `@native` functions returning a bare reference type `-> V` value-copy the result where a `V&`-returning C++ function should alias.** `call_returns_cpp_ref` (`tpyc/value_category.py`) bails out for `is_native_import` (free native functions) before the `-> V` vs `-> Own[V]` return-shape check that now governs both user methods and `@native` *record* methods (the native-record exclusion was removed). So a free `@native` func whose hand-written C++ returns `V&` into an argument has its result copied at the call site, not aliased -- the same asymmetry the record-method fix closed, one layer up. Latent: no in-tree free native function currently returns a bare reference `-> V` (they return values, `Own`, `Optional`, or views). Fix: drop the remaining `fi.is_native_import and not fi.is_method` guard in `call_returns_cpp_ref` (the method half is already handled) so free functions also reach the return-shape check, after auditing free native bindings the way the record-method bindings were audited (confirm each bare-`-> V` free function actually returns `V&`, retype by-value ones to `-> Own[V]`). Surfaced removing the native-record return-convention exclusion.
- **[MED medium] Two-arg `dict.get(k, default)` returns a copy of the stored value where CPython returns the stored object -- warned, not yet aliased.** The dict-accessor aliasing work (audit cluster 7) made `items()`/`values()`/`setdefault()` borrow, but two-arg `get` stayed by-value: its result conditionally borrows EITHER the map slot (hit) OR the default argument (miss), and an rvalue default needs a materialization slot whose lifetime is unresolved for loops (per-iteration freshness vs slot reuse) and resumable frames (suspension-crossing borrows). Loud since the same work: a call-site warning fires for reference-type values, silenced by wrapping in `copy()`; value-type results are parity-clean. Fix shape: `V& dict_get_default(m&, key, V& def)` + const overload (value-type V keeps the by-value helper, dispatched by codegen), an emit-site slot for rvalue defaults with defined loop/suspension semantics, and `return_borrows_from={-1, default}` so borrow tracking roots at both sources; typing the return as a bare `-> V` (not `Own[V]`) then makes the result alias with no extra mechanism (the precedent: setdefault is a plain `-> V` native method that aliases because native `-> V` reference returns are treated as `T&`), and the `@copy_returns_warn` decorator is dropped from the overload at the same time (the warning becomes unnecessary once the result aliases). Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] A *simple* generator (single yield in a tail for-loop) iterating a *temporary* generator source emits an undeclared-temporary capture.** `def passthrough(n): for x in gen_repeat(5, n): yield x` (where `gen_repeat` is a generator, so `gen_repeat(5, n)` is a temporary) -> g++ `'__tmp_1' was not declared in this scope`: the peephole lambda capture spells `__src = std::optional<std::decay_t<decltype(gen_repeat<int32_t>(__tmp_1, n))>>()` referencing a `__tmp_1` that is never declared in the lambda's scope. Pre-existing (reproduces without the self-iterator-copy fix); confined to the simple-generator make_generator peephole over a temporary call source. The *resumable* path (multi-yield, or break/continue) handles this correctly. No silent miscompile -- C++ build error. Workaround: bind the source first (`g = gen_repeat(5, n); for x in g: ...`), or make the consumer resumable. Fix shape: the peephole must materialize the temporary source arg before the lambda capture (or stash it like the resumable `__for_src`). Surfaced authoring the resumable self-iterator-copy fix's inverse tests. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[MED small]** A nested def referenced across a suspension point in an async/generator body emits invalid C++ (`error: 'g' was not declared in this scope`). The lambda is a C++ local of the switch case where the `def` statement lands; any call after an intervening `await`/`yield` resumes in a different case. Fix shape: promote nested-def lambdas to frame fields (like other locals living across suspensions), or have sema reject the across-suspension use with a clear diagnostic. Loud (C++ build error), no silent miscompile. Workaround: define the nested def in the same suspension segment as its uses. Surfaced by the finally-lowering fix's nested-def-in-async test.
- **[MED small]** Async finally-with-await (CFG-based finally) is silently skipped when the coroutine frame is dropped mid-suspension WITHOUT cancellation (a frame abandoned outside the executor's cancel-drain path). The frame destructor runs helper-based finallies and `with.__exit__`s, but a finally body that itself suspends cannot run in a destructor. The generator sibling (yield-in-finally) gets a compile-time warning; the async shape does not, because the normal executor path runs such cleanup via cancellation (CancelledError at the suspension) and a blanket warning would flag every legitimate `finally: await ...`. Fix shape: warn only for frames that can escape executor-managed lifetime, or drain-to-completion in the owning slot before drop. CPython runs the finally at GC close.
- **[LOW small]** An `except BaseException:` (or `except GeneratorExit:`) handler around a `yield` does not observe the close when the generator is abandoned: CPython throws GeneratorExit INTO the frame, so such a handler catches it and its body runs; TPy's frame destructor runs only pending finally helpers and `with.__exit__`s (it cannot resume arbitrary handler code inside a destructor), so the handler body is skipped silently. Same can't-resume-in-a-destructor family as the warned yield-in-finally skip, but no diagnostic fires for the handler shape. Fix shape: extend the yield-in-finally warning to yields lexically inside an except-BaseException/GeneratorExit-guarded try, or accept and document. Surfaced by the GeneratorExit-on-close design pass.
- **[LOW small]** `break`/`continue` crossing a CFG-based finally (finally body contains a suspension) silently skips that finally body: `CFGBuilder._build_break/_build_continue` emit a plain Fall to the loop's break/continue BB without routing through `finally_entry_bb` (returns are parked via the pending-return slot; break/continue have no analogous path). Same hole for break inside an except handler of such a try (`ExceptRegion.parent_finally_entry_bb`). Repro in the 2026-06-10 audit (B62): async `for` + `try/finally`-with-await + `break` drops `cleanup 1`; the generator shape shares the emitter. Fix shape: park a pending-jump target the way pending returns are parked. Helper-based finallies are unaffected.
- **[LOW small] An explicitly-annotated `Array[T, N]` comprehension rejected by the stack-build gates fails with a misleading generic type mismatch.** `xs: Array[Int32, 2000] = [i for i in range(2000)]` (over the `_COMP_ARRAY_MAX_SIZE` cap) and `xs: Array[Int32, 4] = [g(i) for i in range(4)]` with `@error_return` `g` inside try/except both error with "expected Array[...], got list[...]" -- the gates in `_try_comp_array_size` (`tpyc/sema/expressions.py`) suppress the PendingListType machinery entirely, so the explicit-annotation resolution rule never runs; both shapes compiled at the pre-builder base via the old loop build. Loud rejection, not a miscompile, but it rejects previously-valid code with a diagnostic that doesn't name the cause. Fix shape needs a design call: either honor the annotation with a non-lambda build for gated shapes (the loop-fill reintroduces the default-ctor side-effect divergence the builder fixed, so only sound where construction is side-effect-free) or emit a targeted error ("cannot stack-build: element calls @error_return / size exceeds cap") at the annotation-mismatch site. Surfaced by the /tpy-ready retrospective challenger.
- **[LOW small] Reading an `@error_return` property outside try/except is not rejected; the same call spelled as a method is.** The unhandled-error_return check (`tpyc/sema/calls.py:4706`) fires for direct and method calls but not for the getter hidden behind a property read: with `@property @error_return(E) def val(...)`, a bare `g.scaled(1)` outside try/except errors with "must be handled with try/except" while the adjacent `g.val` compiles and the unwrap panics at runtime on error instead. Missing safety diagnostic, not a miscompile (codegen's panic fallback is sound). Fix: run the unhandled check on the sema-attached `property_getter_call` like any other resolved call. Surfaced by /tpy-review of the index-sequence builder (the gate-test regeneration flagged only the method line).
- **[LOW small] Repeating a mutable reference-type element has no "copies" diagnostic, asymmetric with the bulk-copy warning.** `[obj] * N` evaluates the element once and copies it into every slot where CPython aliases the same object N times -- mutating `xs[0]` updates all CPython views but only one TPy copy. The divergence is declared in `docs/LANGUAGE_FEATURES.md` (list repetition) but emits no compile-time signal, while the sibling bulk-copy path (`list(b)` over reference elements) warns "copies X elements" with `copy_iter()` as the acknowledgment spelling (`tpyc/sema/calls.py`). Pre-existing for the vector repeat path; the Array repeat path now shares it. Fix shape (design-gated: new warning + acknowledgment spelling need sign-off): warn on repeating a mutable reference-type element, silenced by an explicit per-slot construction or an acknowledgment spelling consistent with `copy_iter`. Surfaced by /tpy-review of the repeat-pair fix.
- **[MED small] A comprehension over a generator yielding `Own[T]` (owned move-only element) copy-inserts each element instead of moving it -- a silent copy for a copyable `T`, a hard C++ error for a `@nocopy` `T`.** `[node for node in g()]` where `g() -> Iterator[Own[Node]]` emits `std::vector<Node>::push_back(const Node&)` (a copy); with `@nocopy class Node` g++ fails `use of deleted function Node(const Node&)` at `construct_at`. The comprehension element binds as `auto&& node = *__beg` (a reference into the yielded slot) and the insert (`__result.push_back(node)`) does not `std::move` it for owned/move-only elements. Pre-existing and general -- affects both *simple* (lambda-peephole) and *resumable* generators identically (the resumable path just became reachable here once comprehensions over resumable generators were enabled), and any owned-element comprehension source. No diagnostic for the copyable case (silent extra copy); loud build error for `@nocopy`. Workaround: build the list with an explicit `for` + `.append()` loop (which moves), or yield non-owned. Fix shape: `std::move` the bound element into the insert when the comprehension element type is owned / non-value move-only (parallel to the for-append move path). Surfaced by /tpy-review of the resumable-generator comprehension fix. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] A generator *expression* over a resumable generator fails to build, though list/set/dict comprehensions over the same generator work.** `sum(x for x in two_then(3))` (where `two_then` is a resumable generator) -> g++ errors: a fresh (rvalue) source hits the non-lvalue genexpr branch that default-constructs `<cpp_iterable>::iterator()` (generators have no nested `::iterator` type, and `cpp_iterable` mis-renders the `Iterator` typing name); an lvalue source hits `no operator++(int)` because the genexpr loop advances with postfix `*__beg++` while `next_iter_mixin`'s `NextIterator` only defines prefix `operator++()`. Comprehensions go through `_gen_comprehension_iife`, which uses prefix `++__beg` and a primed begin()/end() loop, so they work after the begin()/end() mixin fix; `_gen_generator_expression` is the separate make_generator-wrapping path that does not. Fix shape: give the genexpr generator-source case the same begin()/end() driving the comprehension uses (or add postfix `operator++(int)` + a parent-aware default-constructible NextIterator). Workaround: use a list/set/dict comprehension, or materialize via `list(...)`. Surfaced building the itertools stdlib module. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] A list comprehension whose element is itself a list literal hits an internal error before codegen.** `rows = [[i, i + 1] for i in range(3)]` -> `Internal error: PendingListType should be resolved before codegen (literal_id=0)`. The inner list literal's `PendingListType` element is never resolved when it is the element-expression of an outer comprehension (distinct from the "nested comprehensions not yet supported" rejection, which is about nested *generators* `for x .. for y ..`). No user-facing diagnostic; an internal-error crash. Workaround: build the outer list with `.append()` of an annotated inner list. Fix: resolve the inner list-literal's pending element type when resolving the comprehension's element type. Surfaced authoring the comprehension-element-suitability fix.
- **[LOW] A mixed-arm ternary (`g0 if cond else C(9)`) of a reference type copies the lvalue arm instead of aliasing it.** When one arm is a reference lvalue and the other is a fresh rvalue, the ternary's C++ value category is rvalue (one arm materializes a temporary), so codegen renders the local as an owned value -- copying `g0` when `cond` selects it, where CPython aliases. No diagnostic; silent value-vs-reference divergence. Pre-existing and inherent to the value-category model: the borrow-vs-rvalue predicate (`is_rvalue_source` in `value_category.py`, unchanged by the unification on `fix-tuple-unpack-ref-reassign`) uses OR semantics for ternary arms, so a fresh arm forces materialization. The all-lvalue-arms ternary (`g0 if cond else g1`) correctly aliases. **Fix shape:** would require materializing both arms into a common borrow (e.g. a pointer-of-the-selected-arm) rather than a value -- a codegen change to `_gen_if_expr` for non-value reference ternaries, weighed against the cost of the extra indirection. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[MED small] Annotated assignment to an attribute (`self.vals: list[Int32] = [5]` in `__init__`) is rejected with `error: Invalid assignment target`.** CPython accepts annotated attribute targets (PEP 526); this is the idiomatic way to declare-and-init instance fields in `__init__`, so plain Python sources hit it immediately. The unannotated form (`self.vals = [5]`) plus a class-level annotation works, so the workaround is trivial, but the diagnostic does not say that. **Fix shape:** either accept the annotated form (treat the annotation as the field declaration, mirroring the class-level path) or reject with a targeted message pointing at the class-level-annotation convention. Surfaced writing repros for audit item #5. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] The "copies X into owned storage" warning renders unresolved internal type names for list-literal locals.** When the copied variable is an unannotated list literal, the warning prints the pre-resolution placeholder -- `copies PendingList[IntLiteral(1), 3]#1 into owned storage` -- because the coercion-path warning fires during body analysis, before container-literal resolution picks the final `list[T]`/`Array[T, N]` type.
