Cosmetic only, but the leaked `PendingList`/`IntLiteral`/`#N` internals are baked into two `auto_move` test snapshots (`borrowed_by_generator`, `value_result_keeps_move`-adjacent shapes), which will regenerate when this is fixed. **Fix shape:** defer the warning's type rendering (store the literal id, render at diagnostic-flush after resolution) or print the variable name instead of the type for pending literals. Surfaced adding the auto-move borrow-gate tests.
- **[MED small] A full-Optional match capture read after narrowing inside a GENERATOR/async frame does not deref: C++ build error.** `def gen(v: Int32 | None) -> Iterator[Int32]: match v: case x: yield 1; ... else: yield x` (suspending match; `x` is a `std::optional<int>` frame field; the `yield x` sits under an `if x is None: ... else:` narrowing) emits `return x;` without the `*` deref -- `could not convert std::optional<int> to std::expected<int, StopIteration>`. The sync sibling derefs correctly through the same narrowing, so the gap is the resumable frame-field read path not consulting the Optional narrowing facts for value-repr fields bound by match captures. Loud (toolchain-caught), not silent. Surfaced verifying the binds-full-optional capture semantics on the resumable sibling (audit cluster #3 fix).
- **[LOW small] An `as`-binding on an or-pattern arm of an Optional-subject match emits no C++ binding.** `case (None | 5) as v:` on `Int32 | None`: sema binds `v` (typed to the inner type), but the Optional match emitters deliberately skip or-pattern bindings, so a body that reads `v` hits an undeclared-identifier C++ error (loud, not silent). Also semantically unsettled: on the `None` alternative CPython binds `v = None`, which the inner-typed binding cannot represent -- needs the binds-full-optional treatment that plain captures got, or a clean sema rejection. Filed while unifying the match guard lowering (audit cluster #4).
- **[MED, deferred: MIR] Reading / narrowing the UNION ELEMENT of a borrow-form tuple fails the C++ build.** `def f(pair: tuple[Dog | Cat, Int32]): e = pair[0]; if isinstance(e, Dog): return e.bark` -- binding the union element to a local emits `std::variant<Cat*, Dog*> e = ::tpy::to_ptr_variant(std::get<0>(pair))`, wrong twice: in a borrow-form tuple the element is ALREADY a (const) pointer variant `variant<const Cat*, const Dog*>`, so `to_ptr_variant` (which expects a storage variant) is the wrong helper, and the local `e` is typed with mutable pointees that don't match the const source. The tuple-element read reuses the union-FIELD read path (storage -> pointer lift) instead of recognizing the borrow-tuple element is pointer-form already. No TPy diagnostic; C++ build error only. The boundary conversion (passing a `tuple[A|B,...]`, reading non-union elements, param->param passthrough) now works -- only extracting/narrowing the union element itself is broken. **Fix shape:** a union element read from a borrow-form tuple is already pointer-form -- bind the local to the element's const pointer-variant directly (no `to_ptr_variant` lift), and type the local to match (const pointees). Distinct mechanism from the boundary borrow-conversion (subscript read + `ptr_variant_locals` tracking + narrowing). Surfaced by /tpy-fix-bug of the union-tuple-element boundary fix. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[MED, deferred: MIR] The storage / local / field directions of a union tuple element are not wired (only the param/return/passthrough boundary works).** `TupleType._element_is_pointer_repr` (`tpyc/typesys.py`) deliberately EXCLUDES `UnionType`, so `has_pointer_repr_element()` is False for a union-only / union+value tuple -- the storage<->borrow wrap sites (`tuple_to_storage` / `tuple_to_pointer`) never fire for it, and a union-element tuple bound to a local from a storage source, stored in a record field / container, or moved as `Own[tuple[...]]` is not converted between the value-variant storage form and the pointer-variant borrow form. The boundary case works through a different path (`UnionType.to_cpp_return` for the param/return signature + `borrow_value_elem` for the rvalue call-arg, neither gated on `has_pointer_repr_element`). Also unwired: `readonly[tuple[A|B,...]]` params and nullable union elements (`tuple[A|B|None,...]`) beyond the boundary. No TPy diagnostic; C++ build error (or silent miss) only. **Fix shape:** the missing half is the gate -- extend `_element_is_pointer_repr` (and the per-element tuple storage/borrow converters in `runtime/cpp/include/tpy/format.hpp`) to route union elements through `to_ptr_variant`/`to_const_ptr_variant`/`to_value_variant`, with a const-aware (copy, not move) borrow->storage path since a union tuple element is always a const borrow. Related to the union-element extraction entry above and the recursive-wrapper-element exclusion below. Surfaced by /tpy-review of the union-tuple-element boundary fix. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug` (or `/tpy-add-feature`).
- **[LOW] An inherited async construct is rejected when the coro-struct owner's type args are not concretely bound (not yet supported).** Two triggers, same root cause: (a) a MULTI-LEVEL chain -- `class C(B[Int32])` where `B[U](A[U])` and `A[T]` defines `async def fetch(self) -> T`; (b) a GENERIC subclass of a generic base instantiated concretely -- `class Child[U](Box[U])` then `Child[Int32](...)`. In both, `await c.fetch()` (or inherited `async with` / `async for`) is rejected with a clear diagnostic ("multi-level generic inheritance chain is not yet supported") rather than miscompiled. Root cause: `RecordInfo.mro_ancestors` (`compute_mro_ancestors`, `tpyc/typesys.py`) records each base with the *defining* class's own type params, not the receiver's instantiation -- `C.mro_ancestors` holds `A[U]`, and `Child.mro_ancestors` holds `Box[U]` (U unbound) -- so the coro-struct owner carries an unbound `TypeParamRef`, which codegen cannot spell. Async-only: coro-struct naming is the one path that reads ancestor type args off `mro_ancestors` directly; sync method resolution / field access substitute via the *receiver's* own `type_args` and are unaffected (a sync inherited method on `Child[U](Box[U])` instantiated concretely works correctly). The direct single-level case (`IntBox(Box[Int32])` -- a *non-generic* subclass) also works. **Fix shape:** thread the receiver's instantiation args (and each intermediate level's args) into the `mro_ancestors` binding so ancestor type params resolve to concrete args, then drop the codegen guard in `gen_async.py::_sub_struct_qualname`. Surfaced by /tpy-fix-bug of the single-level generic-base inherited-async fix.
- **[LOW small] `with` / `async with` / `async for` walk the MRO for their dunders, but subscript (`__getitem__` / `__setitem__`) lookup does not.** A subclass that inherits `__getitem__` / `__setitem__` from a base (without overriding) silently fails the subscript operator -- the slice-lookup sites in `tpyc/sema/expressions.py` (`record.methods.get("__getitem__")` / `"__setitem__"`, ~lines 3479/3520) are own-only, unlike the MRO-aware `get_method_overloads_with_parents` now used for the with/async-with/async-for manager/iterator dunders. Pre-existing (present on master); same inherited-dunder gap, different operator. **Fix shape:** route the subscript dunder lookups through `get_method_overloads_with_parents` too. Surfaced by /tpy-review (architecture-fit) of the inherited-async fix.
- **[LOW small] A generic record's type-parameter bound is not enforced at a bare annotation that does not construct.** `x: Box[Rc[Int32]]` where `class Box[T: Send]` is accepted with no error even though `Rc[Int32]` violates the `T: Send` bound -- `tpyc/sema/type_ops.py::validate_record_type_args` (reached from variable-annotation validation) checks arity / kind / forward-refs but never consults `record_info.type_param_bounds` via `satisfies_bound`. Construction sites DO enforce it (`_analyze_record_constructor` in `calls.py`, and the generic-factory call path), so a record obtainable only via a constructor/factory (e.g. `Channel` via `channel[T: Send]()`) is covered in practice; the gap is bare annotations with no construction. Affects every bounded generic record, not just Send/Sync markers (protocol/subtype bounds too); no diagnostic. Surfaced designing `Channel[T]` Send enforcement (Phase 4); applies directly to the channel's `Sender`/`Receiver`/`_ChanState` records (which now carry the `T: Send` bound) -- e.g. `rx: Receiver[SharedCache]` as a bare annotation is not rejected, only `channel[SharedCache](...)` at construction is. **Fix shape:** have `validate_record_type_args` validate substituted type args against the record's `type_param_bounds` via `satisfies_bound`, mirroring the construction-site check. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] The `Iterable[Own[T]]` copy-warning suppression is position-agnostic, silently dropping a legitimate warning for multi-arg containers.** The conformance check (`tpyc/sema/compatibility.py`, `actual_provides_own`) suppresses the "copies T elements; use copy_iter()" warning whenever the actual type carries `Own` in ANY type-arg slot. For an actual whose `Own` sits on a non-iterated arg -- e.g. `dict[K, Own[V]]` passed where `Iterable[Own[K]]` is expected (a dict iterates its keys `K`) -- the copy warning for reference-typed `K` is suppressed even though `K` is not owned. Not unsound (the copy still materializes correctly); a missed diagnostic only. Mirrors the pre-existing position-agnosticism of the expected-side strip. **Fix shape:** make the suppression position-aware -- fire only when the actual's `Own`-position signature aligns with the expected's iterated-element slot, not any slot. Surfaced by /tpy-review (safety-model) of the Own/Iterable symmetric-strip fix.
- **[MED small] An overloaded *method* rejects a `Protocol | Concrete` union implementation param that the same shape on a *free function* accepts.** `class Agg: @overload def add(self, xs: Iterable[Own[Int32]]) -> Int32 / @overload def add(self, xs: Int32) -> Int32 / def add(self, xs: Iterable[Own[Int32]] | Int32) -> Int32` errors at the impl signature with `Cannot mix protocol types ('Iterable[Own[Int32]]') with concrete types in a union` (`tpyc/sema/type_ops.py:405-414`), but the byte-identical free function (`def total(xs: Iterable[Own[Int32]] | Int32)`) compiles and runs (exercised by `tests/cases/iterators/own_iter_overloaded_iterable`). The union here is an overload-*dispatch* union (covers the two stubs, narrowed by `isinstance` in the impl body), never materialized as a value-variant, so the protocol+concrete-union restriction should not apply -- the free-function overload path exempts it, the method path does not. Net effect: the entire overloaded-protocol-stub pattern is unavailable on methods (compounds the separate "overloaded generic methods are not supported" gap; see TODO method-parity item). Rejection of valid code; clear diagnostic, no miscompile. **Fix shape:** exempt overload-implementation param types from the protocol+concrete-union check on the method path, mirroring the free-function path. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[MED small] A `readonly[list[T]]` (and likely other `readonly[container]`) parameter of an `async def` / generator fails the C++ build.** `async def peek(items: readonly[list[Box]]) -> Int32: await asyncio.sleep(0); return items[0].n` -> g++ `no matching function for call to 'std::optional<__coro_peek>::emplace(std::array<Box, 1>&)'` at a spurious `__sub_0.emplace((*items))`. The plain (non-`readonly`) `list[Box]` param compiles and runs; only the `readonly[...]` wrap breaks it. The readonly container param is lowered to a borrow (`(*items)`) and the resumable-frame arg handling mis-emplaces it. No TPy diagnostic; C++ build error only. Workaround: drop `readonly` on the container param. Pre-existing and independent of the borrow-alias-across-suspension fix (the same program without any local alias fails identically). The sibling `readonly[tuple[Record,...]]` coro-param bug is fixed (root: readonly was being stripped from a borrow-form tuple param); the `list` case is a distinct mechanism -- the borrow `(*items)` mis-emplace, not a field-type spelling -- so it remains. Surfaced exercising the const-alias path of the tuple-unpack-across-await fix. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] A `Span[T]` by-value parameter of an `async def` / generator fails the C++ build at coroutine-frame construction.** (`bytes` is now FIXED -- a `bytes` coro-param is captured owned via the `OWNED_COPY` kind in `_classify_params`, copying the incoming `BytesView` into the `tpy::bytes` frame field with `bytes_copy` at construction; see `tests/cases/async/stream_readuntil` and the `async def f(data: bytes)` case.) `Span[T]` has the same shape -- the factory supplies a `std::span<T>` borrow that can't bind the storage-form frame field -- but unlike `bytes` it has NO owned counterpart (a span IS a borrow), so `OWNED_COPY` does not apply. Resolving `Span[T]` needs the borrow-form-in-frame route (store `std::span<T>` in the frame, matching the str-view `VALUE` path) which inherits str's coro-param-borrow-escape footgun (a borrow held across a suspension may outlive the caller's storage; see the create_task escape cluster below) -- entangled with the IR-gated borrow-escape work. Workaround: copy into an owned container in a sync `__init__` of a hand-written awaitable. No TPy diagnostic; C++ build error only. Surfaced building the asyncio epoll reactor's `sock_sendall`; bytes resolved building `StreamReader.readuntil`. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug` (lifetime decision) for the `Span` half.
- **[LOW small] The scalar `Own[T]` non-last-use arg check uses `is_type_nocopy`, which misses `__del__` records (copy is C++-deleted but the type isn't `@nocopy`-declared).** `_check_own_param_arg` (`tpyc/sema/calls.py`) gates its clean use-after-move error on `_is_nocopy_type` -> `is_type_nocopy`; a record with a `__del__` (deleted copy ctor) but no `@nocopy` decorator slips through to the warn+auto-copy path, which then emits a copy g++ rejects. The owned-*tuple* arg check added alongside (same file) uses the correct superset `is_type_non_copyable` ("will C++ reject the copy"), so the two diverge on `__del__` types. Pre-existing scalar gap, exposed by the predicate comparison during /tpy-review of the owned-tuple-arg fix. **Fix shape:** switch the scalar `_check_own_param_arg` nocopy gate to `is_type_non_copyable` (align with the tuple path). Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small, deferred: MIR] Return/param asymmetry for a MIXED owned+borrow tuple (`tuple[Own[A], A]` -- one `Own` element, one bare non-`Own` reference element): the `Own` element is honored as a RETURN but not as a PARAM.** A mixed tuple RETURN lowers to per-element forms -- `std::tuple<Point*, Point>` (element 0 a `Point*` borrow, element 1 an owned `Point` by value) -- so the owned element is genuinely owned (working feature; `tests/cases/tuple/tuple_ref_own_mix`). But the same type as a PARAM collapses to a whole-tuple `const std::tuple<Point, const Point*>&` borrow (`decide_param_const` forces const off the bare-ref borrow surface), so the `Own` element is received as a borrow and cannot be moved out. The owned-tuple-param feature deliberately gates its ownership-transfer `std::tuple<...>&&` ABI on `is_owned_movable()` (`has_own_element() and not has_ref_elements()`), which excludes mixed tuples (correct -- a whole-tuple `&&` can't express "own one slot, borrow another"); the gap is that the param ABI lacks the per-element forms the return ABI already has. Consequence: unpacking a mixed tuple param (`a, b = p`) copies the `Own` element -- silent for a copyable element, a raw deleted-copy C++ build error for `@nocopy`. The mixed tuple as a return/local/unpack-of-rvalue works. No TPy diagnostic for the copyable param copy; toolchain-caught for `@nocopy`. **Fix shape:** give mixed tuple params the per-element borrow/own forms the return path uses (`std::tuple<const Point*, Point>&&`), so each owned slot is movable -- the per-place ownership model the destructive-move-out-of-aggregate entry (Safety section) describes. Reproducer: `def consume(p: tuple[Own[A], A]): a, b = p`. Surfaced reviewing the owned-tuple-param feature. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug` (per-place ownership; IR-gated).
- **[MED small] An owned value-variant union local (`Own[A | B]`) moved onward into an `Own[A | B]` param fails the C++ build: the move emits `std::move` on the borrow `variant<A*, B*>` form, which can't bind to the value-variant `variant<A, B>&&` param.** Reproducer: `def make() -> Own[A | B]: return A(1)` then `v = make(); w = consume(v)` where `consume(v: Own[A | B]) -> Own[A | B]`. g++: `invalid initialization of reference of type 'std::variant<A, B>&&' from ... 'std::variant<A*, B*>'`. The owned union local is materialized in borrow (pointer-variant) form, but transferring ownership into an `Own[union]` sink needs the value-variant storage form -- the storage-form<->borrow-form conversion (the `to_ptr_variant` / value-variant family, sibling of the value-variant-union-return entry below) is not applied on the move. Affects BOTH single-assign (`v = make()`) and whole-tuple unpack (`v, _ = pair()`): the rvalue movability path marks `Own[A | B]` movable in both, then the move hits the form mismatch. The optional analog (`Own[P | None]`, pointer-repr) compiles -- only the value-variant union errors. Toolchain-caught, not silent; no TPy diagnostic. Pre-existing -- independent of the owned-tuple-unpack feature, which only made unpack reach the same movable-rvalue path single-assign already used. Surfaced by /tpy-review (safety-model) of owned-tuple-unpack, confirmed pre-existing via /tpy-fix-bug reproduce (single-assign fails identically). **Fix shape:** materialize the value-variant form when moving an owned pointer-variant-union local into an `Own[union]` sink (mirror the storage-form conversion the field/container-init path uses). Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW] `await asyncio.sleep(0)` does not yield to other scheduled tasks before re-waking the awaiter.** A zero-second sleep builds a `SleepFuture` timer that resolves immediately rather than performing a cooperative yield, so `create_task(f()); await asyncio.sleep(0)` reads the awaiter's view of any state `f()` mutates as still-stale (`f()` has not run yet), whereas CPython runs the spawned task up to its first suspension during the `sleep(0)`. Concretely `ran` stays `False` under TPy where CPython sees `True`. No diagnostic; silent scheduling divergence. Workaround: use a non-zero sleep (`sleep(0.01)`), or join the task explicitly (`await gather(...)`). Surfaced choosing the regression shape for the async-global-mutation fix.
- **[MED small] A structural-protocol method whose name is a C++ keyword (`double`, `new`, `delete`, `operator`, ...) emits an unmangled name in the generated concept, breaking the C++ build.** The struct method is correctly mangled (`int32_t double_() const;`) but the concept constraint emits the raw Python name: `concept Doubler = requires(T& t) { { t.double() } -> std::convertible_to<int32_t>; };` -> g++ `error: expected unqualified-id before 'double'`, and the bounded generic call (`run<N>(...)`) then fails its constraint. The concept-constraint emitter does not apply the C++-keyword mangling the struct-method emitter already applies; the two name renderings must agree. Reachable whenever a structural protocol used as a generic bound declares a method named after a C++ keyword. No TPy-level diagnostic -- the user sees only C++ errors. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug` (mirror the struct-method keyword mangle at the concept-constraint emit site). Surfaced by /tpy-fuzzy-test (protocols) during 0.3.0 smoke testing.
- **[LOW] Simple (single-yield) generator aliasing a static-protocol param into a local mishandles the alias.** In a *simple* generator (lambda-peephole path, not the resumable frame), `def echo(it: Iterable[int]): xs = it; for x in xs: yield x` captures the alias `xs` *by value* in the generator lambda, so it COPIES the iterable: mutating the source after the generator is created but before iteration is NOT observed (TPy yields `1 2`; CPython lazily yields `1 2 3`) -- a silent copy divergence with no `while borrowed` warning. The single-*assignment* form emits `auto& xs = it` then captures it by value; the *reassigned* form (`xs = it; xs = it2`) is worse -- it emits `auto* xs = &it; xs = &it2` and then `__iter__(xs)` on a pointer fails the C++ build with no TPy diagnostic. Both are specific to the simple-generator path; the resumable (multi-yield) path handles single-assignment aliases correctly (forwarding to the captured param) and cleanly rejects reassignment. Part of the "simple proto-param generators are a separate concern" gap (TODO.md generator section: the lambda peephole tries `begin()/end()` semantics rather than the deduced-template-arg capture). Fix: the simple-generator peephole should capture a proto-param alias by reference (borrow) like the direct param, and reject (or pointer-handle correctly) the reassigned form. Surfaced building the proto-param-alias resumable feature.
- **[LOW] Resumable match-arm binding has no `generator_storage_name` redirect for forwarded proto-param aliases (latent, unreachable today).** Forwarded proto-param aliases (`xs = it`, see the proto-param-alias feature) are added to `ctx.generator_field_names` so name reads route through `ctx.generator_storage_name(name)` to the backing param. The match-arm field-write path (`_emit_match_binding`, `tpyc/codegen_cpp/match.py:970`) tests `generator_field_names` membership but does NOT apply the storage-name redirect, so if a forwarded local were ever a match-capture *target* it would emit a write to a non-existent field. Unreachable today: a forwarded local is a static-protocol-typed alias, and a protocol-typed local cannot be a match-capture target. Guarded by a comment at the site. Fix when (if) it becomes reachable: route the bound name through `generator_storage_name` (and reject writes to a forwarded alias, which has no storage). Surfaced by /tpy-review (safety-model) of the proto-param-alias feature.
- **[MED small] `print()` of a `Ptr[T]` field emits the raw pointer value (`0` for null) instead of `None` / a Python-style repr.** `n.nxt = None; print(n.nxt)` outputs `0` rather than `None`; a non-null `Ptr[T]` would print a nondeterministic address. Inconsistent with `Optional[T]` fields, which print `None` correctly. `x is None` narrowing on the Ptr is unaffected (only the print/`__str__`/`__repr__` representation is wrong). Incorrect output, no diagnostic. Fix: route `Ptr[T]` through the Optional-style printer (null -> `None`; non-null -> the pointee's repr, or at least a stable non-address form). Surfaced by /tpy-fuzzy-test (classes-ownership) during 0.3.0 smoke testing.
- **[LOW] Coro-struct topological emit orders direct `await` deps only, not `async with` / `async for` sub-futures.** `_emit_resumable_structs` (`tpyc/codegen_cpp/generator.py`) orders async coro struct definitions so each inline-awaited callee precedes its awaiter, but `_inline_await_targets` collects edges from `TpyAwait` nodes only. `async with` and `async for` also embed by-value sub-future fields -- the context manager's `__aenter__`/`__aexit__` coro structs and the async iterator's `__anext__` coro struct (computed at prescan as `async_with_struct_names` / `async_for_struct_names`) -- but produce no `TpyAwait`, so their ordering edges are missing. Reachable when an async **method** uses `async with`/`async for` over a *same-module* record whose dunder is an async method emitted **after** the awaiter (record/method declaration order); the awaiter's `std::optional<__coro_Cm___aenter__>` field then sees an incomplete type (`invalid use of incomplete type`). Niche: the stable seed order (methods first, in declaration order) already orders the common shapes (a free-function awaiter, or a dunder whose record is declared earlier). **Robust fix:** source the per-coro sub-future dependency set from the CFG (the authoritative set: direct `await` + `async with` + `async for`, all of which carry a `sub_field_cpp_type`) instead of the `_inline_await_targets` AST walk -- this also removes the AST-vs-CFG dual source of truth. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`. Surfaced by /tpy-review of the coro-struct dependency-ordering fix.
- **[LOW small]** The Python-clamp logic in `varargs<T>::slice` (`runtime/cpp/include/tpy/varargs.hpp`, both the value and non-value specializations) does the negative-index adjustment `i += size_` / `j += size_` in `int32_t`; a near-`INT32_MIN` bound combined with a large `size_` overflows (signed UB). Impractical (a vararg never holds INT32_MAX elements) and pre-existing in spirit -- it mirrors the non-value `slice` and the `BasicSlice` clamp; the value-type `slice` added with the `varargs[T]` body-view type copy-pasted the same pattern. Fix: widen to `std::ptrdiff_t` before the adjustment (as the generic `list_slice` in `container_ops.hpp` already does). Surfaced by /tpy-review (runtime-cpp) of the varargs body-view type.
- **[MED small]** A non-value value coerced into a recursive-union-wrapper *tuple element of a local* fails the C++ build instead of giving a clean diagnostic. `def f(n: Int32) -> tuple[Tree[Int32], Int32]: pair = (n, 0); return pair` (or `pair: tuple[Tree[Int32], Int32] = (n, 0)`) emits `std::tuple<int,int>` -> `std::tuple<Tree<int32_t>&, int>` coercion / `tuple<Tree&,int>{n, 0}` construction that g++ rejects (`could not convert` / `no matching function`). Toolchain-caught, NOT a silent dangle. The literal form `return (n, 0)` is correctly rejected with the clean wrapper-element dangle diagnostic; only the tuple-*local* form hits the raw C++ error. Same return-time-tuple-coercion family as the wrapper-as-tuple-element storage/borrow conversion gaps. This is one symptom of the literal/deferred-path divergence in the fresh-member fact derivation: the literal path's per-element loop catches a value coerced into a wrapper element (via the pre-coercion source type), the name-based deferred flag does not -- the eventual single-dataflow-fact fix should unify both rather than patch the coerce case in isolation. Surfaced building repros during /tpy-fix-bug of the bare-name wrapper-tuple-dangle.
- **[LOW small] A *durable* recursive-union-wrapper member of a bound-local tuple still copies (or fails to build) instead of sharing.** The tuple borrow-pointer unification deliberately kept recursive-union wrappers on their nominal `X&` borrow form (excluded from `_element_is_pointer_repr`), so `def f(t: Tree[Int32]) -> tuple[Tree[Int32], Int32]: pair = (t, 0); return pair` remains the wrapper analogue of the (now-fixed) durable non-wrapper case: the bound local does not alias the wrapper member. The *fresh* wrapper member IS rejected (the fresh check and `_tuple_elem_still_borrow` are wrapper-aware); closing the durable sibling means extending the `T*` borrow form (and its construct/access/convert paths) to wrapper elements. Surfaced merging the wrapper-fresh-dangle fix over the durable-member rejection; scoped out of the unification branch.
- **[LOW small] Mutating through a tuple local that aliases an EXPLICITLY-readonly param's element fails the C++ build with no TPy diagnostic.** `def f(items: readonly[list[tuple[Int32, Box]]]): t = items[0]; t[1].val = 99` -- the aliasing binding is `auto&&` over const storage, and the write trips a C++ const error ("assignment of member ... in read-only object") instead of the sema "cannot mutate readonly" rejection the direct form gets. The const-INFERRED variant of this entry was fixed by the storage-provenance work (tuple aliases now register element borrows; write-through marks the source param mutated, so it stays non-const -- `tests/cases/tuple/tuple_storage_local_alias`); the residual is only the explicit `readonly[...]` annotation, where the right outcome is a sema rejection of the write through the alias. Toolchain-caught, not silent. Reproducer: `/tmp/agents/tuple-soundness/probes/readonly_alias_mut.py`. Surfaced probing the /tpy-review borrow-local reassignment fix; narrowed by the storage-provenance fixes.
- **[LOW small, deferred: MIR] An RVALUE generic tuple element (call result of type `T` in a tuple literal with a `val_or_ptr_t<T>` slot) is not routed through the borrow helpers.** `(i, fn(x))` under a generic `tuple[Int32, T]` target falls to the plain value path; at a non-value instantiation the `T` value cannot convert to the `T*` slot (C++ error, no TPy diagnostic). Lvalue generic elements go through `to_val_or_ptr` and work. Same dangling-rvalue family as the concrete rvalue-into-borrow-slot entry below; the concrete case routes through `tuple_value_to_borrow`, the generic case needs the same temp-materializing treatment. Filed during the val_or_ptr generic-tuple work.
- **[LOW small] A reassigned tuple local that mixes an owning-call binding with a storage alias inside a GENERATOR/ASYNC body is not yet handled.** The sync owning/alias mix (`if c: t = make_pair() else: t = h.pair`, straight-line rebind, branch-mixed) is fixed on `tuple-rvalue-slot`: the local stays borrow form (`std::tuple<..., T*>`), an owning-call RHS materializes into a hoisted `std::optional<std::tuple<..., T>>` slot lifted via `tuple_to_pointer`, the owning fact is flow-correct (FlowFacts snapshot + UNION merge), and `_reject_owning_alias_rebind` is deleted. A *non-reassigned* owning tuple local in a resumable body is also fixed (storage `tpy::frame_slot<std::tuple<..., T>>` field). The residual is the REASSIGNED owning+alias mix in a resumable body: it needs BOTH a `frame_slot<std::tuple<..., T>>` slot field (for the owning RHS) AND a borrow `std::tuple<..., T*>` field (for the alias), whereas a frame local currently gets one field. Today such a local would either trip the rvalue-lift static_assert or copy. Surfaced implementing the owning/alias-mix umbrella; the broad case is resolved, this resumable-reassigned sub-case is scoped out.
- **[LOW small] A multi-source borrow-form tuple local with differing source const-ness, written through, fails the C++ build (independent of `Optional`).** `def alias2(h: Holder, h2: Holder): t: tuple[Int32, Box] = h.pair; t = h2.pair; t[1].val = 99` -- the write-through marks only `h2` mutated (the `t->h.pair` binding is overwritten before any write), so `h` stays const; `_compute_borrow_tuple_const`'s OR-over-all-sources then marks the local const, emitting `std::tuple<Int32, const Box*> t` and g++ rejects the write (`passing 'const ...' as 'this' discards qualifiers`) through the live `h2` alias. Toolchain-caught, not silent. The const verdict should track per-reaching-binding (which source is live at the write), not OR across all sources. Surfaced implementing the Optional-of-borrow-tuple shape (the const inference is shared); reproduces on the bare `tuple[...]` form with no `Optional`. (Same const family: `gen_async.py` save/restores `borrow_form_tuple_locals` / `optional_borrow_tuple_locals` around a nested coro body but NOT the matching `const_*` sets, so an outer sync function's const-element election for a (nullable) borrow tuple local is trashed if a nested `async def` precedes those locals in source order -- edge-gated, no current reproducer.) (The spurious *copy warning* and the *type-record split* that used to accompany the `Own`-annotated form of this shape were fixed by the reassigned per-element-`Own` collapse unification; the `Own`-annotated decl now errors at the redundant-`Own` rejection before reaching this const build error, so only the bare-`tuple[...]` form below remains a live const-OR reproducer.)
- **[MED small] Silent divergence: a dict/list LITERAL (and `list.append`) deep-copies a stored tuple's reference members with no warning.** `items: list[tuple[Int32, Box]] = [(1, Box(5))]; d: dict[str, tuple[Int32, Box]] = {"k": items[0]}; d["k"][1].val = 99` leaves `items[0][1].val` at 5 (CPython: 99) -- the dict value slot is storage form, the element copy is silent. The `dict(pairs)` CONSTRUCTOR form warns, and `d[k] = v` / `lst[i] = v` ASSIGNMENTS now warn per pointer-repr element (the tuple branch of the field/container copy detection in `_analyze_assign`); the container-LITERAL element path and method-call inserts (`append`/`insert`) still don't route through it. Same storage-copy family as the warned field cases; pre-existing (predates the tuple borrow-pointer unification, which made the contrast visible by making locals alias). Fix shape: fire the same per-element warning from the container-literal element conversion and the insert-method arg coercion. Reproducer: `/tmp/agents/tuple-soundness/probes/dict_tuple_copy.py`. Surfaced by /tpy-review (cpython-parity) of the storage-provenance pass.
- **[MED small] An `@error_return` function returning a borrowed `T | None` emits ill-formed C++ -- compile failure, not silent.** `@error_return(E) def maybe(self, ok: bool) -> list[Int32] | None: return self.items` lowers the return ABI to `std::expected<::tpy::val_or_ref<std::optional<std::vector<int32_t>>>, E>` (the `val_or_ref` wraps the whole Optional), but the body emits `return &(this->items);` (a `vector*`) for the borrow leg and `return nullptr;` for the None leg -- neither converts. The ABI wrap (`_resolve_return_type`, `tpyc/codegen_cpp/functions.py`) and the return-site emit disagree about where the nullability lives; the borrowed-Optional-return shape needs its own design (a `val_or_ref`-of-inner + null state, or a plain pointer payload). Reproducer: `/tmp/agents/er_alias/opt_ret.py`. Surfaced during /tpy-fix-bug of the @error_return borrow-alias fix.
- **[MED small] Tuple-unpack of an `@error_return` call inside `try` emits ill-formed C++ -- compile failure, not silent.** `try: a, b = pair(5)` (where `pair` is `@error_return(E)` returning a tuple) emits the `__try_tmp_N` declaration inside its own brace block but reads it after the block closes (`'__try_tmp_N' was not declared in this scope`), and re-declares the unpacked locals after the error goto (`jump to label crosses initialization`). The path is `_gen_tuple_unpack`'s error-return intercept in `tpyc/codegen_cpp/statements.py` (~line 2912). When fixing: the success payload must route through `_error_return_success_expr` (`unwrap_ref_move`) so the owned-payload move normalization applies here too -- the intercept still uses the copy-form `unwrap_ref` -- and a tuple-of-borrows payload needs a storage->borrow element conversion (see the `next()` tuple entry below). Reproducer: `/tmp/agents/er_move/tup.py`. Surfaced during /tpy-fix-bug of the @error_return result-into-storage move.
- **[LOW small] Statement-level unwrap of a tuple-of-borrows payload fails the C++ build.** `kv = next(it)` over a `dict[K, Rec].items()` iterator inside `try` assigns the unwrapped payload tuple to the borrow-form local `std::tuple<K, Rec*>` with no element conversion (post-proxy-iterator shape: g++ rejects the tuple `operator=` on const-assignability of the element slots). Sibling of the storage-vs-borrow tuple conversion family; the fix is a per-element `tuple_to_pointer`-style conversion at the unwrap site, plus a function-scope materialization slot for any owned elements. Reproducer: `/tmp/agents/cluster7/next_items_try.py`. Surfaced during /tpy-fix-bug of the @error_return result-into-storage move; error shape updated by the dict-accessor aliasing work.
- **[LOW small] A list-literal `return` from an `@error_return` function with an `Own[list[T]]` return type emits a braced-init that cannot convert to the `std::expected`.** `@error_return(E) def make(n: Int32) -> Own[list[Int32]]: ... return [n, n + 1]` emits `return {n, ...};` -- ill-formed for `std::expected<std::vector<int32_t>, E>` (two user conversions). Binding the literal to a local and returning the local works (and now moves). Fix: wrap the literal in an explicit `std::vector<T>{...}` (or construct in place) when the enclosing function is `@error_return`. Reproducer: `/tmp/agents/er_move/owned_list2.py`. Surfaced during /tpy-fix-bug of the @error_return result-into-storage move.
- **[MED] Silent capture-by-value divergence on in-place MUTATION of an escaping-closure capture (the reassignment half now warns).** An escaping (`Callable`) closure captures a local by value, so the closure holds a snapshot taken at creation; CPython late-binds (captures the variable) and observes later changes. The *reassignment* half (`k = 10; f = hold(lambda: k); k = 20; print(f())` -> `10` in TPy vs `20` in CPython, and counters/aug-assign) now emits a compile-time warning (`_warn_stale_value_captures` in `tpyc/sema/analyzer.py`, covering escaping lambdas and nested defs). The remaining gap is **in-place mutation** of a captured container/record: `xs = [1, 2]; g = hold(lambda: xs[0]); xs[0] = 99; print(g())` prints `1` in TPy vs `99` in CPython with no warning -- the divergence is silent. Mechanism: codegen emits a by-value capture (`[xs]`, not `[&xs]`) because a by-reference capture into an escaping closure would dangle (`docs/CLOSURES_CALLABLE_DESIGN.md` capture table); TPy's no-cell zero-cost model has no shared cell, so the snapshot severs the alias. **Not fixable to match CPython** in the zero-cost model; the remedy is to extend the existing warning to the mutation case. **Blocked on** the mutation-after-capture fact, which is not tracked today (`closure_written_names` collects only `nonlocal`/`global` rebinds, not in-place field/element writes from nested-def bodies) -- see the `closure_written_names` "Closures mutating captures without `nonlocal`" entry in this section. Workaround: bind a fresh non-mutated snapshot (`snap = copy(xs)`) for the closure to capture. (Non-escaping `Fn` closures capture by reference and do NOT diverge -- escaping-`Callable` only.)
- **[MED] Loop-variable capture into an escaping closure diverges silently from CPython, with no warning.** `for k in range(n): funcs.append(hold(lambda: k))` then calling the collected closures gives `0,1,...,n-1` in TPy but `n-1,n-1,...` in CPython. Each TPy closure snapshots the loop var by value at the iteration it is created; CPython shares one cell, so every closure sees the final value (the classic Python loop-capture gotcha). TPy's result is arguably the more intuitive one, but it is still a divergence and there is no diagnostic. The `_warn_stale_value_captures` reassignment warning deliberately does NOT cover this: the rebinding is the loop back-edge (the loop var is re-bound at the closure's own statement, not a later one), and a fix needs loop-back awareness plus a decision on the reversed-divergence direction (warn even though TPy is "more correct"?). Workaround: bind a per-iteration fresh local is no help (same edge); capture into a per-iteration default arg or restructure. Reachable today via `hold(lambda: k)` (the `hold` Callable context lets the lambda infer its type inside the loop). Surfaced by /tpy-review (cpython-parity) of the escaping-capture warning.
- **[LOW small] A tuple-unpack discard of a pure expression emits a bare `0;` statement that trips `-Wunused-value` under `-Werror`.** `k, _ = (2, 0)` lowers to `k = 2; 0;` -- the discarded second element (a literal, or any side-effect-free expression) is emitted as a bare expression-statement, which g++ rejects under the test/CI `-Werror -Wall` flag set (`tpyc/compiler.py`). Normal builds (no `-Werror`) only warn. A discard of a value with side effects (a call) is presumably emitted as the call; only the pure-expression discard degrades. Fix shape: cast pure discards to `void` (the `-Wunused-value` guard codegen already applies elsewhere -- `tpyc/codegen_cpp/expressions.py`) or drop the statement entirely. Surfaced writing a tuple-unpack regression for the escaping-capture warning. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[MED small]** A *mutable* recursive-union wrapper param (now possible since Part B routes wrapper params through const-inference) that is variant-narrowed via `std::get` emits a `const auto&` alias, suppressing writes through the narrowed alternative -> C++ rejects a write that valid Python allows. `_record_field_conditions` / the variant-narrowing alias in `tpyc/codegen_cpp/statements.py` (~`is_const = ... needs_wrapper()`) assumes every wrapper param is `const X&` (true under the old hardcode, false after Part B). Narrow: needs a mutable wrapper param + variant-narrow + write through the narrowed alternative. Fix: derive the alias const-ness from the param's resolved const-ness, not from `needs_wrapper()`. Surfaced by /tpy-review (architecture-fit) of the recursive-union wrapper migration.
- **[LOW small]** `_call_returns_cpp_ref` (`tpyc/codegen_cpp/context.py`) excludes all `UnionType`, so a `@readonly` method returning a *non-generic* recursive-union wrapper (now `Tree&` in C++) has its result copied into a local (`Tree t = m()`) instead of bound `const Tree& t` -- const lost, an alias becomes a copy. Safe (no dangle), but the same borrow-into-owned-storage class as the (now fixed) `@error_return` borrow-copy bug; generic wrappers (`RecursiveAliasInstanceType`) are handled correctly, so the gap is non-generic-wrapper-specific. The predicate now also gates the `@error_return` borrow-alias binding (`_error_return_result_aliases` and the branch-decl pointer form both consult `call_returns_cpp_ref`), so a non-generic wrapper borrow from an `@error_return` callable still copies into its local -- closing this entry fixes that residual for free. Fix: recognize a non-generic wrapper `UnionType` return as a C++ reference in `_call_returns_cpp_ref` (it now lowers to `X&` post-Family-C). Note `_call_returns_cpp_ref` is a parallel predicate to `match.py`'s `_returns_bare_reference` (which already gets the wrapper/ptr-variant union distinction right via `needs_wrapper()`); the clean fix unifies both onto one `to_cpp_return`-aware helper rather than patching `_call_returns_cpp_ref` in isolation. Surfaced by /tpy-review of the recursive-union wrapper migration.
- **[MED small]** Returning a *value-variant* union (a field, or any storage-form union) as a *pointer-variant* union from a function/method emits the whole-variant address instead of converting per-alternative. `class S: pet: Dog | Cat; def pick(self) -> Dog | Cat: return self.pet` -> C++ `could not convert '&...pet' from 'std::variant<Cat, Dog>*' to 'std::variant<Cat*, Dog*>'`: the field is stored value-variant `std::variant<Cat, Dog>` but the return borrow-form is `std::variant<Cat*, Dog*>`, and codegen takes `&` of the whole variant rather than building a pointer-variant from the active alternative's address. Storage-form -> borrow-form conversion gap for unions (sibling of the tuple/Optional storage-vs-borrow conversion family). No match involved. Workaround: return `Own[Dog | Cat]` (by value) or restructure. Surfaced while building a `match`-on-call repro during /tpy-fix-bug of the match-subject binding bug.
- **[LOW small]** Constructing a generic recursive alias instance via an explicit `Own[Tree[T]](<list literal>)` in a call/ctor-arg position fails C++ build: `Holder(Own[Tree[Int32]]([1, 2]))` -> `no matching function for call to 'Tree<int>::Tree(<brace-enclosed initializer list>)'`. A typed-local construction (`tree: Tree[Int32] = [1, 2, 3]`) works, so the gap is the explicit-`Own[...]`-of-list-literal form not routing through the alias's forwarding constructor. Workaround: construct via a typed local and pass that. Surfaced while building a generic-wrapper repro during /tpy-fix-bug.
- **[LOW small]** A fresh `Own[T]` tuple element bound via a local literal is *copied* into the value tuple at the return boundary instead of *moved*. `def f() -> tuple[Own[Tree[Int32]], Int32]: leaf: Tree[Int32] = 5; pair = (leaf, 0); return pair` builds `pair` as borrow form `std::tuple<Tree&, int>{leaf, 0}`, then `return pair` copy-constructs `leaf` through the reference into the storage-form `std::tuple<Tree, int>` rather than moving the last-use local. Output is correct (a deep copy of the wrapper tree), so this is **perf-only, not a dangle** -- same tuple-local borrow-vs-storage conversion family as the durable-local-use `[MED small]` entry above, but for the fresh-`Own`-element case where the missed transfer is a copy instead of a move. Exercised (correctly) by `tests/cases/tuple/tuple_wrapper_member_ok` (`own_escape`); surfaced by /tpy-review (codegen-correctness) of the wrapper-tuple-dangle fix.
- **[LOW small]** `gen_async._classify_params` reads the materialized union-param const verdict (`FunctionInfo.deep_const_borrow_params`) imperfectly in two latent cases, either of which would emit a mutable resumable frame field while the call site / body are deep-const (C++ type mismatch). (1) **Overload:** the method lookup is `_ri.get_method(func.name)`, which returns `overloads[0]`; a generator/async *method* with multiple overloads disagreeing on a union param's const-ness reads overload 0's verdict for all. (2) **Free fn / None fallback:** for a free function `record_name` is None so `_deep_const` stays None and const-ness falls back to `actual is not ptype_inner` -- correct today only because explicit `@readonly` wraps free-fn params in `ReadonlyType` (sema `_normalize_param_type` write-back) and free functions are never *inferred* readonly; if `get_record`/`get_method` returns None the field also silently goes mutable. Both inert today (no overloaded generator/async method with a union param is reachable; the free-fn path is covered by the ReadonlyType wrap). Fix: route the free-fn path through its `FunctionInfo` verdict too, and resolve the overload's actual verdict (the call site uses the *resolved* `method_info`, so mirror that). Surfaced by `/tpy-review` of `fix-readonly-union-param-const`.
- **[LOW medium]** Codegen cannot represent a non-value member nested inside an inner tuple: the tuple borrow/storage conversion (`tuple_to_pointer` / `tuple_to_storage` and the slot layout) is *flat* -- it does not recurse, so a `std::tuple<..., std::tuple<..., Box*>>` slot is miscompiled. `return (1, (2, b))` under `-> tuple[int, tuple[int, Box]]` fails the C++ build (`tuple` ctor mismatch); `yield (i, (i, b))` under `Iterator[tuple[int, tuple[int, Box]]]` builds but produces garbage at runtime -- both even when the member `b` is a valid param/self-rooted borrow. A nested **value** tuple (`tuple[int, tuple[int, int]]`) and a nested **Own** member (`tuple[int, tuple[int, Own[Box]]]`) both work (value-stored / moved). **No longer user-reachable** -- rejected at sema (`tpyc/sema/compatibility.py::_nested_tuple_borrow_member`, reached from `check_dangling_reference`) with "nested borrow slot is miscompiled -- flatten the tuple or make the member Own[T]"; LOW priority because the workarounds are trivial and the shape is exotic, but the codegen size is medium. This entry tracks lifting the restriction. **Fix shape:** make the tuple borrow<->storage conversion helpers recurse into nested `TupleType` members (per-element, mirroring the existing flat per-element conversion). Once codegen handles it, drop the `_nested_tuple_borrow_member` sema rejection. Surfaced while fixing the bare-tuple fresh-yield dangle (risk #4).
- **[MED small, deferred: MIR] A `key=` lambda over a generic-element tuple fails the C++ build for reference-type keys (value-type keys work).** `def ranked[T](pairs: list[tuple[T, Int32]]): return sorted(pairs, key=lambda p: -p[1])` (and the `min(a, b, key=...)` / `max(...)` form) compiles and runs for `T = str`/`Int32` but fails the C++ build for `T = SomeRecord`. The key lambda's param form is reconstructed at its DEFINITION site (`_gen_lambda` `readonly_params` branch -> `_callable_param_cpp`, which renders the tuple element in BORROW form `std::tuple<val_or_ptr_t<T>, int32_t>`), but the form the consumer needs differs by builtin: `builtin_sorted_key` calls `key(items[i])` with the container's STORAGE-form element (`std::tuple<T, int32_t>`), so sorted needs a storage-form param; `min_key`/`max_key` are handed the BORROW-form function args, so the param there correctly stays borrow but the *return* is then borrow (`tuple<T*, int>`) where the declared `Own[tuple[T, Int32]]` return is storage (`tuple<T, int>`). So it is two distinct sub-bugs in one shape: (a) sorted's key-lambda param must be storage form, and (b) min/max needs a borrow->storage RETURN conversion on the result. Value-type keys compile because borrow and storage forms coincide. Toolchain-caught (C++ build error), NOT a silent miscompile; no TPy diagnostic. A single render rule for the `readonly_params` lambda cannot fix both consumers -- the param form is consumer-dictated, not knowable at the lambda definition site. This is the borrow/storage tuple-form duality (`docs/IR_DESIGN.md` Open Questions item 9, listed there as a sharp exhibit): the clean fix is form-as-an-explicit-IR-fact so the lambda param carries its consumer's form and the conversion is a visible node. Reproducer shape: `def ranked[T](pairs: list[tuple[T, Int32]]): return sorted(pairs, key=lambda p: -p[1])` with `T` a record (sorted); same over `min(a, b, key=lambda p: p[1])` (min/max). Surfaced by /tpy-fix-bug while extending the value-type key-lambda fix to reference-type elements. IR-gated; needs `/tpy-fix-bug` or `/tpy-add-feature` once item 9 lands.
- **[LOW small]** The fresh-member rejection for an `Optional`/`Union` tuple member suggests an escape hatch that does not compile. `yield (i, Box(i))` under `Iterator[tuple[int, Optional[Box]]]` is correctly rejected (the member would dangle) with "Use Own[Box | None] for this tuple element", but `Iterator[tuple[int, Own[Optional[Box]]]]` then miscompiles on consumption: the slot is `std::tuple<BigInt, std::optional<Box>>` and the consumer's unpack emits `Box* b = std::get<1>(__tup)` against a `std::optional<Box>` (C++ type error). So a *fresh* `Optional`/`Union` tuple member has no working form today. Two gaps: (a) the diagnostic points at a non-working fix (`_check_tuple_elem_dangle` in `tpyc/sema/compatibility.py` reuses the bare-member wording), and (b) codegen mis-extracts an `Own[Optional[T]]` / value-variant tuple member on tuple-unpack. Exotic shape; surfaced while settling whether Optional/Union tuple members were a dangle bypass (they are correctly rejected -- this is the follow-on fix-suggestion gap). Fix: either make the consumer extract the storage-form (`std::optional`/value-variant) member correctly, or give the diagnostic an Optional/Union-aware suggestion.
- **[LOW small]** A `readonly[tuple[...]]` cannot be unpacked in a `for a, b in ...` loop -- `for i, b in g()` where `g() -> Iterator[readonly[tuple[int, Box]]]` is rejected with "Cannot unpack non-tuple type readonly[tuple[int, Box]]". The tuple-unpack target resolution does not see through the `ReadonlyType` wrapper to the underlying `TupleType`. Index access (`for t in g(): t[1]`) works, so the yield/return of a `readonly` tuple is otherwise fine; only destructuring is blocked. Rejects valid code. Fix: unwrap `ReadonlyType` when resolving an unpack target's element types (the elements are then `readonly[...]`-projected). Surfaced while probing readonly-tuple fresh-yield rejection.
- **[LOW small]** Unverified borrow hole: moving a move-only local out (`take(Own[b])`) while a generator iterator still borrows it (`it = b.gen(); ...; take(b); for v in it: ...`) -- the iterator frame holds `&b` but the borrow isn't tracked against the move, so a post-move read would see moved-from data. Could not force a dangle in a probe (the consuming call didn't actually move in the shapes tried); same family as the mutation-during-lifetime hazards below. Revisit if a probe confirms it.
- **[LOW small]** H1 frame-field registration for a suspending `match` binds pattern names into `current_ns` per-arm with no merge (`tpyc/sema/match.py`): when two arms bind the SAME name to types with different value-ness (one value, one reference), `generator_locals` records the LAST arm's type, so the frame field's storage form (value / `frame_slot` / pointer) may be wrong for the other arm's binding. Untriggered today (no test binds one name across arms with divergent value-ness). Fix shape: use the existing post-match `arm_bindings` merge as the authority for the type registered into `current_ns`, rather than binding per-arm. Surfaced by /tpy-review (architecture-fit).
- **[MED small] Silent divergence: the simple-generator peephole runs the body prologue eagerly and post-yield statements one pull early.** A single-yield generator's lowering executes init/prologue statements at construction (CPython defers the whole body to the first `next()`) and emits post-yield statements before delivering the yielded value (`__val = expr; <post-yield>; return __val`), so a side effect after the yield runs one pull early. Reproducers: `def g(items): print(999); for x in items: yield x` -- `gen = g(...); print(0)` prints `999, 0` (CPython `0, 999`); `def g(items): for x in items: yield x; print(x+100)` over `[1,2]` prints `101, 1, 102, 2` (CPython `1, 101, 2, 102`). No diagnostic. Benign for pure-state post-yield mutation (`i += 1`) that nothing observes between yields -- which is why it stayed hidden -- but observably wrong for any side-effecting prologue or post-yield statement. The resumable path models this correctly (real suspension); the break/continue subset of this family is now routed there, but non-loop-control prologue/post-yield bodies still take the peephole. **Fix shape:** route single-yield generators with a body prologue or post-yield statements to the resumable lowering too (broader sibling of the break/continue eligibility guard) -- a behavior + snapshot change worth its own pass. Surfaced (and confirmed still live) during the simple-gen break/continue fix; documented in the 2026-06-10 compiler audit (B64). Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[MED small] A resumable generator METHOD that iterates a `self` field fails the C++ build.** `class S: data: list[Int32]; def vals(self) -> Iterator[Int32]: yield -1; for x in self.data: yield x` -- the readonly-inferred method takes a `const` receiver, so `(self.data).begin()` returns a `const_iterator`, but the resumable frame slot for the loop iterator is spelled as the mutable `std::vector<int>::iterator`, so the `__for_it.emplace((__self.data).begin())` fails (`no known conversion from const_iterator to iterator`). No TPy diagnostic; C++ build error only. Affects any resumable (multi-yield, or break/continue-bearing) generator method iterating one of its own reference-type fields. Pre-existing (reproduces on master with a plain 2-yield method, independent of break/continue); surfaced routing simple-gen break/continue methods to the resumable path. **Fix shape:** spell the frame iterator slot against the receiver's const-ness (`const_iterator` for a readonly/`const` self), or take a non-const self when the generator iterates a mutable field. Workaround: iterate a parameter or a local copy, not the field directly. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[MED small] An `async def` with a default parameter fails the C++ build when called via inline `await`.** `async def add(a: Int32, b: Int32 = 10) -> Int32: ...` then `await add(5)` emits `__sub.emplace(5)` -- the inline-await path constructs the coro struct's ctor directly (bypassing the factory function where the default lives), and the ctor has no default, so `emplace(int)` finds no matching 2-arg ctor. The factory / `create_task(add(5))` path works (defaults land on the factory forward decl, post the generator-default-args fix). No TPy diagnostic; C++ build error only. Pre-existing -- the inline-await emplace never applied defaults. **Fix shape:** apply the missing defaults at the inline-await emplace site, or route the inline await through the factory. Workaround: pass all args explicitly, or call via `create_task`. Surfaced filling default args on generator factories. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] Default arg values are not emitted on a *resumable* generator/coro METHOD factory.** The generator-default-args fix covers free functions (simple + resumable factories) and *simple* methods (the inline `_gen_params` path), but a resumable generator/coro METHOD with a defaulted param goes through `_emit_method_params_decl` (`gen_async.py`, 4 emit sites with their own in-struct-decl-vs-out-of-line-def split), which was deliberately left out -- so `obj.gen()` omitting a defaulted arg drops the default. No TPy diagnostic; C++ build error only. **Fix shape:** thread `func.defaults` through `_emit_method_params_decl` like `_emit_params_decl`, emitting on the canonical method-factory declaration only (respecting the decl/def placement across its 4 sites). Surfaced (scoped out) by the generator-default-args fix. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[MED small] A resumable generator METHOD receiving a reference-type parameter by rvalue (a literal / temporary) fails the C++ build.** `lim.first(items=[..literal..])` into `def first(self, items: list[Int32]) -> Iterator[Int32]: ...for x in items...` (with break/continue or 2+ yields -> resumable) emits a frame that captures `items` by reference, which cannot bind the rvalue list (`cannot bind non-const lvalue reference of type 'std::vector<int>&' to an rvalue`). The free-function resumable path handles an rvalue list param correctly (it owns/binds the temporary), so this is method-path-specific. No TPy diagnostic; C++ build error only. Pre-existing; surfaced routing simple-gen break/continue methods to resumable. Workaround: bind the argument to a local lvalue and pass that. **Fix shape:** mirror the free-function resumable param handling on the method path (own the temporary in the frame rather than borrowing it). Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small]** Watch-list (mostly unverified): wrapper-aware sites that decide "does this type carry a wrapper struct?" by gating on a concrete carrier class -- `isinstance(x, UnionType) and x.needs_wrapper()`, `isinstance(inner, AliasRef)`, or `.members` access -- instead of the polymorphic `needs_wrapper()` / `recursive_union_alternatives()`, so they *could* mishandle `RecursiveAliasInstanceType` (the carrier for generic recursive aliases). **Triaged by probe on `fix-recursive-alias-wrapper-gates` (2026-05-29): the genuinely reachable bug in this family was a *different* root cause** -- the generic-recursive finalize pass (`_finalize_generic_recursive_aliases`) skipped `RecordInfo.init_params`, so a ctor param typed as a generic recursive alias was checked against a stale `AliasRef` and valid `Summary(tree)` calls were rejected with `expected T | list[Tree[T]], got Tree[Int32]`. **Fixed** by finalizing `init_params` in that pass (regression: `tests/cases/union/generic_recursive_ctor_arg`). The concrete-class-gate sites below did NOT reproduce a bug in realistic probes; keep them as a watch-list, not asserted bugs. Proper unification is the `cpp_shape` redesign in `TODO.md`. A survey on this branch found ~12 same-shape sites (more than the originals below); none reproduced.
  - **Confirmed NOT a bug:** `tpyc/codegen_cpp/functions.py:373` / `:489` -- the `isinstance(own, UnionType) and own.needs_wrapper()` specialcase exists *because* `UnionType.to_cpp_param()` defaults to pointer-variant form; `RecursiveAliasInstanceType.to_cpp_param()` already defaults to the value-wrapper form, so `Own[Tree[T]]` params correctly emit `Tree<T>&&` via the default path (verified). The two carriers deliberately differ here -- routing through `needs_wrapper()` would be wrong.
  - **Not reproduced:** `variant_access.py:50` / `statements.py:3955` (`isinstance(peeled.inner, AliasRef)`) -- `Optional[Tree[T]]` narrow + value-use compiles and runs; no indirection/extraction bug observed.
  - **Latent, not separately probed:** `compatibility.py:1561` (`_check_union_member_type_args`, container-arg coercion), `expressions.py` `_gen_array_literal_with_target` / `_gen_union_arg`, `typesys.py:1640` make_ref skip-list, and the survey's additional `.members`-access sites (`calls.py:~3935/4669`, `statements.py:4119`, `expressions.py:2476`). Reachable only via shapes the generic recursive alias rejects or doesn't construct today; revisit if a probe finds one or `cpp_shape` lands first.
  - `tpyc/sema/compatibility.py:1960` `is_dangling_return` TpyMethodCall branch -- **FIXED** on `fix-recursive-alias-wrapper-gates`. Once the emit-ordering + method-`FunctionInfo`-finalize fixes made `def m() -> Tree[Int32]` compile, this became reachable (`def g() -> Optional[Tree]: return obj.m()` emitted `&(obj.m())` -- address of an rvalue); the branch now mirrors the free-function wrapper-shape check. Regression: `tests/cases/union/error_generic_recursive_method_optional_return_dangle`.
- **[MED small]** A record method (or field) that returns/embeds a **non-generic** recursive union (`type Expr = Lit | Node`, `type Expr = Int32 | list[Expr]`) **by value** fails C++ codegen with `return type 'Expr' is incomplete` -- the wrapper struct's full definition is emitted after the inline method body that returns it by value. (Secondary symptom: a list-literal return like `[v, [v]]` then mis-emits as `std::vector<Expr>{v, ...}` because codegen can't resolve the incomplete type's variant alternatives.) This is the non-generic analogue of the (fixed) generic method-return ordering bug, but **harder**: the generic wrapper is a template whose members need not be complete at definition, so it could move before all records; a non-generic wrapper's `std::variant<...>` embeds its members by value, so when a member is a user record (`Lit`/`Node`) the wrapper must come *after* those records yet *before* any record that returns/embeds it -- a dependency-aware topological ordering, not a flat "before records" move. (For a primitive-only union like `Int32 | list[Expr]` the wrapper could move early, but the general case needs the topo sort.) Free-function returns work (emitted after all type defs); only record-method-return / field-embedding by value breaks. Reproducer: a `Calc` with `def wrap(self, v: Int32) -> Expr: return [v, [v]]`. Surfaced while writing the F2 protocol-param regression test (`union_recursive_protocol_method`).
- **[LOW small]** `_gen_array_literal` (tpyc/codegen_cpp/expressions.py:3911) unconditionally strips `ReadonlyType` from the target before the wrapper-alternative match. For non-wrapper `readonly[Array[T,N]] / readonly[Span[T]] / readonly[list[T]]` targets, the unwrap exposes a different result for `needs_explicit_element_target()` (False on ReadonlyType, True on Array/Span) -- elements then flow through `_wrap_for_owned_slot` with an `elem_target` they did not have before. PLAUSIBLE: no current test surface reaches `_gen_array_literal` with a bare `ReadonlyType` target (call-site machinery and sema strip readonly upstream), so the divergence is latent. Right shape: scope the unwrap to the wrapper-detection branches (UnionType / RecursiveAliasInstanceType), not at the top of the function. Surfaced by /code-review medium of `fix-recursive-alias-consumers`.
- **[MED small]** A `tuple[readonly[T], ...]` return whose element borrows an lvalue (param / field) and is then unpacked at the call site emits ill-formed C++: the unpack declares a non-const `T&` bound to the `const T` tuple element (`error: binding reference of type 'T&' to 'const T' discards qualifiers`). General to all reference types -- records and recursive-union wrappers reproduce identically. The borrow-form tuple element renders `const T&` (correct), but the unpack local (`Tn& x = ::tpy::unwrap_ref(std::get<i>(...))`) ignores the element's const-ness. Reproducer: `def f(a: Foo) -> tuple[readonly[Foo], Int32]: return (a, 0)` then `r, n = f(g)`. Pre-existing; surfaced by /tpy-review of the recursive-union reference-return (Family C) migration. Fix shape: the tuple-unpack local for a `readonly[...]` (const) borrow element must be declared `const Tn&`.
- **[MED small]** Inside a generic function body, a member-template call on a type-dependent expression omits the required `template` disambiguator, producing ill-formed C++. A widening/narrowing conversion on the result of a generic call -- e.g. `total += count(x)` where `count[T](...) -> int` (BigInt) and `total` is `Int32` -- lowers to `count<T>(x).to_fixed_check<int32_t>()`; because `count<T>(x)` is type-dependent, C++ requires `count<T>(x).template to_fixed_check<int32_t>()` (g++ reports `-Wmissing-template-keyword` then `expected primary-expression before '>'`). Affects any generic function that calls `.to_fixed_check<...>()` / `.to_fixed<...>()` (or any member template) on a dependent receiver. Pre-existing on master (independent of generic recursive aliases -- a plain `def total_count[T](xs: list[T]) -> Int32: total = 0; for x in xs: total += count(x)` reproduces it). Workaround: keep the accumulator and the generic call's return type the same width (e.g. both `Int32`, or both `int`) so no member-template conversion is emitted. **Fix shape:** at member-template-call codegen, emit the `template` keyword when the receiver expression's type is dependent (contains a `TypeParamRef`) inside a template context. Surfaced while writing the generic-recursive-alias match test (`leaf_count[T]` accumulating an `int`-returning recursive call into an `Int32`).
- **[LOW small]** `_check_union_member_type_args` (`tpyc/sema/compatibility.py:~1559`) resolves only `(NominalType, AliasRef)` before its `UnionType.needs_wrapper()` branch, so a `RecursiveAliasInstanceType` container-element type-arg (e.g. a `list[Tree[int]]` slot whose element is the generic recursive alias) is not recognized as a wrapper and falls through to the `return False` rejection. **Likely unreachable today** -- the generic-recursive happy-path tests that coerce `list[Tree[int]]` into the wrapper pass (the path is reached via the equality short-circuit or a `UnionType` arg, not an instance arg), so this is a latent gap, not an observed failure. Same root cause as the other "instance unrecognized at wrapper-gated sites" entries: the new `RecursiveAliasInstanceType` was not threaded through every `needs_wrapper()`/`UnionType` consumer. Fix shape: recognize the instance (via `is_recursive_union_like`) alongside the `(NominalType, AliasRef)` resolution there. Surfaced by /tpy-review of the generic-recursive-alias branch.
- **[LOW small]** Returning a freshly-constructed exception as `Own[<Exception>]` emits a confusing same-type diagnostic. `def make_err(...) -> Own[ValueError]: return ValueError("boom")` is rejected with `Type mismatch in return value: expected ValueError, got ValueError`. The `Own[...]` wrapper is mishandled in the return-value type compatibility check for native exception types (same family as the Own-wrapper-stripping return bugs already tracked below). Workaround: return the exception by a different path or raise it directly. Surfaced while authoring the `error_raise_read_after_consume` regression test (switched the test to `raise ValueError(f"... {h.fd}")` to sidestep it).
- **[LOW small]** `_check_polymorphic_slicing` false-positive on `Box(exc)` where `exc: Own[BaseException]` (an Own-typed param of the enclosing function). `def store(exc: Own[BaseException]) -> Box[BaseException]: return Box(exc)` is rejected with the slicing diagnostic even though it's a legitimate ownership transfer -- moving an owned value into a Box preserves whatever the value holds, no slicing. The check at `tpyc/sema/compatibility.py:_check_polymorphic_slicing` has an `if isinstance(actual, OwnType): return` escape at the top, but it doesn't fire here because sema strips the Own when typing the bare name `exc` at the Box-ctor argument site, so the check sees a borrow-shaped `BaseException` instead of `Own[BaseException]`. Rejects valid code. Fix: consult the name's *declared* type (not the use-context type) when applying the Own-skip rule. Workaround: use `Box[Throwable]` (the recommended polymorphic-storage convention) or `tpy.copy(exc)`. Surfaced during the (dropped) covariant-return exploration while attempting to narrow `asyncio.Future.set_exception`.
- **[LOW small]** `_check_polymorphic_slicing` false-positive on taking a pointer to a polymorphic lvalue: `take_ptr(d)` (or the implicit `d -> Ptr[Dog]` address-of coercion) where `d` is a value of a polymorphic class (one that transitively implements a `@dynamic` protocol) is rejected with "cannot store 'Dog' borrow as owned 'Dog'". Taking an address produces a `Dog*` to the exact object -- no copy, no slicing -- so the rejection is spurious and the suggested `Box(...)` remediation is wrong for the pointer case. Reproducer: `@dynamic class Pet(Protocol): ...; class Parrot(Pet): ...; bird = Parrot("x"); pp: Ptr[Parrot] = take_ptr(bird)`. The guard's `target_inner not NominalType -> return` early-out doesn't fire because the address-of coercion is checked pointee-against-value (`Parrot -> Parrot`) with a NominalType expected, so the polymorphic-source branch trips. Fix: skip the slicing check when the destination is a `Ptr[...]` (pointer borrow, never an owned value slot). Pre-existing (reproduces with no involvement from bound-coercion work); blocks a `@dynamic`-conformer `Ptr` round-trip, which is why `tests/cases/generics/bound_coercion_*` exercise class bounds for the exec path. Surfaced while writing tests for `feat-bound-subtype-coercion`.
- **[LOW small]** Sema/codegen asymmetry on Own-peeling for polymorphic-subclass narrowing facts. Sema's `_polymorphic_subclass_fact` in `tpyc/sema/statements.py:1384` applies a local `_peel` that strips `OwnType` and `ReadonlyType` before calling `is_polymorphic_subclass_fact` -- so an `Own[Pet]` parameter narrowed via `isinstance(p, Dog)` produces a fact. The three codegen call sites in `tpyc/codegen_cpp/statements.py` (`_build_isinstance_init_clause`, `_emit_isinstance_extractions`, the `_gen_if` post-guard filter) pass `lookup_var_type(...)` directly, and `polymorphic_source_inner` (`tpyc/typesys.py:3389`) only strips `Readonly` -- so codegen sees the OwnType and the predicate returns False. Result: sema thinks the fact is alive, codegen drops it; the std::get/std::any branches don't match either and the emit falls through to the variant-extraction path or produces a C++ compile error. **Fix shape:** bake Own-stripping into `polymorphic_source_inner` itself so it benefits all 5+ call sites uniformly (sema fact filter, codegen if-init, codegen extraction, codegen post-guard filter, expressions.py inline isinstance). Pre-existing on master; widened to a 3rd codegen call site by `fix-poly-early-return-narrowing`. Filed during /tpy-review of `fix-poly-early-return-narrowing`.
- **[LOW medium]** `isinstance(x, C)` on a generic type-parameter subject is currently *rejected* for any subject the compile-time `tpy::isinstance_static` trait cannot resolve soundly: an **unbounded** `T` (`def f[T](x: T)`), a `T` bounded by a **@dynamic protocol / polymorphic class**, and a `T` bounded by a **structural protocol** all raise a clean sema error (`tpyc/sema/calls.py::_analyze_isinstance_type_param`). The non-polymorphic class-bounded case works per-instantiation (see `tests/cases/generics/isinstance_typeparam_bound`). Lifting the rejections needs a **per-instantiation runtime dispatcher** -- a C++ helper that `if constexpr`-dispatches on the instantiated `decltype(x)` shape: union -> `holds_alternative` (value-variant *and* pointer-variant reps), `Any` -> `typeid`, polymorphic/@dynamic -> `dynamic_cast` / `dyn_adapter_cast`, else -> the current same-or-derived trait. This is the only sound way to support those instantiations, because a generic body is emitted as one C++ template (sema can't pick a per-instantiation lowering). The polymorphic-bounded sub-case alone could instead be lifted more cheaply by extending `polymorphic_source_inner` to consult a `TypeParamRef`'s bound and threading the type-param case through the existing `dynamic_cast` codegen path. Deferred as the documented follow-up to the bounded-`T` fix; demand is currently hypothetical (no test exercises generic-subject isinstance beyond the supported case).
- **[LOW small]** Soundness-gate hole in the trait lowering above, now **loud-guarded** (was silent): `_analyze_isinstance_type_param` (`tpyc/sema/calls.py`) rejects a *bound* that is polymorphic (`is_dynamic_dispatch_inner`), but does not stop a caller from instantiating `T` with a **polymorphic subclass of a non-polymorphic bound**. `validate_record_inheritance` (`tpyc/sema/registration.py`) imposes no rule that a polymorphic class's @dynamic origin must come from its parents, so `class D(B, SomeDynProto)` is legal with `B` a plain (non-poly) class; `def f[T: B](x: T)` then accepts `T=D`, and the body's `isinstance(x, E)` lowers to `isinstance_static<E, D>()` -- a compile-time fold that cannot see `D`'s runtime dynamic type, so it would return False where `dynamic_cast` / CPython return True for an `E` instance behind a `D&`. **Stopgap shipped:** `tpy::isinstance_static` (`runtime/cpp/include/tpy/dynamic.hpp`) carries a `static_assert(!std::is_polymorphic_v<XT>)` that turns this into a (cryptic, C++-level) loud compile error at the offending instantiation instead of a silent wrong answer. Not a regression (this instantiation folded silently-False before the trait existed too); the hole is in the "non-poly bound => static type is dynamic type" invariant, which holds for the bound but not for its polymorphic subclasses. **Proper fix (removes the static_assert):** re-validate substituted type args at the generic call site (reject with a clean tpyc-level diagnostic, or route to `dynamic_cast`, when the concrete `T` is polymorphic) -- the same "re-validate substituted types at call sites" pass other entries need; per-module Phase-1 visibility means a cross-module caller's instantiation isn't seen during the function's own analysis, so the call-site pass is the principled home. No harness test today (the guard fires as a C++ static_assert, not a tpyc diagnostic); add one with the clean diagnostic. Use `/tpy-fix-bug`. Surfaced by `/tpy-review` (safety-model + cpython-parity) of the bounded-`T` trait fix.
- **[LOW small]** Mutual hashed-container-element cycle between two records (`class A: s: Own[set["B"]]; class B: s: Own[set["A"]]`) silently emits ill-formed C++. The Kahn-algorithm cycle-fallback in `tpyc/codegen_cpp/records.py::sort_records_by_inheritance` appends both records in source order; whichever emits second sees a complete `std::hash<>` for its element, but whichever emits first does not -- the user sees a wall of C++ template errors instead of a TPy-level diagnostic. The new hash-element edges introduced on `validate-tuple-own-opt-poly` make this failure mode newly reachable (inheritance-only edges could not produce element-side cycles). **Fix shape:** detect cycles where the dependency type is `hash-element` (as distinct from `inheritance`) and emit a TPy diagnostic ("`set`-of / `dict`-keyed cycle between records X and Y") with guidance to break the cycle via an indirection (`Own[set[Box[Y]]]` etc.). Filed during /tpy-review of `validate-tuple-own-opt-poly`.
- **[LOW small]** Property setter for an `Optional[Polymorphic]` field emits a confusing diagnostic. The user writes `@pet.setter def pet(self, value: Optional[Pet]) -> None: ...` (where `Pet` inherits a `@dynamic` protocol). Sema's `_apply_property_setter_wrapping` (`tpyc/sema/method_expansion.py:270-276`) auto-wraps the first setter param in `Own[]`, producing `Own[Optional[Pet]]`. The centralized `validate_type` rejection then fires with "Use `Optional[Pet]` or `Box[Pet]`" -- but the user already wrote `Optional[Pet]`; the auto-wrap is invisible. **Fix shape:** at setter wrap time, skip the Own wrap for `Optional[<polymorphic class>]` params (the unwrapped Optional[Pet] shape is the right setter surface for polymorphic targets). Requires threading the type registry into `_apply_property_setter_wrapping` via `expand_methods_for_record`. Alternative: emit a setter-context-aware diagnostic from validate_type's OwnType branch when the call-site indicates an auto-wrap, though that requires passing context through validate_type. Filed during /code-review of the `own-opt-poly-reject` branch.
- **[LOW small]** Free-function and free-method signatures containing `Own[Optional[Polymorphic]]` skip the centralized sema rejection when the polymorphic target class is defined LATER in the same module. `register_function` (`tpyc/sema/registration.py:2619`) calls `validate_type` once at registration time, and `is_polymorphic_class_type` returns False for unregistered records (the "forward-ref defer" path documented in the OwnType rejection). The deferral works for record fields/methods because `validate_record_field_protocols` re-runs validate_type after inheritance is finalized; no analogous second pass exists for free functions. **Fix shape:** add a per-module post-Phase-1 pass that re-validates free-function param + return types after all records' inheritance is processed (analogous to `validate_record_field_protocols`). Filed during /code-review of the `own-opt-poly-reject` branch.
- **[LOW small]** `await coro` where `coro` is a local typed as a `@dynamic` protocol (e.g. `coro: Own[Cancellable[T]]`) is rejected at sema with "await operand must be a direct call to an async def, a Task[T] / Future[T], or a value of a type with a __poll__ ... method", and a sema-side workaround that recognizes the protocol shape exposes a downstream codegen bug: the borrowed-await path emits `__sub_0 = &(coro)` (`unique_ptr<P>*`) where it should emit `__sub_0 = coro.get()` (`P*`). Both halves need fixing together: extend the await operand check in `tpyc/sema/expressions.py` (around the `_extract_awaitable_inner` fallback) to accept `Cancellable[T]` / `Awaitable[T]` protocol-typed operands by unwrapping their type-arg slot, and teach the borrowed-await codegen in `tpyc/codegen_cpp/gen_async.py` to call `.get()` on `unique_ptr<P>` sources instead of taking address-of. New code path opened by the `feat-cancellable-async-typing` work (Cancellable is now an implicit-stdlib protocol so users can write `Own[Cancellable[T]]` directly); no test exercises the shape today because the existing async tests all await fresh call expressions, not protocol-typed locals.
- **[LOW small]** A user-defined top-level function whose name collides with an imported stdlib function (e.g. `def run(...)` alongside `import asyncio` + `asyncio.run(...)`) makes codegen emit the wrong namespace qualifier for the qualified call: `asyncio.run(caller())` generates `::tpyapp::__main__::run<...>(...)` (the user module's namespace) instead of the asyncio namespace, so the call references a non-existent symbol and C++ fails (`'tpyapp::__main__' has not been declared`). Sema picks the right FunctionInfo (the generated signature is asyncio.run's `run<T>(Adapter...)`), so this is a codegen namespace-qualification bug -- the qualified-call emit keys the namespace off the bare name and a same-named local function shadows the import. Workaround: don't name a local function the same as an imported stdlib function you also call qualified. Surfaced while authoring the Phase-D3 `await_in_for_else` test (a local `async def run` shadowed `asyncio.run`).
- **[LOW small]** Asymmetric variable-lookup between the two arms of the post-guard fact filter in `_gen_if` (`tpyc/codegen_cpp/statements.py` around line 4032). The `is_recursive_union` arm uses `self.ctx.var_types.get(k)` (locals + params only) while the new polymorphic arm uses `self.ctx.lookup_var_type(k)` (locals + params + globals + self). A recursive-union `self.field` or module-global narrowed via early-return is silently excluded from `post_facts` while a polymorphic one in the same shape is included.
