Fix paths: (a) treat TupleType slots as non-value-like in `_tuple_literal_slot_info` so the rvalue+REF fallback fires, then emit the inner cpp_part as `et.to_cpp_return()` (borrow form) -- changes outer's slot to `std::tuple<T&, T&>` matching the param; (b) extend `tuple_value_to_borrow`'s `borrow_value_elem` to recurse on TupleType slots, converting value-form sub-tuples to borrow-form on the fly. (a) is smaller; (b) is more robust if mixed source slot shapes show up. **Optional-inner variant (same root cause):** `tuple[tuple[T | None, T | None], T | None]` as a call arg or field type breaks the same way -- `tuple_to_storage` / `tuple_to_pointer` don't recurse into nested-tuple slots, and a borrow-form-outer param paired with a destructure of the inner local picks storage form for the local even though the source is pointer form (`std::tuple<std::optional<P>, std::optional<P>> inner = std::get<0>(__tup_1)` on a `std::tuple<std::tuple<P*, P*>, P*>` source). Fix path is the same recursive form propagation. Reproducer: `/tmp/agents/tuple_fuzz/f1a_nested_minimal.py` (during the rvalue-tuple-borrow fuzz pass). **IR note:** part of the borrow/storage-form bug class -- see `docs/IR_DESIGN.md` Open Questions item 9 (tuple form as a first-class type fact).
- **[LOW small]** Rvalue init/rebind of a pointer-form generator/coro frame slot (e.g. `h: P | None = P(...)` or `h = P(...)` with `h` a frame field) routes through `_gen_pointer_local_rebind`'s rvalue-slot path (`statements.py:1234-1262`), which declares a function-scope `T __slot_N = rvalue();` and stores `frame_var = &__slot_N;`. `__slot_N` is local to `__next__()` / `poll()` and dies at the next yield/await; `frame_var` (a `T*` field) then dangles. Currently masked because no test rebinds a pointer-form frame slot from a fresh rvalue (the new fixture goes through `maybe_p()` which returns `T*`). Fix path: in the rvalue-slot branch, when the target is a generator pointer-form frame slot, hoist the storage slot itself to a frame field (or refuse the rvalue source). Same shape as BUGS.md:237's async-future borrow gap, but for direct value rvalues instead of borrowed awaitables. **Second route as of the generator->resumable-frame migration (Phase C):** an eligible generator (free, non-generic, no-param, linear body, non-tuple yield) emits its frame via the resumable emitter (`gen_async.py`), so a pointer-form `P | None` local rebound from an rvalue inside such a generator now reaches this same dangling slot through the resumable path rather than `gen_generators.py`. Still unexercised; the fix (hoist the storage slot to a frame field, or refuse the rvalue source) is shape-neutral and covers both emitters.
- **[LOW small]** A generator that yields a reference-type brace literal (`yield [1, 2]`, dict/set literal) fails to compile: the resumable yield-return emit (`gen_async.py::_emit_generator_yield`) emits `return {yield_expr};`, and a bare brace-init doesn't bind to the `__next__` return type `std::expected<std::vector<T>, ::tpy::StopIteration>` (g++: "could not convert '{1, 2}' from '<brace-enclosed initializer list>'"). Also reachable on the simple-generator peephole (shared `gen_yield_value`). **Fix:** wrap the yielded value in its element type at the yield-return site (mirror `gen_generators.py::_wrap_brace_init_for_emplace`). Lvalue/typed-expression yields are unaffected; only bare brace literals hit this.
- **[LOW small]** On the exception path, `return`-in-helper-finally skips outer `with.__exit__` in resumable generators.
  When a generator has `with CM: try: yield X; finally: return` and an exception is thrown INTO the generator at the yield site (via a future `gen.throw()` API -- not yet exposed by TPy), the inner catch calls `__finally_0()` (sets `__finally_stop = true`) and the stop-check in `_emit_try_region_catches` returns `StopIteration` via C++ `return`. A C++ `return` from inside a `catch(...)` block does NOT trigger the enclosing `try`'s outer catch handlers, so the outer `with.__exit__` is never called. Python semantics require `return` in `finally` to suppress the exception AND still call context manager `__exit__`. The normal iteration path is correct (tested in `gen_resumable_with_return_in_finally`). **Currently untriggerable:** TPy does not expose `gen.throw()`. Fix path: when the stop-check fires inside a catch block, the outer `with.__exit__` must be called before returning -- either by hoisting the check outside the C++ try/catch structure, or by restructuring the catch-all to emit `__exit__` inline before the stop-check.
- **[MED small]** Cross-module generator delegation inside a resumable frame is rejected (clean diagnostic, was a silent infinite loop). `for x in walk():` with a yield/await in the body, where `walk` is a generator imported from another module, raises "only supported for a direct call to a generator defined in the same module" (`gen_generators.py::_temp_iterator_field_cpp`). Root cause: the `__for_src` frame field needs the callee's struct type, and a cross-module *simple* generator has no named struct (its factory returns an `inline auto` lambda wrapper) -- simplicity is not visible across the module boundary, so all cross-module callees are rejected. Fix shape: spell the field as `std::decay_t<decltype(::ns::walk(<declval per callee param>))>` -- the callee's definition (simple) or concrete declaration (non-simple) is in the included header, so the decltype resolves for both; needs per-param declval rendering (Own[T] -> rvalue, non-value -> `T&`) and a carve-out for protocol params (concepts can't declval). Same-module callees, incl. forced-off-peephole simple ones, work as of the for-src materialization fix. Pinned by `tests/cases/iterators/error_gen_delegate_cross_module`. **Related rejection, same by-value-frame root cause:** *bounded* recursive delegation -- a tree walk `for x in walk(child): yield x` inside `walk`, valid and common in CPython -- is rejected by the same cycle check (the embedded frame type would be infinite-size regardless of runtime depth); the `list(...)` workaround materializes each subtree. Lifting it needs a heap-indirected recursive sub-frame (e.g. a boxed `__for_src`), a feature on its own.
- **[LOW small]** A mutual delegation cycle between two *simple* generators (`def a(): for x in b(): yield x` + `def b(): for x in a(): yield x`) recurses on the first pull (each pull lazily emplaces and pulls the other side's `__src`) -> stack overflow at the same point where CPython raises a catchable RecursionError. Self-delegation (1-cycle) and any cycle involving a non-simple generator are caught at compile time ("recursive generator delegation" / forced promotion in `generator.py::_prescan_for_src_embedding`); only the pure simple-simple mutual cycle escapes, because simple consumers never enter the forcing worklist. Pathological shape (the cycle yields nothing under CPython either). Fix shape: extend the pre-scan worklist to walk simple generators' delegation targets and force any cycle participant.
- **[MED small]** Silent divergence: a non-value loop var over a universal-strategy (`iter_next`) source in a resumable frame binds as a `frame_slot<T>` *copy*, so element mutations through the loop var are lost. `for n in bag: n.val += 10` (with `bag: Bag` a user class whose `__iter__` is a generator method, inside a multi-yield generator) leaves `bag`'s elements unchanged where CPython mutates them (verified 2026-06-04; the producer's `val_or_ref` slot preserves the reference, but the bind copies). `begin_end` sources alias via `pointer_form_loop_var` (`T*`), and the `next` strategy (typing.Iterator sources) now mirrors that; only `iter_next` still value-copies. Likely also reachable through `Iterable[T]` protocol params (same bind path) -- verify when fixing. Fix shape: set `pointer_form_loop_var` for non-value, non-readonly elements in `_analyze_for_strategy`'s universal branch (the advance at `gen_async.py::_for_advance_parts` already handles pointer-form for `next`/`iter_next`); audit the begin_end parity edge cases (readonly carve-out, tuple-unpack targets) when widening.
- **[LOW small]** Over-broad ephemeral-borrow rejection: `yield n.val` (a *value-type field read* of a loop var borrowed from a generator/iterator source) is rejected with "Cannot yield 'n': it borrows an element of a generator / iterator" even though the field read copies and nothing borrowed escapes. The consumer-escape check keys on the root name rather than the expression shape. Reproducer: `def vals(it: Iterator[Node]) -> Iterator[Int32]: yield 0; for n in it: yield n.val` (verified 2026-06-04). Workaround: accumulate into a local and yield that. Fix shape: allow yields whose expression is a value-type projection of the borrowed root (field access / method call returning a value type).
- **[LOW small]** A local bound to a generator call cannot be iterated across a suspension: `it = src()` then `for x in it: ... yield ...` in a multi-yield generator is rejected with "local 'it' of protocol type aliasing a protocol-typed parameter is not yet supported across a suspension" (`gen_async.py` hoisted-local emit; the local's TPy type is the `typing.Iterator` protocol, which has no concrete C++ frame rendering). Clean rejection of valid Python; the direct form `for x in src():` works as of the for-src materialization fix, so the workaround is to inline the call into the for statement. Fix shape: when the local's initializer is a direct call to a same-module generator, type the frame field via the same callee-struct spelling `__for_src` now uses (`gen_generators.py::_temp_iterator_field_cpp`).
- **[LOW small]** A *named* `Iterator[T]` source with a non-value `T` in a resumable frame spells the result slot as `std::expected<T&, StopIteration>` -- ill-formed C++ (references are not allowed in `std::expected`), toolchain-caught. `_analyze_for_strategy`'s `typing.Iterator` branch keeps the elem-type formula for named sources; temporary sources now spell `decltype(std::declval<__gen_X&>().__next__())` from the stored struct instead. Mostly shadowed by the over-broad ephemeral-borrow rejection above (a yield touching the loop var is rejected at sema first), so the reachable window is a body that mutates but never yields anything derived from the loop var. Fix shape: for named sources, derive the slot from the source's deduced struct/template type (`decltype(std::declval<T_<pname>&>().__next__())` for params) instead of the element type.
- **[LOW small]** A for-loop over a non-value *readonly* element (begin_end strategy) fails to compile with a const-correctness error. `_analyze_for_strategy` sets `pointer_form_loop_var` for non-value, non-readonly elements (binding the loop var as `T*` via `&(*it)`); the readonly carve-out is `not isinstance(unwrap_ref_type(native_elem), ReadonlyType)`, but `unwrap_ref_type` strips the `readonly[...]` wrapper, so the check never fires and a readonly element still takes the pointer-form path -- emitting `&(*it)` of type `const T*` assigned to a `T*` frame field. Reproducer: `def g(items: Span[readonly[Node]]) -> Iterator[Int32]: for it in items: yield it.v; yield it.v + 1` -> g++ `invalid conversion from 'const Node*' to 'Node*'`. Shared `_analyze_for_strategy`, so pre-existing on legacy too; rare shape. Fix: check readonly-ness *before* `unwrap_ref_type` (or emit a `const T*` slot for readonly elements). Surfaced by /code-review of the Phase D branch.
- **[MED small]** Generator yielding `Optional[T]` directly (not in a tuple) breaks consumer-side narrowing -- the for-loop iter var is rendered as storage-form `std::optional<T>` but sema treats it as pointer-repr Optional, so narrowing emits `p.has_value()` (works) plus `p.x` (fails: storage form has no `.x`).
  Reproducer: `def gen() -> Iterator[P | None]: yield P(1)` consumed via `for p in gen(): if p is not None: print(p.x)`. The iterator's `__next__()` returns `std::expected<std::optional<P>, ...>` -- inner storage form, presumably for value-ownership across frame resume (a `P*` would dangle if the source goes out of scope). The for-loop iter binding becomes `const auto& p = unwrap_ref(*__r)` deduced as `optional<P>`. Sema sees `p` as pointer-repr Optional and applies the pointer-form narrowing (`p != nullptr`-style with `.x` direct access) -- but `p` is storage form, so `.has_value()` happens to compile while `p.x` does not. Symptom: cryptic `'class std::optional<P>' has no member named 'x'`. Two fix paths: (a) make the generator iter return type pointer-form (`P*`) for non-value Optional yields, with a borrow-checker rule that the consumer can't outlive the generator frame; (b) wrap the for-loop iter binding to convert storage to pointer at the boundary, e.g. `const auto* p = ::tpy::optional_to_ptr(unwrap_ref(*__r));`, then narrowing's pointer-form emit just works. (b) is more localized and matches the existing `tuple_to_pointer` boundary pattern. Surfaced while probing the generator-promoted-Optional-local fix; orthogonal to that work.
- **[LOW small]** `is_storage_form_source(TpySubscript)` returns `True` unconditionally regardless of whether the subscripted container actually stores in storage form.
  `tpyc/codegen_cpp/context.py:1219` returns `True` for any `TpySubscript` expression. This is a useful approximation for the common case (list/dict/array elements are stored in storage form) but misses nested-tuple-of-tuple-of-Optional shapes where the outer subscript yields pointer-form data: e.g. `t[0]` on a `tuple[tuple[P|None, P|None], Int32]` param yields `std::tuple<P*, P*>` (pointer form), but the predicate returns `True` and downstream callers may emit a `tuple_to_pointer` lift on already-pointer-form input. The runtime helper's same-pointer-shape branch (`runtime/cpp/include/tpy/format.hpp` `to_pointer_form`) absorbs the no-op for the simple case (P* -> P*), but a nested tuple-of-tuple-of-Optional that needs to mix const-qualified inner pointers across a subscript would surface as a C++ template error. No current reproducer in the test corpus. Fix: track per-expression form (pointer vs storage) at sema rather than approximating from expression shape.
- **[LOW small]** Consumer-side `storage_form_tuple_locals.add(loop_var)` flag is semantically stale for generator-sourced `tuple[T | None, ...]` iter elements -- the var is now pointer form, but the flag still claims storage.
  `tpyc/codegen_cpp/statements.py` (`_gen_for_each` / `_gen_generator_loop_body`) adds the loop var to `storage_form_tuple_locals` whenever the iter element is a `TupleType` with `has_pointer_repr_optional_element()`. This was correct pre-`f414b3ebb` (when generators yielded storage form for these tuples) but is stale now that generator `__next__()` returns pointer form. Downstream uses of `is_storage_form_source(loop_var)` fire and emit redundant `tuple_to_pointer` wraps that the runtime helper's same-pointer-shape branch makes no-ops. Functionally safe (each call is a compile-time identity), semantically wrong. Fix: distinguish iter-from-NativeIterable (storage-form var) from iter-from-generator (pointer-form var) at the consumer site, or query the iterable's resolved iterator slot type. Discovered during the `f414b3ebb` reviewer pass.
- **[MED small]** for-loop rebind residuals. Scalar value-type rebind of an existing local/param now follows CPython (the var holds the last element after the loop). Remaining gaps: (a) REFERENCE-type rebind is rejected ("not yet supported") -- the hoisted assignment cannot express CPython's per-iteration aliasing, and writing through a reference param would mutate the caller's object; the real fix is a borrow-tracked pointer binding (loop-var alias loans, see the auto-move-gate residuals). (b) TUPLE-UNPACK targets (`for k, v in ...` over existing locals) still silently shadow (`not stmt.is_tuple_unpack` exclusion in _check_loop_var_rebind). (c) A CONDITIONALLY-assigned local (assigned only inside an earlier branch/loop) is not definitely_assigned, so it still shadows; prescan's flow-insensitive reassigned marking also allocates an unused slot scaffold for that shape (visible in pointers/escape_local_safe foreach_shadow_safe). Surfaced by the parser-fidelity cluster fix (audit #8).
- **[LOW small]** Tuple unpack cannot be the first assignment of a bare-annotated local; diagnostic mirrors use-before-init wording.
  `a: Int32; b: Int32; a, b = get_pair()` errors with `variable 'a' may not be assigned at this point` -- wording mirrors the definite-assignment use-before-init message, but the user is writing, not reading. Regular assignment after a bare annotation works (`a: Int32; a = Int32(5)`); only tuple unpack is refused as the first assignment. Reproducer: `/tmp/agents/fuzz-tuple-unpacking-20260511-114019/edges-negatives/probe_09.py`. CPython accepts the pattern. Workarounds: initialize first (`a: Int32 = Int32(0); b: Int32 = Int32(0); a, b = get_pair()`, wasteful) or omit the bare annotations and let unpack declare the locals from inference. Two fix paths: (a) treat tuple-unpack targets as a valid first-write for bare-annotated locals (consistent with regular assignment); (b) keep the rejection but rename the diagnostic to name the rule. (a) is the better fix.
- **[LOW small]** Codegen patterns that GCC silently accepts but Apple clang flags under `-Werror` -- currently suppressed via `_CLANG_ONLY_WARN_FLAGS` in `tpyc/compiler.py`. Each is a separate codegen quality item:
  - `-Wpessimizing-move`: codegen wraps construction-site values in `std::move` even when the value is a temporary (e.g. `unsafe_init(p, Point(10, 20))` -> `::new(p) Point(std::move(Point(10, 20)))`). Defeats copy elision. Fix lives in the placement-new / unsafe-init codegen path.
  - `-Wshorten-64-to-32`: implicit `std::size_t -> int32_t` narrowing at varargs / comprehension / array-comp boundaries. Fix: emit explicit `static_cast<int32_t>` (mirrors how the bounds-checked subscript path already casts).
  - `-Wfloat-conversion`: implicit `double -> bool` in `if x` for float locals, in `logical_and_or_value` codegen. Should route through `tpy::to_bool(x)`.
  - `-Wparentheses-equality`: `if ((x == y))` -- comparison codegen wraps in parens and the if-statement also wraps, producing redundant double-parens. Strip outer parens at the if-emission site (or at the comparison codegen site for the if/while/for-cond context).
  - `-Wtautological-overlap-compare`, `-Wunused-lambda-capture`, `-Wdangling-gsl`, `-Wdefaulted-function-deleted`, `-Wunused-value`: smaller occurrences each, surfaced in `tuple/tuple_hash`, `iterators/gen_for_range`, `iterators/dict_iterable`, `str/fstr_decompose`, `native/extern_c_import_call` respectively. Each likely a small targeted codegen fix.
- **[MED small]** `str(e)` rejected at sema for any enum, even though `repr(e)` works and produces the right output.
  Reproducer: `class Color(Enum): Red = 1; c = Color.Red; str(c)` errors with `str() cannot convert Color`. `repr(c)` on the same value succeeds and prints `"Color.Red"`. Asymmetric: `repr()` is gated on the `Representable` protocol (which enums match via the existing operator<<-fallback path / the new EnumUtil-driven path), but `str()` is gated on `Stringable`, which enums don't satisfy. CPython doesn't make this distinction -- `str(Color.Red)` and `repr(Color.Red)` both work. Fix: either widen `Stringable`'s match to include enums (cheapest), add `__str__`/`__repr__` to the enum-generated surface in sema's protocol-conformance check, or special-case `str()` of an enum in `sema/calls.py` to lower to `::tpy::__str__(e)` directly (the runtime template added for native enums already returns `"TypeName.MEMBER"` for any enum with EnumUtil, so codegen Just Works). Surfaced during the @native enum implementation when the natural smoke test (`print(str(e))`) was rejected. Workaround: use `repr(e)` (same output) or build the string explicitly via the enum's `.name`.
- **[MED small]** f-string interpolation of an enum emits the integer value instead of `"TypeName.MEMBER"`, diverging silently from CPython.
  Reproducer: `class Color(Enum): RED = 1; ...; c = Color.RED; print(f"{c}")` -- TPy prints `1`, CPython prints `Color.RED`. Lives in `tpyc/codegen_cpp/expressions.py:5009`, in the `_gen_fstring` arg-rendering switch: enum args lower to `static_cast<int>(arg)` (the int-value path), not to the str/repr formatting routes used for user types. Pre-existing; surfaced during the `@native enum` planning audit. `repr(c)` and `str(c)` already produce `"Color.RED"` correctly via the operator<<-fallback `__repr__` template. Fix: replace the `static_cast<int>` branch in `_gen_fstring` with `::tpy::__repr__({gen_arg})` (or equivalent) when the arg is an enum and no explicit format spec is given. With a format spec (e.g. `f"{c:d}"`), keep the int cast as today -- format specs are an explicit opt-in to the underlying-value representation.
- **[MED small]** ALL_CAPS-without-Final warning recommends `Final[T]` that's then rejected for `@native` types (upstream #16; user-reported during porting work).
  Reproducer: `BUY: E = native_global("x::E::BUY")` (where `E` is a `@native ValueType`) warns `ALL_CAPS variable 'BUY' without Final annotation; use Final[E] if this is a constant`. Adding the suggested annotation produces `error: Final[E] is not supported; only primitive types ... are allowed`. Compiler is giving inconsistent guidance: the warning leads users into an error. Canonical pattern for binding char/int-backed C++ `enum class` named values (`enum class side_t : char { BUY = '1', SELL = '2' }`). **Fix (pick one):** (a) suppress the ALL_CAPS warning when the variable is bound via `native_global` -- the value is an extern reference whose immutability is guaranteed C++-side; (b) allow `Final[T]` for `@native` types in this position -- the value is compile-time constant from the C++ side and codegen already emits `<qualified-name>::VALUE`. Either resolves the contradiction; (a) is smaller. Less load-bearing than it once was: `@native` on enums now ships (`tests/cases/enum/native_enum*`), so the typical native-enum binding uses `@native class E(Enum): ...` and the `native_global`-per-member pattern that triggers this warning is rarely needed. **Workaround:** prefer the `@native class E(Enum)` form for new bindings; live with the warning if a legacy `native_global`-per-member pattern is required, or rename to lowercase (loses parity with C++ ALL_CAPS spelling).
- **[MED medium]** Generic type-arg inference fails when any later parameter has a union type, even if an earlier parameter fully determines the type variable (upstream #12).
  Reproducer: `def H[M](mod: M, opt: int | None) -> None: ...` called as `H(m, None)` errors `Cannot infer type arguments for 'H'. Specify explicitly: H[M](...)`, even though `mod: M` alone determines `M`. Removing the union (`def H[M](mod: M, x: int)`) makes inference succeed -- so the failure is scoped to the inference walker, not the signature shape. Lives in `sema/type_ops.py:match_type_with_inference` (per prior triage); the union handling short-circuits inference for the whole call rather than just the union-typed parameter. **Fix:** make union-typed parameters non-blocking for inference of *other* type variables -- partial inference from non-union parameters should commit, with the union's contribution bounded by what's already inferred. **Workaround:** pass the type argument explicitly: `H[Mod](m, None)`. Lower urgency in current porting work since upstream #14 removed the generic-helper use case, but the bug stands for any future generic code that mixes type-var params with union-shaped params.
- **[LOW medium]** Readonly-deduced `def m(self) -> T | None` returns `const T*` even when the value is a fresh pointer unrelated to `self`, asymmetric with `Ptr[T]` returns from readonly methods (which stay mutable `T*`).
  Previously latent behind the `Ptr[T]` vs `OptionalType(T)` duplicated-type bug (#17 + #21, now fixed) -- the sema rejection at the assignment site masked the codegen asymmetry. Now reachable: `inline const T* Source::maybe() const` returning a pointer the user expects to be mutable, into `T* field`, fails with `invalid conversion from 'const T*' to 'T*'`. Sketch: in `_resolve_return_type` (functions.py), `const=True` projects through `to_cpp_return_const()` which adds const to pointer-repr `OptionalType`; `PtrType` deliberately doesn't (a readonly method returning `Ptr[T]` stays `T*`). The asymmetry is only safe in TPy because the readonly method body provably can't extract a mutable T* from `self.field` (sema enforces this for direct pointer extraction). **Naive fix attempted in a prior session and reverted:** blanket-dropping the const projection for `Optional[T]` returns broke `tests/cases/records/self_type` -- `find_match -> Optional[Self]` returns `self` (a borrow of the const receiver) and TPy currently relies on the const projection to keep that sound. So the proper fix needs to distinguish "Optional return of a fresh pointer" from "Optional return of a self-borrow" -- options: (a) per-overload return-type adjustment in `@auto_readonly` (the const clone's `Self` becomes `readonly[Self]`); (b) body-walk to detect self-aliased return expressions and force const only there; (c) treat any `Self`-typed Optional return as borrowed by default. **Workaround:** declare the return as `Ptr[T]` instead of `T | None`, or mark the method `@auto_readonly`.
- **[MED medium]** Generic template with `V | None` return + `V = Ptr[T]` substitutes to `Node**` instead of `Node*`.
  Reproducer: `def lookup[K, V](d: dict[K, V], k: K) -> V | None: ...; d: dict[str, Ptr[Node]]; lookup(d, "a")` -- generated C++ template signature is `V* lookup(...)`, and instantiation with `V = Node*` produces `Node** lookup(...)`. The TPy `OptionalType.__new__` collapse fires at the call-site substitution (`OptionalType(PtrType(Node))` -> `PtrType(Node)`), so sema thinks the call returns `Ptr[Node]`, but the C++ template doesn't strip the redundant `*` at instantiation time. Fix shape: either render the template return as `std::conditional_t<std::is_pointer_v<V>, V, V*>` (lift the per-instantiation collapse into the template), or sema-reject `V | None` with V bound to a pointer type and instruct the user to spell the return as `V` directly. The conditional-type fix preserves source-level Pythonic spelling but requires coordinating the template body (any `&result` etc. would need the same conditional). **Workaround:** spell the generic return as `V` (no `| None`) when V is expected to be `Ptr[T]`. **IR note:** part of the borrow/storage-form bug class -- see `docs/IR_DESIGN.md` Open Questions item 9.
- **[LOW small]** Generator `yield` of a storage-form Optional source emits the storage shape into a pointer-form yield slot.
  `gen_yield_value` (statements.py) bridges storage->pointer for *tuple* yields via `_maybe_wrap_tuple_to_pointer`, but has no analogous bridge for bare-Optional yields. A generator whose yield slot is `P*` (pointer-form Optional) but whose body yields a `pairs[i]` where `pairs: list[P|None]` (or a field of a record yielding a storage-form Optional field, or any other `is_storage_form_optional_source` shape) would emit `std::optional<P>` into a `P*` slot and fail C++ compile. Not exercised by current tests. Fix: at `gen_yield_value`, when the yield slot is a pointer-repr Optional and the source is a storage-form Optional source (consult `is_storage_form_optional_source`), insert `tpy::optional_to_ptr(...)`. Parallel to the tuple-yield bridge already in place. **IR note:** part of the borrow/storage-form bug class -- see `docs/IR_DESIGN.md` Open Questions item 9.
- **[LOW small]** Pointer-local first-declared via lvalue OPTIONAL_STORAGE source then rebound from an `Own[Opt[T_ref]]` call return fails C++ compile with a type mismatch; no current reproducer in tests.
  Reproducer (synthetic): `def f(x: Own[P | None]) -> ...: y = x; y = make(...); ...` where `make() -> Own[P | None]`. First decl `y = x` takes the `is_name_src` (lvalue OPTIONAL_STORAGE) path in `_gen_pointer_local_init` and emits `P* y = ::tpy::optional_to_ptr(x);` without allocating a rebind slot. Subsequent rebind `y = make(...)` takes the `is_call_src` path in `_gen_pointer_local_rebind`, finds no `rebind_slots[y]`, and falls through to the generic rvalue path which emits `P __slot = make(...);` -- but `make()` returns `std::optional<P>`, so this fails C++ compile with a type mismatch. Pre-existing on master; the OPTIONAL_STORAGE unification refactor preserved the same defensive fall-through. Fix: in `_gen_pointer_local_init`'s `is_name_src` branch, pre-allocate a `std::optional<cpp_type>` rebind slot when `name in rvalue_reassigned_vars`, parallel to the existing pre-allocation in the None-init and lvalue-lookup paths. Lives in `tpyc/codegen_cpp/statements.py:_gen_pointer_local_init` around the unified OPTIONAL_STORAGE branch. Surfaced by `/tpy-review` of the OPTIONAL_STORAGE unification.
- **[LOW small]** `hash(Box[NotHashable])` produces a generic "No matching overload" diagnostic instead of the specific class-shadowed bound violation message.
  When a class has `__hash__[T: Hashable]` shadowing class T and the receiver is `Box[NotHashable]`, the protocol-conformance path in `tpyc/sema/protocols.py:type_has_method_with_signature` correctly rejects conformance to `Hashable` (so the call is rejected), but it does so by silently dropping the overload via `continue`. The user-facing diagnostic at the `hash()` call site becomes "No matching overload for hash(Box[NotHashable])" rather than the more informative "Method '__hash__' requires type parameter 'T' to satisfy 'Hashable', but 'NotHashable' does not conform" that the operator dispatch path emits. Same root cause: every protocol that walks `type_has_method_with_signature` (Equatable, Comparable, Iterable, etc.) loses the bound-specific reason. Fix shape: thread an optional "rejection reason" back through `type_has_method_with_signature` / `classify_protocol_conformance` so callers (function-call argument check, etc.) can surface the specific class-shadowed bound when that was the sole rejection cause. Surfaced during /tpy-review of the method-level T-shadow sema fix.
- **[LOW medium]** Walrus reassignment of an existing NON-VALUE local (class / list / dict / set / pointer-repr tuple / Optional-of-record) is rejected rather than supported.
  `f = Foo(1); (f := Foo(2))` raises "walrus reassignment of non-value local 'f' is not supported yet". Value-typed walrus reassignment (scalars, str/bytes, views, Optional-of-value) works and is type-checked (`tpyc/sema/expressions.py::_analyze_named_expr` reassignment branch). The non-value case is rejected because its storage form (`T*` / `std::optional<T>` / borrow slot) can't be reassigned in place via the walrus binding path without the var-decl rebind machinery (`rvalue_reassigned_vars` / `rebind_slots` / pointer-rebind), and the walrus codegen's fresh-binding `std::optional<T>`+deref model would otherwise emit a conflicting redeclaration against the existing var. Proper support means routing walrus reassignment through the same rebind path the var-decl reassignment uses, picking up the existing var's storage form instead of the walrus fresh-binding form. Workaround: use a separate assignment statement. Surfaced by /tpy-fix-bug while fixing the silent-miscompile of value-typed walrus reassignment.
- **[MED medium]** Generic-instantiation bypass of the `set[T]` / `dict[K, V]` element-constraint gate: a non-hashable / @nocopy concrete type substituted into a generic function's `T` slips past sema and surfaces as a wall of C++ template errors from std::pair / std::unordered_map internals.
  Reproducer: `def make_set[T](first: T) -> Own[set[T]]: s: set[T] = set(); s.add(first); return s; make_set(Point(1, 2))` where `Point` defines neither `__hash__` nor `__eq__` -- sema accepts; C++ build fails on `std::hash<Point>` / `std::equal_to<Point>` constraints. Same root cause as the literal-vs-annotation gap that was just fixed (`tpyc/sema/type_ops.py::validate_type` routes set/dict annotations through `validate_hashable_container_elem`), but routed through a different code path: at function-definition time `T` is a `TypeParamRef` and the gate explicitly skips with `allow_type_param_ref=True`; at the call site, the substituted parameter / return / local types aren't re-routed back through `validate_type`. Fix shape: after type-arg binding in `tpyc/sema/calls.py` (the generic-function-call resolution path), walk the substituted signature and re-validate any `set[ConcreteType]` / `dict[ConcreteType, V]` via `type_ops.validate_hashable_container_elem`. Same pattern is needed for generic methods, generic-class instantiation, and any other site that substitutes a type-param into a hash-table-backed container slot. Generalizes to other constraint classes that get bypassed by the same TypeParamRef escape hatch -- this is the third instance ("constraint checked at one entry point, bypassed at another"); a clean fix here likely points at a broader "re-validate substituted types at call sites" sema pass. Surfaced during the followup investigation of the annotation-time hashability gate.
- **[LOW small]** Generic-param bound `[T, U: SomeProto[T]]` (cross-param bound referencing a sibling type param) fails to parse with `Parse error: Unknown type: T`.
  Reproducer: `def f[T, U: SomeProto[T]](x: U) -> T: ...`. The parser doesn't accept `T` inside a bound expression for a later type param. **Workaround:** make the function generic over a single param and use cpp_template + `std::remove_cvref_t<decltype({0})>` to recover the concrete type. The v1.2-step-4 `_box_coro` (`lib/tpy/asyncio/_executor.py`) hit this and used the workaround. **Fix shape:** parser should bind earlier type params into scope when parsing later bounds in the same generic list. Self-referential bounds (`[T: SomeBound[T]]`) are a separate concern.
- **[LOW small]** Two `NominalType` instances for the same builtin compare unequal when one was resolved with a module qname and the other without, producing nonsensical "expected `T`, got `T`" diagnostics.
  Reproducer: `class Holder: exc: BaseException | None; ... def main(): h = Holder(); h.exc = BaseException("boom")` (no `from builtins import BaseException`). Errors with `Type mismatch in assignment: expected BaseException, got BaseException`. Workaround: add `from builtins import BaseException` -- the constructor and the field annotation then both resolve with `_module_qname='builtins.BaseException'` and compatibility succeeds. Confirmed via patched `_check_compat` trace: actual is `NominalType(name='BaseException', _module_qname='builtins.BaseException')`, expected is `NominalType(name='BaseException', _module_qname=None)`. The unqualified resolution path comes from the field-annotation type-resolver; the qualified path comes from the catch binding / constructor call which runs `qualify_exception_name`. **Fix shape:** canonicalize `_module_qname` for implicitly-visible builtin types at the resolver layer (so the annotation path lands on the same qualified name as the call path), or make `NominalType.__eq__` ignore `_module_qname` when one side is `None` and the bare names match a unique registry entry. The display formatting collapses both forms to the same user-facing string, which is why the diagnostic looks nonsensical. Surfaced during the Phase-20 fix-shape investigation for the BaseException-slicing entry above; orthogonal to that fix (this one would fire even if storage weren't sliced). Likely affects any unqualified builtin reference in field annotations vs. value positions.
- **[LOW small]** `await` in divergent-type ternary branches rejects with the desugarer's synthetic temp name in the message. `x = await fa() if c else await fb()` where the two branches have different types (e.g. `int` vs `str`) is correctly rejected -- the pre-sema suspension desugar (`tpyc/parse/desugar_suspensions.py`) lowers the ternary to per-branch assignments into one temp, and TPy does not widen a temp across if/else branches -- but the diagnostic reads `Type mismatch in reassignment to '__await_ternary_0': expected int, got str`, leaking the synthetic name for code the user wrote `a if c else b`. Same-type branches work correctly. Fix: tag the synthetic `TpyVarDecl` (e.g. a `desugar_origin` field) so the sema reassignment-mismatch site (`tpyc/sema/statements.py:3172`) rewords to "await in ternary branches with differing types is not yet supported". The short-circuit / `while`-condition / chained-compare eval-order miscompiles in this family are fixed (the desugar pass; tests under `tests/cases/async/await_*`).
- **[LOW small]** `raise StopIteration("msg")` fails to compile: `runtime/cpp/include/tpy/core.hpp:59` declares `struct StopIteration : Exception {};` without `using Exception::Exception;`, so the message-taking constructor is not available. All other built-in exceptions in the family (ValueError, OSError, ..., and the new `StopAsyncIteration` from v1.5 M6) inherit the base ctor. The stdlib's `_exceptions.py` declares `class StopIteration(Exception, ReturnException): ...` with no `__init__` stub, mirroring the runtime asymmetry. Python permits `raise StopIteration("done")` so this is a real gap; in practice it's invisible because TPy iterators raise bare `StopIteration` (the @error_return path doesn't carry a message). **Fix:** add `using Exception::Exception;` to the runtime struct AND a `def __init__(self, message: str = "") -> None: ...` stub to the stdlib class, mirroring `StopAsyncIteration`. Surfaced by /tpy-review of M6 (the new StopAsyncIteration follows the correct pattern; the asymmetry was pre-existing).
- **[MED small]** `@readonly async def f(a: A | B)` (readonly async free function, non-value union param) called with a union *local* from an async caller frame fails C++ codegen: the call site builds deep-const `ptr_variant_to_const<std::variant<const A*, const B*>>(local)` but `local` is a value-variant coro frame field (`std::variant<A, B>`), and `to_const_visitor` can't deduce from a value-variant (`variant_ref.hpp` deduction failure). An async-frame-local-storage interaction (the union local is stored value-variant in the frame, not pointer-variant), orthogonal to the union-param-const verdict. Non-readonly async free fns, sync callers, and `readonly[A | B]` params are unaffected. Workaround: drop `@readonly`, or pass `readonly[A | B]`. Surfaced on `fix-readonly-union-param-const`.
- **[MED small]** Guard-narrowing on a value-union `match` subject is dropped: a class-pattern arm whose guard references the matched subject emits the guard on the raw `std::variant`. Reproducer (plain sync, no generator): `def f(a: int | str): match a:` with `case int() if a > 10:` -> g++ `no match for 'operator>' (operands 'std::variant<tpy::BigInt, std::string>' and 'int')`. The arm body narrows correctly (`__case_N`); only the *guard expression* runs un-narrowed. Pre-existing in `tpyc/codegen_cpp/match.py` -- the guard is emitted before/outside the per-arm subject-narrowing (`narrowed_vars[subject] = __case_N`) takes effect for the guard. **Fix shape:** apply the arm's `case.type_facts` narrowing to the guard expression's codegen too (set `narrowed_vars` for the subject before emitting the guard). **Workaround:** bind the narrowed value first (`case int() as n if n > 10:`). Surfaced while adding `match`-arm narrowing-across-suspension.
- **[LOW small]** `match`-arm subject narrowing across a suspension is not handled for a **recursive-union-alias** subject (value-wrapper `.value` form). The arm-body re-emit at the resume case uses `_emit_isinstance_extractions` (`std::get<T*>(subject)`), but a recursive-union subject is a wrapper struct accessed via `.value`, so the re-emitted access can be ill-formed -- same `VariantAccess`-for-wrapper class as the existing match-field gaps elsewhere in this file. Bare-union and enum subjects (the common case) work; `@dynamic`/polymorphic subjects are sema-rejected for suspending `match`. No in-tree reproducer. **Fix shape:** route the resume re-emit through `VariantAccess` so the `.value` indirection fires for wrapper-struct subjects. Surfaced by Codex /co-validate of the `match`-arm narrowing-across-suspension feature.
- **[LOW small]** Resumable-frame narrowing kill-on-reassignment is an enumerated denylist, so a narrowed frame var rebound by a form it doesn't inspect keeps a stale fact and re-emits a wrong-alternative `std::get`/`dynamic_cast` at the resume case (a silent wrong-type borrow -- `bad_variant_access`, or a null-deref for `dynamic_cast`). `tpyc/codegen_cpp/resumable_cfg.py::_kill_narrowings` covers `TpyVarDecl`, `TpyAssign`(name target), `TpyTupleUnpack`, and merges `TpyAssert` facts, but misses: a walrus `(a := ...)` nested in an expression, a `for a in ...:` loop target reusing the narrowed name, and exception/catch edges into a handler inside a narrowed `try` whose var was reassigned before the throw (the handler BB is stamped at build time, not via a meet over predecessors). **Currently masked, not live:** the realistic triggers are independently broken today -- `for`-target reuse of a captured param hits a pre-existing frame-field redeclaration error, and walrus-rebind-of-a-captured-param in a resumable body is itself broken (see next entry) -- so no clean silent miscompile is reachable purely from this gap; the one clean form (tuple-unpack) is handled. **Proper fix:** derive the kill set from sema's assignment/flow-fact tracking instead of enumerating node types, or compute each block's `entry_narrowings` as a meet over predecessors rather than a build-time stamp (also closes the exception-edge case). Surfaced by /tpy-review + Codex /co-validate of the resumable-frame-narrowing fix.
- **[MED small]** A walrus (`:=`) or `for`-loop rebind of a **captured parameter/local inside a generator/async body** miscompiles. `b = (a := 99)` where `a` is an `int` param yields g++ `request for member 'to_string' in 'a', which is of non-class type 'int32_t'` (the rebind drops the frame field's declared type), and the walrus also doesn't write the captured frame field (a subsequent read sees the old value). Reusing a param name as a generator `for`-target (`for a in xs:`) instead trips `redeclaration of '__gen_*::a'` (the loop var collides with the frame field). Independent of narrowing (reproduces with no `isinstance`). **Fix shape:** route a walrus / loop-target assignment to a captured frame field through the same frame-field write path as a plain `a = ...` reassignment, and suppress the shadowing local re-declaration for a loop target that names a frame field. Surfaced while probing the resumable-narrowing kill-gap during /co-validate.
- **[LOW small]** Async coro `_classify_params` for `Optional[TypeParamRef]` params (`async def f[T](p: T | None) -> ...`) hits the POINTER branch and emits `T*` unconditionally, but at instantiation with value-typed T (e.g. `Int32`) the correct frame shape is `std::optional<T>`. The TypeParamRef branch doesn't fire here because `unwrap_ref_type(ptype)` is `OptionalType`, not `TypeParamRef` -- so the TypeParamRef trait dispatch (`param_val_or_ref_t<T>` / `val_or_ref_t<T>`) never sees the wrapped T. M7's REF branch had the symmetric issue (emitted `std::optional<T>&` regardless of value-vs-reference T). Latent: no current test exercises generic async with `Optional[T]` param. Fix: extend the trait-based dispatch to encode the Optional case, or reject `Optional[unbounded T]` async params at sema until the trait is generalized.
- **[LOW small]** `async for` pays one C++ throw per iteration end. `StopAsyncIteration` is throw-tier (plain `Exception`), not return-tier (`ReturnException`) like its sync sibling `StopIteration`. Lowering `__anext__` to the return tier would give zero-cost iteration termination matching `__next__`, but it's blocked on **`@error_return` + async composition not being designed yet**: an `@error_return` async method would need to return something like `Coro<expected<T, StopAsyncIteration>>`, and the `await` site would need to unwrap both layers; the `try/except` semantics across the suspension are also unspecified. Cost is one throw per loop end (not per element), so amortized cost is invisible for typical async iteration over more than a handful of items but real for very short async loops. **Trigger to revisit:** when async + `@error_return` is designed for any reason (general async error returns, performance need, or a real workload hitting this). **Fix scope when triggered:** design async + `@error_return` composition, auto-decorate `__anext__` to be `@error_return(StopAsyncIteration)` (mirroring how `__next__` is auto-decorated today), make `StopAsyncIteration` a `ReturnException`, and rewrite the `async for` lowering to use the new sentinel shape instead of the synthesized TryRegion+ExceptRegion. See `docs/ASYNC_DESIGN.md` "`async for y in it:` desugaring" section.
- **[LOW small]** Frontend IR `Continue` inside `RepeatUntil.body` is mis-desugared. `tpyc/frontend_ir/lower.py:451-469` rewrites `RepeatUntil` as `while True: body; if cond: break`, but a user-emitted `Continue` IR node inside the body lowers to a `TpyContinue` that jumps to the `while True` header -- skipping the synthesized `if cond: break` that encodes the `until` test. Pascal semantics require `continue` inside `repeat...until cond` to re-evaluate `cond`, not bypass it. Latent today: no in-tree plugin emits `Continue` (the Pascal translator currently rejects `break`/`continue` outright); the gap activates the first time a frontend plugin gains `continue` support and a user writes `continue` inside a `repeat...until`. Fix when triggered: at `RepeatUntil` desugar time, either reject `Continue` in the body with `PLUGIN_IR_INVALID`, or rewrite each `Continue` in the body to jump past the body to the cond-evaluation point. Surfaced by /code-review of the Break/Continue IR scaffolding diff.
- **[MED small]** Four match-pattern record-field union-access sites bypass `VariantAccess` and emit raw `std::holds_alternative<T>(subject.field)` / `std::get<T>(subject.field)`. For a record whose field is a *recursive*-union type, `subject.field` is the wrapper struct (not `std::variant`), so the emitted access is ill-formed. `tpyc/codegen_cpp/match.py:1799, 1802, 1898, 1910` -- known migration debt flagged by the `VariantAccess` helper docstring at `variant_access.py:27`. **Repro shape** (not in any current test): `class Node: child: Tree` where `Tree = int | list[Tree]` plus `match n: case Node(child=Int32() as v): ...`. **Fix:** route these four sites through `VariantAccess(f"{subject_expr}.{field_name}", field_type, is_ptr_variant=False)` so the `.value` indirection fires for wrapper-struct fields. Surfaced by /code-review of the merge-conflict-resolution branch.
- **[LOW small]** `tpyc/codegen_cpp/expressions.py:3540` -- assign-narrowed indirect-name path constructs `VariantAccess(f'(*{name})', None, is_ptr_variant=False)` with `typ=None`, so `_needs_value_indirection` returns False and the `.value` wrapper indirection is missed for indirect references to recursive-union wrappers. **Repro shape** (not in any current test): a module-level `tree: Tree` of recursive-union type rendered as a pointer-global. After assign-narrowing, codegen emits `std::get<int>((*tree))` instead of `std::get<int>((*tree).value)` -- C++ type error. **Fix:** pass the resolved declared type rather than None, or peel one indirection layer in `_needs_value_indirection`. Surfaced by /code-review.
- **[LOW small]** While-loop header isinstance-narrowing alias may collide with a body-level persistent re-narrowing of the same variable, producing a C++ redeclaration. `_gen_while` (`tpyc/codegen_cpp/statements.py:4456`) calls `_emit_isinstance_extractions` with default `persistent=False`; the alias is emitted but not registered in `declared_persistent_aliases`. A body-level `assert isinstance(x, A)` (persistent=True) picks the same name via `_fresh_alias_local` because the set is empty, emitting a second `auto& __x = std::get<A>(x);` in the same C++ block. Same pattern at the if-body extraction (4018), elif chain (4195), else-body (4050). **Repro shape**: `while isinstance(x, A): assert isinstance(x, A); x.a_method()`. **Fix:** either pass `persistent=True` at these four sites, or have the persistent=False emission register the alias name in `declared_persistent_aliases` so the suffix bump kicks in for nested re-narrowing.
- **[LOW small]** Two modules independently declaring structurally-identical recursive union aliases collide in `compiler.union_wrapper_index` via `setdefault` (first-write-wins). The second module's `iter_imported_recursive_unions` then yields the first module's `RecursiveUnionInfo` (origin != second_module_name), so the second module's wrapper-struct definition is skipped while its uses still reference the (missing) local symbol. **Repro shape**: modules A and B both declaring `type Tree = int | list[Tree]` independently (no cross-import). Link error: undefined reference to `tpyapp::b::Tree`. **Fix:** key the index by `(members, defining_module)` or accept the duplication and emit a wrapper in every defining module's TU. Surfaced by /code-review.
- **[LOW small]** `_gen_for_each` (`tpyc/codegen_cpp/statements.py:4868`) snapshots `narrowed_vars` and `declared_persistent_aliases` around the for-body but does NOT save/restore `literal_facts` or `protocol_narrowings`. Body-level isinstance/literal narrowings on free (non-loop) variables leak past the loop end. `_gen_while` at 4444-4445 correctly snapshots all four maps; `_gen_for_each` is divergent. **Repro shape**: `for x in xs: assert isinstance(item, SomeProto); ...` where `item` is a non-loop variable. After the loop, downstream code sees a stale `protocol_narrowings[item]` and routes the wrong typed path. **Fix:** mirror `_gen_while`'s save/restore of all four maps.
- **[LOW small]** `_gen_try_throw` between-block restores at `tpyc/codegen_cpp/statements.py:3354, 3368-3369, 3378-3379` reset `narrowed_vars` and `declared_persistent_aliases` between try/handlers/else, but do NOT touch `literal_facts` or `protocol_narrowings`. A narrowing introduced inside one handler leaks into the next handler or the else body. **Repro shape**: `try: ... except A as e: assert isinstance(p, SomeProto); ... except B: ...`. The second handler's emit sees the first handler's stale `protocol_narrowings[p]` entry. **Fix:** include `literal_facts` and `protocol_narrowings` in the snapshot/restore tuple between try-body / handler / else-body.
- **[LOW small]** `tpyc/codegen_cpp/context.py:1057` -- `LocalScopeSnap` captures `narrowed_vars` and `declared_persistent_aliases` but excludes `isinstance_init_locals`. The dataclass docstring states "If a new what-locals-exist field is added to CodeGenContext, add it here too" -- this field was missed. Constexpr-elif early-return paths at statements.py:3975/4014 emit `}` + return without running the per-iteration `isinstance_init_locals.pop(...)` at 4030-4031, leaking the entry past the if-chain's `restore_local_scope` (4029). Subsequent `_emit_isinstance_extractions` for the same variable finds the leaked entry and routes reads through a dead C++ local. **Fix:** add `isinstance_init_locals: dict[str, str]` to `LocalScopeSnap` and to the snap/restore helpers in `CodeGenContext`.
- **[LOW small]** `make_union` sort order can diverge across phases because `UnionType.__str__` now consults `compiler.union_display_names` (populated mid-sema by `_register_union_wrappers` neighbors at analyzer.py:572). The sort key (`str(t)`) for an inner aliased union changes from the expanded form (e.g. `"Circle | Rect"`) to the alias name (`"Shape"`) once display_names is populated. The same logical union, constructed before vs. after that moment, produces two different canonical `members` tuples; lookups in `union_wrapper_index` / `union_display_names` / `union_alias_names` keyed by `typ.members` then split into two entries. Likely latent today (each union is constructed once per pipeline pass), but a future macro / substitute_type_params / regeneration code path could trigger it. **Fix:** stabilize the sort key independently of display-name registration -- either sort by a phase-invariant string (e.g. `expanded_str()`) or by a member identity that doesn't depend on `__str__`. Surfaced by /code-review.
- **[LOW small]** `compiler.union_display_names` cross-module first-write-wins picks the dependency-topological winner. If a vendored stdlib and the user's module each declare `type X = A | B` with the same canonical members tuple, the vendored module's name leaks into the user's diagnostics. Diagnostic-quality issue (no correctness impact); fix shape: key display_names by `(members, defining_module)` and pick by the diagnostic's owning module, or prefer the entry module's name. Surfaced by /code-review.
- **[MED small]** `Task[T]` handle escaping `asyncio.run`'s scope carries a dangling `Ptr[Awaker]` in its stamped `_waker`. Calling `task.cancel()` on the escaped handle post-run dispatches `wake() -> Awaker.mark_runnable(...)` through a destroyed `Executor`, dereffing freed memory. Same v1 invariant `_ExecutorScope` (in `lib/tpy/asyncio/_executor.py`) documents for every stamped Waker, but the M10 cancel-runnable-mark hook broadened the surface from "Wakers stored in the executor's timer_heap" to "every `Task[T]` returned by `asyncio.create_task`". `drain_spawned_with_cancel` walks `self.slots` and cannot reach external handles. `Waker.wake`'s `try/except BaseException` only covers caught exceptions, not raw memory faults. This is NOT a borrow-param escape (the borrow checker sees a legal ownership move of an owned `Rc` handle; it has no visibility that the handle internally aliases a shorter-lived scoped resource) -- so escape analysis does not reach it. **Disposition (v1): accepted documented footgun** -- the `Task._waker` field comment already documents the invariant, and `asyncio.run` drains non-escaped spawned tasks at teardown, so an escaped-then-cancelled handle is the only reachable path and is unusual. The clean fix is (b) make `Awaker` an `Arc`/`Weak` handle so a post-run wake is a safe no-op -- a runtime design change touching the Awaker protocol / Waker / every wake site, folded into the same THIR-region effort that owns `create_task` ref-param escape (same lifetime model). Option (a) (executor tracks issued `Task` handles and nulls their wakers at `_ExecutorScope.__del__`) is a smaller available-now alternative if this becomes reachable in practice. Surfaced by /code-review of the task-cancel-mark-runnable branch.
- **[MED small]** Async coro **and sync generator** capturing `&list[i]` (or other container-element addresses) via the pointer-form Optional[NonValue] param shape inherits a borrow-lifetime hazard: any list mutation (push_back, pop, clear, insert) during the resumable's lifetime invalidates the captured `T*` pointer, including across suspension/yield boundaries. Existing borrow-checker covers some `T&` shapes via `return_borrows_from` but not this `T*` source: `tpyc/codegen_cpp/expressions.py::_gen_optional_ptr_arg` lifts `T&` to `&T` at the call site, the resulting pointer goes into the resumable-frame, and the caller's borrow checker doesn't see this as an escaping borrow with cross-suspension lifetime. Pre-existing class of bug, newly reachable since `_classify_params` (gen_async.py) and `gen_generator_struct` (gen_generators.py) started emitting `const T*` / `T*` for pointer-form Optional resumable params on the `async-scope-reset` branch. Fix shape: extend `analyzer.py`'s `gen_borrows` injection (currently `func.is_generator` only, line ~1088) to also cover `func.is_async`, then add mutate-during-resumable-lifetime detection at the call site. Surfaced by /tpy-review of the `async-scope-reset` branch. **Same hazard class, additional route (generator-resumable migration Phase D2):** the for-loop pointer-form loop var binds `it = &(*(*__for_it_N)++)` (begin_end) or `it = &(unwrap_ref(...))` (iter_next) -- a `T*` aliasing a container element held across a `yield`/`await` inside the loop body. A structural mutation of the iterable during iteration invalidates it. Partially mitigated by the existing mutation-during-iteration *warning* (e.g. `tests/cases/iterators/gen_borrow_mutate_complex` warns on `items.append(...)` while iterating), but the warning is non-fatal, so the dangle is reachable; same fix direction applies. **Note (declaration-driven yield ABI):** this is now the genuine *residual* of the borrow ABI -- the *consumer-side* retention of a yielded borrow past its iteration step is checked (re-yield-onward / nested-`def` capture rejected; return/stash caught by dangling/lifetime; value-stores copy), but a *caller* that mutates/frees the borrowed container while the generator is live remains only a warning, and is not closed by the consumer-escape check. **Further route (Phase F3):** tuple-unpack for-loops over `list[tuple[..., Rec]]` add a two-level alias -- `__for_tup = T*` aliases the container element, then each non-value unpack target binds `t = &std::get<i>(*__for_tup)` (a `Rec*` into the same element). Same container-mutation invalidation hazard, same partial warning mitigation, same fix direction. **Further route (statement-level borrow alias):** a single-assign (`a = items[0]`) or a tuple-unpack borrow target from a call/name source (`a, b = first_two(items)`) held across a suspension now binds a `T*`/`const T*` frame field aliasing the live source (the borrow-alias-across-suspension fix). Same container-mutation-during-suspension invalidation hazard as the for-loop and param shapes; same escape-analysis fix direction.
- **[LOW small]** `async for` sema branch (`tpyc/sema/statements.py::_analyze_async_for`) ships with two of the four safety analyses that the sync for-loop branch applies; two remain:
   1. **Readonly propagation**: `async for x in some_readonly_aiter:` does not wrap `x` in `ReadonlyType` even when the iterable is readonly-qualified. Near-zero exposure for typical user-defined async iterators (the readonly would have to be on the iterable's value type).
   2. **Dangling-temporary diagnostic**: for iterables that are calls with `return_borrows_from`, the sync branch warns when the borrowed source is a temporary; async-for skips this check. Async iterators are typically user records (not borrow-returning stdlib calls), so exposure is rare.
   Iterable borrow tracking (TpyName path) and `loop_var_iterable` map population shipped with v1.5 M6 -- see `tests/cases/async/async_for_warn_iter_mutation`. Surfaced by /tpy-review of M6.
- **[LOW small]** Missing diagnostic: passing `coro: Awaitable[T]` (non-`Own`) to a consuming sink emits invalid C++ instead of a sema error.
  Reproducer: `def consume_it[T](coro: Awaitable[T]) -> Task[T]: return _make_executor_owned_task[T](coro)`. The non-`Own` param is a borrow (emitted as `T_coro&`); the sink consumes its arg via `make_executor_owned_task<T>(CoroT&&)`. Codegen correctly doesn't move from a borrow, but sema doesn't reject either, so `CoroT` deduces to `T_coro&` and `std::optional<T_coro&>` fails the C++ build with an unhelpful template-stack error. **Workaround:** annotate the param as `Own[Awaitable[T]]`. **Fix:** sema should detect "borrow protocol passed to consuming-only sink" and emit a TPy diagnostic suggesting `Own[Awaitable[T]]`.
- **[LOW small]** Missing diagnostic for non-last-use of an `Own[T]` arg passed to a forwarding-style `@cpp_template`; user gets an unhelpful C++ template error instead of a TPy sema error.
  Reproducer: `def caller[T](coro: Own[Awaitable[T]]) -> Task[T]: task = _make_executor_owned_task[T](coro); return _make_executor_owned_task[T](coro)`. Codegen auto-moves `Own[T]` args at last use (post v1.2 step 2); the first call is not last-use, so `coro` is emitted as a bare lvalue, the C++ forwarding ref deduces `CoroT = T&`, and `std::optional<T&>` fails to compile with a deep template-stack error. **Workaround:** copy explicitly via `tpy.copy(coro)` if you need two uses (only valid for copyable T), or refactor to call once. **Fix:** sema should detect "Own[T] referenced after a non-last-use consume site" and emit a clear TPy diagnostic. (Before v1.2 step 2 this was a runtime use-after-move via a hard-coded `std::move({N})` in the template; now it's a C++ build failure.)
- **[MED small]** Generic `Own[...T...]` params are exempt from the Own-param dangling-return rejection, so a non-value instantiation can still return a dangling borrow. `def first_val[T](items: Own[list[T]]) -> T: return items[0]` is accepted at the generic level (the body is analyzed pre-instantiation, where a `T` return may be by-value), but instantiated with a reference type the returned element borrows the dying owned list. Concrete `Own[T]` roots are rejected (`_name_is_param_or_global` Own-awareness, `tests/cases/auto_move/error_own_param_borrow_return`); the exemption is `contains_type_param` in the same predicate. Closing it needs instantiation-aware return checking (or a conservative generic-level rule that exempts only value-bounded `T`, e.g. `T: ValueType`). Surfaced scoping the Own-param provenance fix.
- **[MED medium]** Simple-generator yields holding references into the iterable (`tuple[..., T]` borrow form) dangle if the iterable is structurally mutated between polls.
  Both simple-for-NativeIterable and simple-while branches in `tpyc/codegen_cpp/gen_generators.py` emit a `make_generator<std::tuple<..., T&>>` slot when the yield type is a non-Optional tuple of non-value elements -- the yielded tuple holds a `T&` into the iterable's backing storage. If the consumer or the lambda's post-yield code structurally mutates the iterable (`items.append(...)`, `items.clear()`, dict insert that triggers rehash), already-yielded references dangle. The borrow checker doesn't track iterator-captured containers, so the unsafe pattern compiles silently. Fix path: extend the borrow checker to track "iterable captured by reference in generator lambda", reject structural-mutation methods on it for the duration of any outstanding yielded reference. In the meantime, falling back to value-form yields (`elem_type.to_cpp()` instead of `to_cpp_return()`) trades performance for safety -- could be wired behind an opt-in if user impact warrants.
- **[MED medium]** `async def` with reference-typed parameters: a `Task<T>` constructed via `asyncio.create_task` (or `Task::from_coro`) that escapes the caller's scope can dangle on those parameters.
  `tpyc/codegen_cpp/gen_async.py:_classify_params` stores non-value-type async-def params as C++ reference fields (`T& param`) in the coroutine struct. When the struct is moved into a `Task<T>` via `create_task` and the Task outlives the parameter's source storage, the reference becomes dangling. Per `docs/ASYNC_DESIGN.md` "Parameter capture": "For escaping calls (`create_task(some_async(items))` where `items` is local to the caller), the borrow checker rejects it." That rejection is not yet implemented. Workaround: pass `Own[T]` so the coroutine struct stores by value, or pass via `Box[T]` / `Rc[T]`, or borrow only module globals (they outlive `asyncio.run`). **Fix is gated on the IR/region migration -- do not bolt this onto the current borrow checker.** A prototype escape+discharge check confirmed the accept/reject boundary works, but it had to key borrow facts on string root names and hardcode the `Task` type / `gather` names -- exactly the "string-key => not designed yet" smell `CLAUDE.md` warns against, and enough ad-hoc machinery that it fights the migration it should be built on. The right shape (Rust's model): this is a lifetime feature. `create_task` should declare a run-stable / `'static`-equivalent **bound on its coroutine argument** in its signature, enforced by the general region checker -- so a user's own `spawn` gets safety for free with no compiler edits; the borrow-a-local-but-join variant is a structured **`TaskGroup`** API whose block scope bounds the borrowed tasks (cf. `std::thread::scope`). Neither is expressible until TPy has lifetimes/regions (the THIR/MIR work); until then this stays an open, documented footgun with the workarounds above. **Extended by M7 (generic async free functions / methods, branch `feat-generic-async-free-funcs`)** to the `_CoroParamKind.TYPE_PARAM` storage form: when T is non-value at instantiation, `val_or_ref_t<T>` resolves to `T&`, so a generic async free function or method called with a borrowed reference-type arg whose source goes out of scope before the next poll has the same dangling-ref class -- not a new symptom, just a new path through which the existing hazard can manifest. Same fix applies. **Extended by H4 (protocol-param generators)** to two more vectors: (a) `_CoroParamKind.STATIC_PROTOCOL` -- a static-protocol param captured as `T_<pname>` deduces to a reference for an lvalue arg, so the same escape applies (`create_task(consume(local_iterable))`); (b) the **sync-generator** field-store form, newly reachable now that protocol-param generators compile -- a generator that borrows a reference-type iterable (`T_it = list&`) stored into a field/container/global (`self.g = echo(local_xs)`) dangles when `local_xs` goes out of scope, with only a non-fatal "mutation while borrowed" warning at the mutation site and no escape diagnostic. The borrow-checker escape analysis is the same fix; it must cover generator factories, not just `create_task`. **Extended by the `_CoroParamKind.FN` form** (Fn params in resumable generators): the callable is forwarded and stored by value in the frame (sound for the callable object itself), but an `Fn` lambda that captures a local *by reference* (`[&local]`) and is passed into an escaping generator dangles when `local`'s scope ends -- the lambda's interior reference targets are not tracked, distinct from the lvalue-arg-deduces-a-reference route but the same escape class and same IR-gated fix.
- **[MED medium]** Borrowed-mode `await` operands can dangle if the awaited reference-type object does not outlive the suspension.
  Async codegen stores reference-type awaitables (for example `Future[T]`) as a raw pointer in the coroutine frame so `await f` preserves object identity and another task can complete the same Future. This is correct for stable lvalues such as frame fields, but it is unsafe if the operand is a temporary or a shorter-lived local whose lifetime ends before the awaiter resumes. The missing borrow check is the same shape as the escaping `create_task` capture bug above: any borrowed await operand must be proven to outlive the coroutine suspension, or the compiler should reject it with a clear diagnostic. **Same gap, now reachable via more arg shapes (await of a static-protocol-param coroutine):** `await consume(rows[0])` where `consume(it: Iterable[int])` and `rows` is a param/field borrows `rows[0]` by reference into the sub-future (the forwarding-deduction field type deduces `U&` for the lvalue) and dangles if the container is structurally mutated across the suspension -- no diagnostic. Sound for a stable, unmutated source (the named-iterable case is the intended, shipped form); the fix is the same await-operand escape/outlives-suspension check, which should cover the inline static-protocol-arg path (`_extra_template_args_for_await`) too. Collection-literal / comprehension args of this shape are already rejected cleanly; the residual is the stable-but-mutable lvalue (subscript / field-chain element). **Another vector (async-with borrowed manager):** a reference-type lvalue `async with mgr:` now stores `mgr` as a `T*` borrow in the coroutine frame (`__with_ctx_<n> = &mgr`) so `__aenter__`/`__aexit__` act on the original (the fix that unified async-with with sync `with`). Sound when `mgr` outlives the suspension (the common synchronous-shaped case); but if the async-with-containing frame escapes (e.g. `create_task`) past `mgr`'s lifetime, the manager pointer dangles -- same missing escape/outlives check, IR-gated like the rest of this entry. **Another route (abandonment-cleanup destructor):** a generator/coroutine frame suspended inside `with mgr:` (borrowed lvalue manager, `with_borrowed_fields` -> `T*` frame field) that ESCAPES the manager's scope -- verified reachable: `def make() -> Own[Iterator[int]]: cm = CM(); g = gen(cm); next(g); return g` compiles with no diagnostic -- now dereferences the dead manager in the frame DESTRUCTOR's `__exit__` cleanup as well as on the resume path (the resume-path deref predates the destructor). Same escape-analysis fix; the destructor adds the abandon-without-resume trigger.
- **[MED medium]** Borrow tracker only checks param names; global mutations are silent (`ref = g[0]; g.append(x)` produces no warning).
  Globals not tracked in borrow system: `mark_param_mutated` only checks `current_param_names` (`context.py:688`), so global variable mutations are invisible to the borrow tracker. `ref = g[0]; g.append(x)` produces no warning.
- **[LOW small]** Non-self param mutation through a FIELD alias borrow is silently dropped from mutation propagation.
  `def f(p: Container) -> None: alias = p.field; alias.mutating_method()` -- the alias is a FIELD borrow of `"p.field"` in the borrow tracker. `methods.py:790` calls `mark_param_mutated("alias")`; `alias` is not in `current_param_names`, and `effective_storage_through_borrows("alias")` returns the dotted key `"p.field"`, which is also not in `current_param_names` -- so `p` is never flagged mutated. Symmetric to the self-mutation gap that was fixed by extending `_is_self_call_deferred` / the methods.py chain walk via `_local_traces_to_self`, but the non-self analog wasn't generalized. Fix shape: extend `mark_param_mutated` so a dotted key whose root is a param name marks the root, mirroring how `loop_var_iterable`'s dotted iterables already do (`context.py:1024-1028`).
- **[LOW small]** Phase-1-immediate vs Phase-2-deferred asymmetry in mutation tracking for borrow-traced receivers; can silently block readonly inference on chained-call methods.
  `tpyc/sema/methods.py:798+` chain walk marks `self_mutated` directly in Phase 1 when chain_root is "self" or borrow-traces to self -- this is conservative and doesn't consult the callee's actual mutation facts. The non-chained path (`_is_self_call_deferred` + call edge with `receiver_is_self=True`) defers to Phase 2 and propagates only if the callee actually mutates. A method that is genuinely readonly but happens to invoke a non-readonly sub-method through a chained call (e.g. `frame.get().pure_read()` where `pure_read` is auto-inferred const) hits the chain walk via the `not info.is_readonly` gate -- which fires because the inference hasn't run yet at Phase 1. Pre-existing conservatism extended in the BUGS-204 fix to cover borrow-traced chain roots. Fix shape: lower the chain walk to recording a call edge with `receiver_is_self=True` (covering each call in the chain) so Phase 2 settles it precisely, mirroring the non-chained path.
- **[MED medium]** Nested function analysis runs in isolated borrow state; nonlocal mutations inside closures don't propagate.
  Nonlocal mutations invisible to outer scope: nested function analysis runs in isolated state (`save_function_state`/`reset_function_tracking`). Mutations to nonlocal variables inside closures don't propagate back to the outer function's borrow tracker.
- **[MED medium]** Borrow check runs in Phase 1 using direct mutation facts; transitive structural mutation through helper methods is missed.
  User-type indirect structural mutation not detected: `_is_invalidating_method` uses `direct_structural_mutated_params` (Phase 1), so `add_twice()` calling `self.add()` which does `self._items.append()` is not caught. Phase 2 propagates `structural_mutated_params` transitively, but the borrow check runs during Phase 1. Fix: add deferred borrow checks after Phase 2, similar to `resolve_pending_borrow_checks`.
- **[LOW medium]** `v = s[0]` where `s: Span[str]` registers `s` as the str-borrow source, but mutations to the backing container don't invalidate `v` (alias chain).
  `Span[str]` subscript view: `SpanType.subscript_borrows()` is intentionally not overridden because `v = s[0]` registers `s` as the str-borrow source, but mutations to the backing container (`arr[0] = "x"` where `s = Span[str](arr)`) call `mark_str_borrowers_mutated("arr")` -- missing `s`. Fix requires `mark_str_borrowers_mutated` to chase the borrow tracker's alias chain so backing-container mutations also invalidate views borrowed through spans.
- **[LOW large]** Only built-in view types (Span, Ptr) are tracked as borrows; user-type view tracking needs the field-vs-class annotation design.
- **[MED small]** Codegen-side narrowing kill misses reassignments inside non-suspending leaf compounds in resumable frames. `resumable_cfg.CFGBuilder._kill_narrowings` is applied per top-level statement in `_build_block`; an inner `if` with no suspension is appended whole to the basic block, so a reassignment of a narrowed variable inside it is never seen and later resume cases re-establish the stale narrowing binding. Sema-side fact kills (loops/handlers/finally, `InitTracker.apply_fact_kills`) don't cover this -- it's the codegen CFG's own narrowing re-establishment. Fix shape: `_kill_narrowings` should walk leaf compounds recursively (or reuse `prescan.collect_fact_kills` on the leaf statement). Surfaced by the 2026-06-10 compiler audit (gap finding on resume narrowing).
- **[LOW small]** A local that is mutable at loop entry but rebound to a readonly source inside the loop body is mutated at the top of the body with no sema rejection (iteration 2 writes through the readonly binding; C++ const backstop catches some shapes). Same single-pass back-edge hole as the narrowing/value-range family fixed by `apply_fact_kills`, but the readonly-ness lives in scope variable types, not in the `FlowFacts` families, so the kill-set mechanism doesn't cover it. Fix shape: at loop entry, re-merge scope types for body-rebound names with the documented "readonly on either branch -> readonly after" policy. Surfaced by the 2026-06-10 compiler audit.
- **[LOW small]** Definite-assignment in `finally` bodies assumes the no-exception path: a variable first assigned inside `try` is treated as definitely-assigned in `finally`, but a mid-try exception reaches the finally with it unbound (C++: default-constructed hoisted slot; CPython: NameError). Deliberately left as-is when the flow-fact kill-sets landed -- the try-vars-hoisted-into-finally pattern depends on it, and the blast radius is wrong-value reads, not memory unsafety. Fix shape: track a "maybe-unassigned on exception path" set for finally bodies and warn (or runtime-check) on reads.
- **[LOW small]** Alias-group fact invalidation misses rebound pointer-locals. `NarrowingTracker._alias_group` reads prescan's `alias_sources`, which deliberately excludes reassigned locals (they become rebindable `T*` pointer-locals, so which object they alias is flow-dependent). A mutation through such an alias (`h2 = h; ...; h2.clear()` where `h2` is later rebound elsewhere in the function) therefore does not invalidate field facts rooted at `h`. Sound handling needs flow-sensitive alias tracking (MIR places/loans); until then the static never-reassigned group is the covered subset. Surfaced implementing the flow-fact kill-sets.- **[MED small]** Field-access call arguments don't invalidate the passed object's field facts. Both the live invalidation (`NarrowingTracker.invalidate_field_facts_for_call` / `_for_method_call`) and the kill-set collector (`prescan._kills_in_expr`) add only bare `TpyName` arguments to the mutated-receivers set, so `f(h.opt)` -- passing a field by mutable reference -- leaves `h.opt.*` sub-path narrowing facts and `len(h.opt)`-derived range facts alive after a callee that mutates the object. Subscript arguments (`f(xs[i])`) share the gap and need separate handling: `_expr_to_narrowing_key` has no subscript form, so the conservative kill there is the container root. Unsoundly elides checks in the same class as the fixed loop/handler holes, but requires sub-path facts on a passed field to bite. Fix shape: extend both sites to `_expr_to_narrowing_key(arg)` (dotted paths), invalidating the key's subtree across its alias group; for subscript args, fall back to `_expr_root_name(arg.obj)` as a receiver.
- **[MED small]** Closures mutating captures without `nonlocal` are invisible to fact invalidation. `closure_written_names` / `prescan._collect_nested_def_writes` collect only `TpyNonlocal`/`TpyGlobal` rebind targets, but a nested def that mutates a captured object in place (`captured.field = None`, `captured_xs.append(...)`) declares neither -- so calling it (`f()`, no name args) invalidates nothing on the live path, and the loop/handler kill-sets inherit the same blindness. Field facts and len-derived ranges on the captured name survive the call: stale check elision, same class as the fixed `nonlocal` leg. Adjacent to the existing nested-function borrow-state entry above (that one is borrow state; this is the narrowing/range-fact leg). Fix shape: collect field-write roots and mutated receivers (not just rebind targets) from nested-def bodies into `closure_written_names`-style receiver kills.
- **[LOW small]** Calls don't invalidate narrowing on module globals; currently shielded by two unrelated restrictions. `if g is not None: clobber(); g + 1` where `clobber()` does `global g; g = None` keeps `g`'s narrowing across the call (only closure `nonlocal` targets are killed at call sites). No silent UB today: reassigning a *non-value* global from a function is rejected outright ("Cannot reassign global variable of non-value type"), and the value-type leg miscompiles into a C++ conversion error (`std::optional<int>` vs `int` -- toolchain-caught, cryptic). The stale fact is still wrong and becomes unsound the moment either restriction is lifted. Fix shape: kill facts for module-global names on calls to callees not proven readonly (Phase-1/2 mutation facts could gate this precisely).
- **[LOW medium]** Hoisted `string_view` (or `const auto&`) loop variable still references container memory after the loop; mutation post-loop dangles it.
  Hoisted non-value loop variable as view: `for s in items: ... print(s)` where `s` is `std::string_view` -- the hoisted `s` is a view into the container, not a copy. If the container is mutated between loop exit and use of `s`, the view dangles. Same concern exists with `const auto&` loop vars. Consider emitting a value copy for hoisted non-value loop variables.
  Additional route (dict-accessor aliasing work): a post-loop-used BORROW-form tuple-unpack target hoists as `T* v = nullptr;` and is assigned per iteration. On a ZERO-iteration loop (empty container) a post-loop read derefs the null pointer -- silent segfault where CPython raises NameError (verified). Same zero-iteration class as the pre-existing hoisted shapes (disengaged optional / stale value), now with a deterministic crash. A test cannot pin this until the class is fixed (UB has no stable snapshot). Proper fix: a sema definite-assignment diagnostic for post-loop reads of loop-bound names not provably assigned (loop provably entered), covering all hoisted shapes at once.
