- **[LOW small]** Structural mutation warnings treat `setdefault` as invalidating while element borrows are live, producing a false positive for the existing-key case: `lst = d.setdefault(k, []); ...; d.setdefault(k2, [])` warns "Mutation of 'd' while borrowed" even though `ordered_map` nodes are heap-stable and insert never invalidates element borrows. `__setitem__` carries `@native_preserves_refs` for exactly this reason; whether `setdefault` (and other node-stable inserters) should too needs its own analysis -- the flag is a safety assertion and `setdefault`'s new `V&` return interacts with later `del`/`pop` of the same key. Surfaced by /tpy-review of the dict-accessor aliasing branch.
- **[MED medium]** Async return convention is borrow-form for pointer-repr Optional (scalar B41 fixed) but NOT yet for its siblings, which still diverge from the sync convention. The scalar `async def -> T | None` case is fixed: `_ret_cpp` returns `Poll<Box*>`, the await binding aliases the source like the sync twin and CPython (safe because reference-type params live in the frame by reference (`H& h`), so the returned borrow points into durable caller storage that outlives the suspension, and the existing dangling-return check already gates what may be returned -- a fresh `return Box(n)` is rejected). Remaining siblings keep storage form and so fail to compile or copy where sync aliases:
  - **Pointer-variant Union async return** (`async def -> A | B: return h.pet`): `_ret_cpp` keeps `Poll<std::variant<A,B>>`. The await-**return** ABI half is straightforward and was prototyped working: `_ret_cpp` -> `Poll<variant<A*,B*>>`, the return lifts via `to_ptr_variant` (the cluster-#9 `convert()` chokepoint), the await binding assigns (not `.emplace()`) into a bare `variant<A*,B*>` frame field, and `isinstance` narrowing uses `std::get<A*>` -- all driven by classifying the union frame-local as a ptr-variant local (`context.py` frame-local loop, mirroring the pointer-repr Optional branch). The **blocker** is the consumer side: classifying union frame-locals as ptr-variant broadly regresses a *direct* storage binding (`pet = h.pet`, union field -> local) -- the async body emits a plain `pet = h.pet` assignment with NO `to_ptr_variant` lift, where the sync var-decl emits `pet = ::tpy::to_ptr_variant(h.pet)`. Unions have a value-vs-pointer-variant binding duality that Optionals lack (a pointer-repr Optional local is uniformly `T*`), so the clean Optional precedent does not transfer. A correct fix needs EITHER (a) async-body frame-local bindings to apply form-conversion lifts for storage sources -- a general async-binding gap overlapping the deferred 2c -> THIR work (migrate async-body binding sites onto `convert()`); OR (b) classify ONLY await-result union locals as ptr-variant (thread await-bind-target info into the `context.py` classification), leaving direct-bound union locals storage. Repro: `async def get(h) -> A | B: await asyncio.sleep(0); return h.pet` then `t = await get(h); if isinstance(t, A): t.x` -- g++ `holds_alternative<A>(frame_slot<variant<A,B>>)`. Attempted under `/tpy-fix-bug` and backed out (the broad classification regressed `pet = h.pet`); the await-return ABI groundwork is understood and re-applicable once the consumer-binding approach is chosen.
  - **async-generator yield slots** and **`Task[T | None]` results** -- likely the same storage-vs-borrow convention gap on the yield / Task-payload path; unverified.
  Needs `/tpy-fix-bug` for the Union consumer-binding + narrowing work. The scalar Optional fix landed on `borrow-storage-convert-chokepoint` (audit finding B41).
- **[MED medium]** A borrowed pointer-repr `T | None` loses its borrow provenance when it flows through a ternary or if/else branch-join, so a later narrowed `return` move-guts the borrowed source.
  Repro: `def first[T](xs: list[T]) -> T | None: for x in xs: return x; return None` then `def via(xs, c): y = first(xs) if c else None; if y is not None: return y; return -1`. `first` returns a borrow (`T*` into `xs`); the ternary join (`make_union`) correctly keeps the pointer-repr Optional (post-B38) but drops the call-result borrow fact, so codegen treats `y` as an owning `T | None` local and emits `return std::move((*y))` -- gutting `xs[0]`. Observable for heap-backed `T` (`int`/BigInt -> 0, `str`/`list` emptied); invisible for trivially-copyable `T` (`Int32`). The plain-assignment form (`y = first(xs)`, no join) is correct -- borrow provenance survives and no move is emitted. Same defect via the `if c: z = first(xs) else: z = None` branch-merge. CPython returns the value without disturbing the source; no TPy diagnostic. This is the B30 / cluster-#5 family (auto-move on a borrowed pointer-repr source); the join is where provenance is lost. **Workaround:** bind the borrowing call to a plain local before the conditional (`y = first(xs)`), or declare the callee return `Own[T] | None` so the value is genuinely owned. **Fix shape:** propagate the call-result (and field/element) borrow fact through ternary / branch-merge joins in the BorrowTracker, so the last-use move at `return` is gated on provable absence of a borrow -- the gate `fix-auto-move-borrow-gate` applied at direct consume sites, extended to join-merged locals. Surfaced while landing the B38 `force_pointer_repr`-join fix (cluster #9), which unmasked it (the pattern did not compile before). Minor adjacent wart: the branch-merge form also emits a dead `std::optional<T> __slot_N;` materialization slot for the pointer-form local.
- **[LOW small]** Borrow/storage chokepoint (`convert()`) residuals -- latent, surfaced by `/tpy-review` of the chokepoint branch. (a) `FormValue.move` is set nowhere (defaults False) and only read on the Optional STORAGE->BORROW path; a future caller that needs a move but forgets to set it gets a silent copy-instead-of-move -- wire it at the move-aware site or drop the field. (b) Untested suspicion: a walrus from a Union value-variant field (`if (t := h.pet) is ...:`) may hit the non-value non-Optional fallback `(name = code, *name)` which dereferences a `std::variant` directly (likely ill-formed); may be sema-blocked earlier. Whole class is reabsorbed by THIR form-as-node-fact (IR_DESIGN OQ9). (The `source_form()` TpyCoerce-peeling inconsistency that was also here is fixed -- `source_form` now peels `TpyCoerce` internally so all call sites classify the underlying source uniformly.)
- **[MED small]** `return StrView(local_str)` not caught -- explicit StrView construction from a local owned string slips through the return-dangling check.
  Both `def f() -> StrView: s = some_str(); return StrView(s)` (non-generic) and `def f[T](x: T) -> StrView: s = str(x); return StrView(s)` (generic) compile silently and produce a dangling view at runtime. The output starts with garbage bytes because `s` is destroyed at function return. The check at `compatibility.py:check_dangling_reference` covers the implicit-coercion case (returning `s` directly with declared return `StrView` is rejected) and explicit `StrView(s + "!")` constructed from a temporary, but the `_view_constructor_arg` path gates on `expr.call_type is not None` -- which is not set when `StrView(s)` is analyzed as a regular function call. The fallback `is_dangling_return(TpyCall(StrView, ...))` reaches the "regular function call -- assume safe" branch and returns False. Same gap inherited by the new lambda/yield view-dangling checks for `lambda x: StrView(local_str)` patterns where `local_str` is a named local rather than constructed inline. Fix: extend `_view_constructor_arg` to recognize `StrView(...)` / `BytesView(...)` / `Span[...](...)` calls regardless of the `call_type` setting, then check whether the constructor's argument is dangling.
- **[LOW small]** `_inherits_protocol` (sema, `tpyc/sema/type_ops.py:1215-1238`) and `directly_implements_dynamic` (codegen, `tpyc/codegen_cpp/protocols.py:225-268`) disagree on non-`@dynamic`-rooted protocol chains.
  Reproducer shape (not yet exercised by a test): `class Concrete: ...` implementing a regular protocol `NonDynProto` that extends a `@dynamic DynProto`. For `inferred[T] = Concrete` with LHS hint `Container[DynProto]`, sema's Layer 1 (`_apply_lhs_hint_to_function_return`, branch `rc-dyn-protocol`) sees `_inherits_protocol(Concrete, DynProto) = True` (walks via `is_subtype` which includes protocol-extends-protocol chains) and skips the LHS-hint switch, keeping `T = Concrete`. Codegen's `directly_implements_dynamic(Concrete, DynProto)` returns `False` (only walks user-record inheritance through `@dynamic` ancestors), so it would emit an Adapter wrap -- contradicting sema's "no wrap needed" claim. End state: either a C++ compile error or silent slicing, depending on the wrapper. Pre-existing divergence; newly reachable on the function-inference path after `rc-dyn-protocol` shipped Layer 1. **Fix shape:** align the two predicates -- either codegen learns to walk through non-`@dynamic` protocol intermediates, or sema's helper restricts to the same path codegen uses. Surfaced by `/tpy-review` of the `rc-dyn-protocol` branch (safety-model specialist). Note (single-alloc Rc): a third "does X C++-inherit @dynamic P's base?" predicate now exists -- `ProtocolChecker.directly_implements_dynamic` (sema, `tpyc/sema/protocols.py:151`), a hand-copied mirror of the codegen helper used to decide `representational_subst_params`. It tracks codegen today, but the lockstep surface is now four-way: the isinstance-through-protocol-borrow work added a fourth caller of the sema mirror at `tpyc/sema/calls.py` (`_validate_polymorphic_subclass_dispatch` -> `self.protocols.directly_implements_dynamic`). A fix here must keep the sema mirror and codegen copy identical while reconciling `_inherits_protocol`. The clean end-state is one shared predicate both phases call (defer to THIR, cf. the default-ctor-predicate unification in TODO.md).
- **[LOW small]** `AliasRef.is_send()` / `is_sync()` return `False` unconditionally, so a recursive union alias whose members are all `Send` (or `Sync`) reports as not-Send / not-Sync when the alias placeholder is encountered.
  `tpyc/typesys.py::AliasRef` is the parser-emitted self-reference inside a recursive alias body (`type Tree = int | list[Tree]` -- the inner `Tree`). Returning `False` matches the prior bare-`NominalType` placeholder behavior so codegen branching (`UnionType.is_value_type()`, `uses_pointer_repr()`) stays unchanged. The correct value would resolve the alias and aggregate its members, but that's a fixpoint computation. Reachable when sema asks Send/Sync of a `UnionType` member that is an `AliasRef` (or container thereof, e.g. `list[Tree]`). Latent: no current test exercises a recursive union across a thread boundary where rejection would be incorrect. **Fix shape:** treat the alias body's resolved Send/Sync as a least-fixed-point over members, breaking the recursion conservatively (e.g. assume `True` during own resolution and refine if a member proves `False`); or resolve the AliasRef via the registry at the question site. Surfaced by `/tpy-review` of the union/variant unification.
- **[LOW small]** Async coro frame holds `Counter& __self` bound to `*__for_itr_0` where `__for_itr_0` is an `std::optional<Counter>` frame field; the reference's validity depends on the optional not being reset/reassigned between emplace calls.
  `tpyc/codegen_cpp/gen_async.py` emits the for-loop iterable as a `std::optional<T>` slot (loop-source storage) and a `T&` field for the sub-coroutine's iterator binding. Current emission only `emplace()`s the optional once per loop entry, so the reference stays valid for the loop's lifetime; however, the invariant "optional not reset between emplace and final read" is not enforced anywhere. Any future change that resets `__for_itr_N` on loop restart (e.g. a `continue`-to-top rewrite, or a generator iterable that re-yields) would silently dangle every pending `T&` capture in the sub-coro frame. **Fix shape:** either store the iterable by value-with-fixed-address (e.g. `T` directly in a frame field) so no dependent reference can ever dangle, or add an invariant assertion at every reset site that no sub-coro is currently parked on this iterable. Surfaced by `/tpy-review` of the async-control-flow branch.
- **[MED medium]** Post-`is None` isinstance narrowing on a recursive-union `T | None` parameter produces wrong C++.
  Repro: `type Tree = int | list[Tree]; def f(t: Tree | None) -> int: if t is None: return -1; if isinstance(t, int): return t; return len(t)`. Sema correctly narrows `t` to `list[Tree]` for the post-`isinstance(t, int)` early-return path, but `_filter_union_codegen_facts` in `tpyc/sema/statements.py` filters the fact out because the declared type post-`_fix_recursive_optional_annotations` is `OptionalType(AliasRef('Tree'))` -- not a UnionType for the existing `isinstance(_peel(declared), (UnionType, AnyType))` check. Even when the filter is loosened (peel OptionalType(AliasRef) -> alias body), the downstream codegen at `_emit_isinstance_extractions` substitutes `narrowed_vars[t] = '__t'` while the variable was originally rendered as `Tree* t` (pointer-form Optional), so reads of `t` continue to emit the `(*X)` deref pattern -- producing `(*__t)[0]` and `__len__((*t))` where `__t` is a std::vector and `t` is a struct, both ill-formed. **Both layers** need a coordinated fix: (1) `_peel` in `_filter_union_codegen_facts` should resolve `OptionalType(AliasRef)` to its alias body so the fact survives the filter; (2) `_emit_isinstance_extractions` should detect the pointer-form-Optional input shape (`var_name` is a `Tree*` param) and bind the alias as a value reference (`auto& __t = std::get<list<Tree>>(t->value);`) rather than as a wrapper-struct alias that the caller then re-derefs. **Workaround today:** introduce a local copy of the variable after the `is None` check (`tree = t  # mypy: assert-not-none`) and isinstance-narrow that local; the local's storage form is the wrapper struct directly, not Tree*. Surfaced by the union/variant unification's attempt to add coverage for this shape.
- **[LOW small]** Inner `__finally_exc_<n>` slot can stay stale across CFG-finally re-entry when the inner finally body itself raises. The slot is set by the inner TryRegion's catch when the try body raises; it is cleared by inner `AsyncFinallyExit`'s rethrow / forwarding code. But if the inner finally body raises a NEW exception *before* reaching AsyncFinallyExit (the raise propagates directly to the outer catch via the C++ stack), inner's AsyncFinallyExit never runs and inner's slot stays populated with the original exception. Today the inner is exited and the coro continues to the outer finally, so the stale field is never re-read -- but a future re-entry to the same inner try (e.g. inside a loop) would observe stale state. Fix when triggered: clear `__finally_exc_<n>` at inner FinallyRegion entry, OR at the C++ catch that captures the new exception in an outer region. Latent today; not introduced by H3, but surfaced during the design pass.
- **[MED medium]** `print(x)` of a union/Optional-typed value fails the C++ build -- the print path has no `operator<<` for `std::variant`. Reproducer: `x: int | str = 5; print(x)` errors at the C++ stage with `no match for 'operator<<'` on `std::variant<tpy::BigInt, std::string>` (the `tpy::operator<<(ostream&, const Any&)` candidate doesn't convert). `repr(x)` and `f"{x}"` work -- they route through the variant-visiting formatter -- but bare `print()` does not. Holds for value-unions, pointer-variant record unions, and even assignment-narrowed unions (`x: int | str = 5`). `repr(x)` / `f"{x}"` are working alternatives, so this is a specific-pattern gap rather than a first-program blocker -- but still a surprising miscompile on a feature marked "Done". **Fix shape:** route `print()`/`str()` of a union value through the same variant-visiting `__repr__`/formatter the f-string and `repr()` paths already use, instead of emitting a bare `os << value`. Surfaced during the feature-completeness audit (probe `p_union_print.py`).
- **[MED small]** `@dataclass` `__post_init__` is silently ignored -- it compiles clean and never runs, no diagnostic. Reproducer: `@dataclass class P: x: int` with `def __post_init__(self) -> None: self.x = self.x * 2`; `P(5).x` is `5` in TPy, `10` in CPython. The dataclass macro generates `__init__` from the annotated fields but does not emit the trailing `self.__post_init__()` call CPython's dataclass appends. Silent wrong output on a common dataclass idiom (validation / derived fields). `DATACLASS_DESIGN.md` notes it as "low priority", but the silent-accept (rather than sema-reject-until-implemented) is the footgun. **Fix shape:** in the dataclass class-macro, if the class defines `__post_init__`, append a call to it at the end of the generated `__init__` (and forward `InitVar` args once those are supported). Surfaced during the feature-completeness audit (probe `p_postinit.py`).
- **[LOW small]** `@function_macro` ignores `MACRO_DEPS`: `_populate_macro_deps` (`tpyc/sema/analyzer.py`) has class-macro (`all_records()`/`pending_macros`) and builder-macro branches but no `module.functions` walk, so a function-macro module declaring `MACRO_DEPS` does not get its deps folded into `macro_ns` -- synthesized code referencing a dep symbol would fail resolution. Latent: no current function macro uses `MACRO_DEPS`. **Fix shape:** add a `module.functions` branch mirroring the class/builder paths. Surfaced by /tpy-review (architecture-fit) of the `@function_macro` spike.
- **[LOW small]** `@function_macro` is not recognized through a re-export chain: there is no `SymbolKind.FUNCTION_MACRO`, and `_resolve_macro_chain` / `_MACRO_KINDS` (`tpyc/sema/analyzer.py`, `tpyc/symbol_binding.py`) enumerate only CLASS/CALL/BUILDER macro kinds. A `@function_macro` imported via an intermediate re-exporting module is not installed as a macro binding, and `_resolve_macro_chain`'s registry probe omits `get_function_macro`, so the lookup silently fails. Latent: the tests import the macro directly. **Fix shape:** add `FUNCTION_MACRO` to `SymbolKind`/`_MACRO_KINDS` and the registry probe. Surfaced by /tpy-review (architecture-fit) of the `@function_macro` spike.
- **[LOW small]** `FunctionMacroContext.replace_expr` (`tpyc/macro_api.py`) has no guard against reusing one `new` node instance for two distinct `old` nodes; sema then annotates the shared AST object with conflicting coercion/type facts from two call sites (last write wins), a silent miscompile path. No current macro does this. **Fix shape:** deep-copy `new` per replacement, or reject a node already spliced elsewhere. Surfaced by /tpy-review (safety-model) of the `@function_macro` spike.
- **[LOW small]** `FunctionMacroContext.annotate_local` via the `TypeInfo.raw_type` escape hatch can set a local's declared type to a sema-internal `RefType`: `_analyze_var_decl` rejects `OwnType`/`ReadonlyType` on locals but has no `RefType` guard, so a macro could inject a `RefType`-annotated local that bypasses the borrow-form/storage-form distinction. Latent: no current macro extracts `raw_type`. **Fix shape:** reject `RefType` (and audit other sema-internal wrappers) in `_analyze_var_decl` alongside `OwnType`/`ReadonlyType`. Surfaced by /tpy-review (safety-model) of the `@function_macro` spike.
- **[LOW small] A compile-time-only call (`assert_send[T]()` / `assert_sync[T]()`) used in a value position emits an undeclared C++ identifier instead of a clean sema rejection.** The codegen elision (`TpyCall.compile_time_assert`) only fires in the `TpyExprStmt` arm, and sema doesn't reject a `VOID`-typed call result in a value position, so `x = assert_send[T]()` reaches expression codegen and emits a call to a nonexistent C++ function -> g++ error. Toolchain-caught (not silent UB), and the asserts are statement-only by intent so this is misuse. Pre-existing `VOID`-in-value-position gap shared with `print()` (but `print` emits valid C++). **Fix shape:** reject a compile-time-assert call (or any `VOID` result) used as a value in sema, with a clear "statement-only" message. Surfaced by /tpy-review (codegen-correctness) of the Send/Sync Phase 3 diagnostic surface.
- **[LOW small] `re.sub` / `Pattern.sub` `count` is typed `Int32`, narrower than CPython's arbitrary-precision `int`.** A `count` above `Int32.MAX` (2147483647) would overflow where CPython accepts it. Practically unreachable (nobody passes 2B+ replacements), but the signature diverges. **Fix shape:** widen `count` to `int` (BigInt) or document the cap. Surfaced by /tpy-review (cpython-parity) of the re.sub count feature.
- **[LOW small] An owned-`String` (`tpy.String`, param `const std::string&`) overload param has the same string-literal mis-rank as `str`, but the str-literal pin excludes it.** The pin in `gen_call_arg` (`_wants_str_literal_pin` + the `is_str_type`/`is_str_view_type` guard) only fires for `str`/`StrView` params (C++ `std::string_view`); a `String` param renders as `const std::string&`, where a bare `"x"` literal (`const char*`) still has `const char*->bool` outranking the user-defined `->std::string`, so an overloaded callee with a `String` param and a competing `bool`/pointer overload would silently mis-bind. Excluded deliberately because `std::string_view("x")` does not implicitly convert to `const std::string&`. **Fix shape:** for a `String` (owned) param, pin to `std::string("x")` instead of the view form. Unverified whether any current code uses a `String`-typed overload param. Surfaced by /tpy-review (codegen-correctness) of the str-literal overload pin.
- **[LOW small, perf] A `native_global` constant reads through an extern global at each use instead of an immediate.** A `Final[T] = native_global("sym", binding="C")` lowers every use site to a load of the extern symbol (`::sym`), where a plain `Final[T] = <literal>` would be a compile-time constant the C++ compiler folds into an immediate. The socket/errno constants in `socket_impl.cpp` (`tpy_const_sol_socket = SOL_SOCKET`, etc.) thus cost a memory load per use (e.g. in `setsockopt`/`getsockopt`/errno comparisons) rather than an immediate -- a tiny but real regression vs the previous hardcoded `Final` literals. Inherent to native_global's cross-TU model: the value comes from a system macro visible only in `socket_impl.cpp` (the header is deliberately hidden from consumer TUs), so the consumer can't see it at compile time. **Fix shapes (any):** (a) LTO would const-propagate the global-initialized-from-constant across TUs; (b) mark the C++ definitions `const`/`constexpr` and teach native_global to emit a matching `extern const` decl, enabling more aggressive folding; (c) for non-cross-compilation builds, let tpyc query the host's constant values and emit literals (wrong for cross-compilation, so gated). Surfaced by the macOS socket-constant portability (`feat-macos-kqueue-reactor`). Optimization, not a defect.
- **[LOW small] The macOS self-pipe / kqueue wake-fd setup ignores `fcntl` failures (review-only `#else` branch).** In `runtime/cpp/src/stdlib/signal_impl.cpp` `create_wake_fds()` (macOS branch) the `fcntl(F_SETFL, O_NONBLOCK)` and `fcntl(F_SETFD, FD_CLOEXEC)` return values on the self-pipe ends are unchecked; likewise the kqueue fd's `fcntl(F_SETFD, FD_CLOEXEC)` in `epoll_impl.cpp` `tpy_epoll_create()`. Two consequences if a (rare) `fcntl` fails: a blocking write end means `on_shutdown_signal` could `write()`-block in the signal handler (deadlock risk if the pipe ever fills); a missed CLOEXEC leaks the fd across `exec`. The Linux path is immune (`eventfd(EFD_CLOEXEC|EFD_NONBLOCK)` / `epoll_create1(EPOLL_CLOEXEC)` set both atomically). **Fix shape:** in `create_wake_fds()` check every `fcntl` and fail (close fds, return -1) on error; do the same for the kqueue CLOEXEC set. NOTE: the macOS `#else` branches do not compile on Linux CI, so this must be edited + built on a macOS box (or with libkqueue), not blind-patched here. Surfaced by /tpy-review (runtime-cpp-correctness, safety-model) of `feat-macos-kqueue-reactor`.
- **[LOW small] `tpy_signal_consume`'s `close_wake_fds()` zeroes the wake-fd globals after `close()`, not before (review-relevant for future threading).** In `runtime/cpp/src/stdlib/signal_impl.cpp`, `close_wake_fds()` copies `g_wake_rd`/`g_wake_wr` into locals, `close()`s them, then zeroes the globals -- leaving a window where the still-pending signal handler reads a just-closed (potentially recycled) fd from `g_wake_wr`. Harmless under today's single-threaded asyncio (the handler runs synchronously on the same thread, never during teardown), but a real hazard if the reactor ever becomes thread-aware. Fix shape: zero the globals before the `close()` calls. Same review-only `#else`-adjacent file as the entry above; fold the fix in when that file is next edited on macOS. Surfaced by /tpy-review (safety-model) of `feat-macos-kqueue-reactor`.
- **[LOW small] A `native_global` `Final` is not admitted into `analyzed_finals`, so it can't seed a later `Final` initializer.** In `tpyc/sema/statements.py` the `native_global` branch (~line 3451) sets `stmt.linkage`/`native_name`, clears `stmt.init`, and `return`s before the `analyzed_finals.add(stmt.name)` at ~line 3578. The name is registered in `final_globals` (so reassignment is blocked) but not in `analyzed_finals`, so a subsequent `X: Final[Int32] = SOL_SOCKET + 1` in the same module would be rejected as "requires a compile-time constant initializer" even though `SOL_SOCKET` is a `Final`. Pre-existing (`statements.py` not touched by `feat-macos-kqueue-reactor`); no current code composes a native_global into another Final. Inconsistent invariant: a native_global Final isn't a compile-time constant in TPy's model, so admitting it into `analyzed_finals` would also let it leak into genuinely compile-time contexts (array sizes) where it can't be folded -- so the right fix may be to keep it out AND give a clearer diagnostic, not just add it. Fix shape: decide the intended semantics, then make `final_globals`/`analyzed_finals`/the array-size check agree. Surfaced by /tpy-review (safety-model) of `feat-macos-kqueue-reactor`. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] The `@builtin_type` / template constructor sema path (`_analyze_template_constructor`, `tpyc/sema/calls.py`) does not accept a `@dynamic`-protocol-typed argument for a `Ptr[protocol]` param, while the normal user-record ctor path does.** Constructing a `@builtin_type` record whose `__init__` takes a `Ptr[P]` param (P a `@dynamic` protocol) and passing a `P`-typed value rejects-valid with `<Name>() cannot convert (P, ...)` -- the `P -> Ptr[P]` arg coercion (the address-of/adapter conversion the normal record-ctor resolution applies) is missing in this path. A plain user class *and* a plain `ValueType` ctor both accept the same `P -> Ptr[P]` arg; only the builtin/template construction path falls through to the "cannot convert" arity/type fallthrough. The same coercion *does* work in assignment position (`q: Ptr[P] = p`), which is the workaround (bind a `Ptr[P]` local, then pass it). Reachable only by stdlib authors (`@builtin_type` is not user-facing); no user-facing repro. Hit building the immutable-`ValueType` migration: `Waker(@builtin_type, ValueType)` in `lib/tpy/tpy/coro` takes `awaker: Ptr[Awaker]` (Awaker is `@dynamic`), and `_make_waker` (`lib/tpy/asyncio/_executor.py`) had to route through `awaker_ptr: Ptr[Awaker] = handle` instead of `Waker(handle, ...)`. Sibling of the codegen-side `_gen_union_arg`-missing-on-the-`call_type` path entry above (both are the builtin/template ctor path lacking an arg-conversion arm the bare-name path has). **Fix shape:** run the dynamic-protocol -> `Ptr[protocol]` arg coercion in `_analyze_template_constructor`, mirroring the normal record-ctor resolution. Surfaced building the immutable-`ValueType` feature. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.

## Safety / borrow checker
- **[LOW small] (parity, not unsound) Binding a borrow-yield's `next()` result to a local silently COPIES where CPython aliases.** `g = gen(); a = next(g); b = next(g)` over a generator that yields a borrow (`Iterator[list[int]]` yielding a frame-local / param / self-field) binds `a`/`b` as independent owned copies snapshotted at each `next()`, so a later mutation through `a` is not seen via `b` (or the source), nor vice versa -- CPython aliases all of them. No warning fires (unlike the `copies ... into owned storage` warning that DOES fire for a container-insert / field-store of the same value). Memory-safe (the copy owns its storage, so it survives frame destruction); purely an observable copy-vs-alias divergence. Pre-existing and independent of the frame-local borrow-yield feature -- it manifests identically for the param/self-field borrow yields that already worked; the consumer-side binding is what copies, regardless of the producer root. The for-loop consumer is unaffected (the loop var is a live borrow -- mutation IS visible on resume, matching CPython). **Fix shape:** either bind `next()` of a borrow-yield as a borrow (alias) under the same ephemeral-borrow rule the loop var uses, or at minimum emit the existing copy-into-owned-storage warning so the divergence is acknowledged + `copy()`-silenceable. Surfaced probing the yield-frame-local-borrow feature.
- **[MED small, deferred: MIR] Reassigning a narrowed value-Optional iterable to `None` inside a resumable-frame for-loop dangles the frame's iterator (use-after-free).** In a generator/async, `for x in b: b = None; yield x` over a narrowed `b: bytes | None` / `str | None` sets the frame's owned `std::optional<V>` field to `nullopt`, destroying the buffer the frame's `__for_it` / `__for_end` iterator fields borrow into; the next resume reads dangling iterators (UB). Sema's reassign-of-borrowed check skips value-Optionals (`OptionalType(bytes).is_value_type()` is True), so no diagnostic fires. This is another instance of the general resumable-iterable reassign-escape gap (a borrow into the iterable invalidated by reassigning/mutating it mid-loop) -- NOT a fresh root cause; the value-Optional shape became reachable when narrowed-value-Optional for-loop iteration was fixed (it was a C++ build error before). The non-resumable (sync) case is safe -- `string_view`/`span` iterators point into external data, not the optional object. Region-checker territory (cf. the async borrow-escape / `TaskGroup` work, IR-gated); do NOT re-attempt as a standalone value-Optional patch. Requires a deliberate reassign-while-iterating pattern. Surfaced by /tpy-review (safety-model) of the narrowed-Optional for-loop fix.
- **[LOW small] Field-assign through a raw `Ptr` to a readonly-payload `Deref` (`Ptr[Rc[readonly[T]]]`) is ungated -- it can reach the readonly payload silently.** The deref-target readonly check in `_enforce_readonly_assignment_target` (`tpyc/sema/statements.py`) excludes `PtrType` receivers (a `Ptr[readonly[T]]` keeps its own "assign through read-only pointer" diagnostic via `is_readonly_ptr`). But a `Ptr` whose pointee is itself a readonly-payload `Deref` handle -- `p: Ptr[Rc[readonly[Counter]]]`, then `p.n = 5` (auto-deref through the Ptr, then through the Rc, onto the readonly payload) -- is gated by neither: `is_readonly_ptr(Ptr[Rc[...]])` is False (the Ptr pointee isn't `readonly`), and the new deref-target check skips all `PtrType` receivers. Pre-existing relative to the deref-readonly fix (neither closed nor worsened by it), and obscure (requires a raw `Ptr` to an `Rc[readonly[T]]`), but the readonly payload is reachable for mutation with no diagnostic. Fix shape: when the receiver is a `Ptr` whose pointee is a `Deref` type, still peel the inner deref chain for a readonly target (don't blanket-skip `PtrType`). Surfaced by /tpy-review (safety-model) of the deref-readonly-assignment fix.
- **[LOW small] An `interior` field's readonly-boundary exemption only fires for a direct `self.<field>.method()` call, not a chained receiver.** `tpyc/sema/methods.py` -- the `interior_receiver` guard that suppresses self-mutation demotion checks `isinstance(expr.obj, TpyFieldAccess) and expr.obj.accessed_field_is_interior`. A chained call whose receiver is not directly the interior field access -- e.g. `self._cell.get_inner().mutate()` (where `get_inner()` returns something rooted in the cell) -- would NOT be recognized as interior, so the mutation roots back to `self` and demotes the enclosing method. Not exploitable by current `Rc`/`Weak` code (every `_cell` call is single-level: `self._cell.incr_strong()` etc.), but fragile given `interior`'s semantics are stated generally ("mutations reached through the field"). Fix shape: climb a method-call chain to its field-access root when deciding `interior_receiver`, mirroring how `_root_name_of_expr` is transparent to accessor calls. Surfaced by /tpy-review (safety-model) of the interior-mutable feature.
- **[LOW small] `Rc[readonly[T]]`'s no-laundering guarantee has no C++-type backstop -- a `@native` function can mutate the payload.** `readonly[Rc[T]].clone()` yields `Own[Rc[readonly[T]]]` at the TPy level, but `ReadonlyType.to_cpp()` strips the modifier, so the C++ local is a plain `Rc<Counter>` whose mutable `get()` overload is callable. TPy sema enforces the readonly-payload contract (a TPy caller can't reach the mutable `get()`), but a `@native` function handed the C++ value, or any C++-level code, can. Pre-existing for `readonly[T]`-of-reference generally (readonly is a sema-layer/`const&`-param construct, not a deep C++ type), but materially worsened by the interior feature because `Rc`'s no-laundering soundness story rests on this invariant. No fix without a distinct C++ type for `Rc<const T>` (a real `Rc` instantiation over a const payload, vs the current modifier-strip); revisit if `@native` interop with `Rc[readonly[T]]` becomes load-bearing. Surfaced by /tpy-review (safety-model) of the interior-mutable feature.
- **[LOW small] `_view_source_is_temporary`'s method-call arm assumes a view-returning method borrows its RECEIVER, ignoring `return_borrows_from` arg indices.** `tpyc/sema/statements.py` -- for a `TpyMethodCall` whose return `is_borrowing_view_type`, the predicate recurses into `expr.obj` (receiver) only, unlike `is_dangling_return`'s method branch which checks each `return_borrows_from` index. A hypothetical view-returning method that borrows an ARG (not the receiver) with a stable receiver but a temporary arg would be classified non-temporary -> an inferred view local would NOT promote and an explicit `StrView` bind would not reject, binding a dangling view with no diagnostic. Latent: no method in scope today returns a view borrowing an arg rather than the receiver (str/bytes/Span views all borrow the receiver). Fix shape: mirror `is_dangling_return`'s per-index `return_borrows_from` traversal in `_view_source_is_temporary` (and ideally consolidate the two -- see the TODO.md duplication note). Surfaced by /tpy-review (safety-model) of the view-lifetime fix.
- **[MED] A str/bytes SELF-view (`a[3:9]`, `s.strip()`) silently dangles when its source local is rebound, because str/bytes self-views follow a warn-not-demote model and the warning misses plain rebinds.** `a = make(); v = a[3:9]; a = make()` (slice) and `s = make(); x = s.strip(); s = make()` (method) both leave `v`/`x` a `std::string_view` into the freed first buffer -- no diagnostic, no demotion -- diverging from CPython (where the local holds an independent value). Verified matrix: a **container-element** view (`x = c[0]`, list/dict/record) DEMOTES to owned on any source mutation OR rebind (sound, no warning); a **str/bytes self-view** (slice or view-returning method on a str/bytes local) instead only WARNS "while borrowed" on `+=` / method-mutation and is SILENT on a plain rebind -- so rebind falls through both guards. Two facts behind it: (1) the borrow-conflict warning treats augmented-assign / method-mutation as a conflict but not a plain rebind; (2) the view-vs-owned source-root tracking in `_infer_new_local_view_type` (`tpyc/sema/statements.py`) registers only `TpySubscript`/`TpyFieldAccess` *element* sources in `view_source_borrows_map`, never a str/bytes self-source (slice of a str local, or a view-returning call rooted at its receiver via `return_borrows_from`), so those views have no demotion path at all. Distinct from (and not a hole in) the compound-view-roots fix, which demotes correctly on rebind for the element sources it tracks. **Chosen fix (direction A -- the sound, model-unifying one):** register str/bytes self-source views in `view_source_borrows_map` rooted at the borrowed source (the sliced str/bytes local; a view-returning call's `return_borrows_from` receiver/args) so they DEMOTE to owned on mutation/rebind exactly like container-element views -- one model for all view sources. (The weaker alternative -- merely extending the borrow-conflict warning to rebinds -- was rejected: it labels the UB instead of removing it and keeps two divergent safety models.) This flips `tests/cases/view_lifetime/warn_view_source_mutation`'s str/slice cases from warn-and-view to demote-to-owned (the warnings become unnecessary, consistent with that test's own note that they are a stopgap pending last-use precision) -- an existing-snapshot change to confirm before regenerating. Surfaced by /tpy-fix-bug of the compound-view-source root tracking fix; own branch.
- **[MED small] A local bound from a native `-> V` reference-returning method aliases its source with no escape/dangling check.** Now that native `-> V` methods alias (the native-record exclusion was removed), `p = obj.accessor()` binds a `T*`/`T&` into `obj`'s storage -- but native stubs carry `return_borrows_from=None`, so `_register_call_result_borrow` registers no loan and `is_dangling_return(TpyMethodCall)` returns False. A local aliasing storage that then dies (local receiver) or is structurally mutated, or the alias returned/stored-in-field/inserted-into-container, escapes with no diagnostic. Broadened (not introduced) by the native-aliasing change -- before it the call copied, so there was no alias to track; same family as the dict-view field-escape entry below and the StrView/Span field gap. There is also no static check that a bare `-> V` native binding actually returns `V&` in C++ (a by-value binding mistyped `-> V` dangles silently -- caught here only because the full-suite audit happened to exercise it; see the `-> V`/`Own[V]` contract note in `docs/NATIVE_INTEROP.md`). Fix shape: give native `-> V` reference-returning methods a declared `return_borrows_from` (receiver borrow) so the escape/dangling checks have a root. Surfaced by /tpy-review (safety-model) of the native-return-convention fix.
- **[MED small] Storing a dict view in a FIELD escapes the view-lifetime checks: `self.view = d.items()` of a local dict dangles silently.** Returning a locally-backed view is caught (`is_borrowing_view_type` feeds provenance tracking and `is_dangling_return`), but field assignments bypass `check_dangling_reference`, and `check_escape` skips dict views because their TypeDef says `is_value_type=True`. Same pre-existing class as the StrView/Span field-storage gap, newly reachable for `dict_keys`/`dict_values`/`dict_items` since the views became first-class borrowing wrappers (dict-accessor aliasing work). Fix shape: route field stores of `is_borrowing_view_type` values through the same provenance check returns use (or reject storing views in fields outright until region tracking lands). Surfaced by /tpy-review (safety-model).
- **[LOW small] An `@readonly` method that mutates `self` THROUGH a callable arg gets no sema diagnostic.** `def poke(self): self.cb(self.data)` where `cb: Callable[[list[Int32]], None]` and the stored callback mutates its arg: the callable-value call correctly marks `self.data` mutated (so const inference demotes `poke` when undecorated), but an explicit `@readonly` decorator on `poke` is not rejected at sema -- the only backstop is the C++ const-method check at build time. Same class as mutation through external/builtin callee facts (a readonly method calling an unknown-mutation callee), and a sibling of the readonly-vs-callable-field entry in the Compiler-bugs section (`Wrap[T]` / `cmp_to_key`). **Fix shape:** when the eager opaque-callee mutation mark sets `current_self_mutated` inside a method whose `@readonly` is explicit (not deduced), emit the same "readonly method mutates self" diagnostic the direct-mutation path raises. Surfaced by /tpy-review (safety-model) of the callable-values fix.
- **[MED small] `is_rvalue_source` classifies a subscript on an RVALUE container as a borrow -- binding it takes a pointer/reference into a dying temporary.** `tpyc/value_category.py` (~line 100) returns False for any plain `TpySubscript` without recursing into the container's own rvalue-ness (unlike the `TpyFieldAccess` arm), so `v = f()[0]` / `(v := f()[0])` with `f() -> Own[list[T]]` emits `&(f()[0])` (or a `T&` bind) into the expired temporary. Pre-existing for statement bindings; the walrus borrow arm widens reach to expression positions (conditions, argument lists). Fix: mirror the field-access arm -- a subscript is an rvalue source when its container expression is (recurse into `expr.obj`); the binding then takes the owned-storage path. Surfaced by /tpy-review (safety-model) of the branch-decl borrow-fact materialization.
- **[MED medium] An `@overload`-grouped borrowing callee escapes the auto-move borrow gate in BOTH directions.** Calls into an overload group resolve to the MATCHED stub FunctionInfo, but body-analysis facts land only on the group's last entry (`get_function(name)[-1]`, see the finalize sites in `tpyc/sema/analyzer.py`): non-last stubs keep `return_borrows_from=None` forever. Backward-defined overloaded callees therefore never register the call-result borrow (the gate misses a real borrow -- silent use-after-move family), and the forward-reference pending set (`_seed_pending_borrow_fact_fis`) seeds only `[-1]`, so the conservative `OPAQUE` path also never fires for a stub-resolved call. **Fix shape:** propagate finalize facts to every group member -- but the param indices of `return_borrows_from` are per-signature, so the propagation needs an index mapping from the implementation's params to each stub's (or per-stub body analysis for bodied stubs); naive copying is wrong for reordered/defaulted overloads. Until then, seeding all group FIs into the pending set alone would over-demote backward-defined overloads (permanent OPAQUE) and must not ship by itself. Surfaced by /tpy-review (safety-model) of the forward-ref gate fix. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[MED medium] A generator METHOD's receiver borrow is not represented, so consuming the receiver while its generator lives escapes the auto-move gate.** The generator frame stores `__self` by reference, but `return_borrows_from` cannot carry `-1` for generator methods: `-1` blocks readonly inference (a self-borrowing return pins non-const), and stamping it flips every generator method non-readonly -- observed breaking const callers of stdlib `re.Pattern.finditer`. So `g = obj.walk(...); take(obj); next(g)` silently iterates a moved-from receiver (same family as the iterable-param case, which IS gated via the param indices). **Fix shape:** either represent the receiver borrow in a channel that readonly inference ignores (a separate `frame_borrows_self` fact consumed by `_register_call_result_borrow`), or make the readonly-inference check distinguish "returns self" from "frame references self with the method's own constness". Surfaced implementing the generator-method borrow facts (forward-ref gate review round). Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW medium] A borrowing callee in a cross-module import CYCLE escapes the auto-move borrow gate.** The same-module forward-reference hole is closed (the analyzer seeds `pending_borrow_fact_fis` so unanalyzed same-module callees register conservative `OPAQUE` borrows), but a cycle peer's function whose body is analyzed after the current module's leaves `return_borrows_from=None` at the bind with the FI outside the pending set -- no borrow is registered and a later consume of the argument still auto-moves while the bound result borrows it (silent use-after-move). Requires an import cycle plus a borrowing callee in the not-yet-analyzed peer; same staleness family as the cross-cycle readonly-method deferral. **Fix shape:** extend the pending-set seeding to cycle-peer modules (the compiler knows the SCC), or defer those binds to the workspace-wide finalize pass like cross-module borrow checks. Residual of the forward-ref gate fix (`fix-forward-ref-borrow-gate`). Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[MED medium] A borrow hidden inside a NESTED call argument escapes the auto-move borrow gate (asyncio.create_task shape).** `task = asyncio.create_task(total(xs))` -- `total`'s coroutine temporary stores `xs` by reference, `create_task` retains the coroutine inside the returned Task, but `_register_call_result_borrow` only relates the bound name to the OUTER call's `return_borrows_from`, so no `task -> xs` borrow is registered and a later consume of `xs` moves it while the pending task still iterates it: TPy prints `1 0` where CPython prints `1 6` (bind create_task, consume `xs`, `await task`), no diagnostics. Direct coroutine binds (`c = total(xs)`) are unaffected -- the coroutine must-use rule rejects them. The general form is any `outer(inner(xs))` where `inner` borrows and `outer` retains its argument. Note: async defs also do not set the generator-style `return_borrows_from` for their reference-stored params (`analyzer.py` gates on `is_generator` only); that fact alone is dead code today because of the must-use rule, so set it together with the transitive registration, not separately. **Fix shape:** when an argument expression is itself a call whose `return_borrows_from` roots at a name, register the outer call's bound result as borrowing that root (conservative: the callee may retain the temporary). Residual of the audit root-cause #5 fix. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[MED medium] A loop-var alias outliving its loop escapes the auto-move borrow gate: consuming the iterable post-loop dangles the alias.** `for x in xs: last = x` then `drop(xs)` then `last.read()` -- `last` is a reference into `xs`'s element storage, but the only BorrowTracker edge on `xs` is the `"__for_iter"` sentinel, which the gate excludes (it never expires and would block consuming-iteration activation), and the prescan alias map relates `last` to `x`, not to `xs` (the loop-var -> iterable relation lives only in `loop_var_iterable`). The post-loop consume therefore still auto-moves `xs` while `last` dangles -- same silent use-after-move family as the forward-referenced-callee entry above. **Fix shape:** at for-loop analysis, register the loop var (and transitively its aliases) as a NAMED element-borrower of the iterable -- not the shared sentinel -- so the gate sees it; or fold `loop_var_iterable` into the gate's known-borrow sources. Real fix is loop-scoped borrow expiry in the MIR place/loan model. Residual of the audit root-cause #5 fix, surfaced by the /tpy-ready retrospective. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[MED] Non-scalar match arm bindings are `auto&` borrows into the subject's storage; mutating the subject root in the arm body (or guard) dangles them.** The visible and method-call mutation shapes are now WARNED; only mutation through an ALIAS of the subject and via an opaque (unknown-readonly) method remain silent. FREE-COPY-SCALAR bindings (fixed int, bool, `Char`, float, enum) are COPIED at the arm, so they survive subject mutation and match CPython at zero cost (a register move) -- this closed the originally-verified [HIGH] shapes, whose bound fields were scalar (`Int32`). The binding form is a single sema fact (`bind_by_value` on each capture node, set from `is_free_copy_scalar` via the shared `iter_capture_bindings` traversal); codegen's `auto`/`auto&` choice AND the dangle warning both read it, so they cannot drift -- no capture path can silently alias a scalar (a missed path defaults to a warned `auto&`, and a codegen emit site without the per-arm map asserts). The copy is deliberately restricted to scalars: copying a heap-backed value type (`str`, `BigInt`), a view, or a reference type would pessimize the common path (performance is design goal #1) or diverge from CPython aliasing, so those keep the zero-cost `auto&` borrow and dangle if the subject root is mutated while the binding lives. The stopgap (`_warn_arm_subject_mutation`, gated on a non-scalar binding being present) now warns on: assignment to the subject path/owner prefix, an invalidating container method on the subject base (both synchronous), AND a non-readonly method call whose receiver prefix-aliases the subject (deferred to `finalize_borrow_checks` once readonly is settled; `resolve_pending_match_subject_checks`). Pointer-variant LOCAL subjects are safe (slot model). **Remaining SILENT residual:** mutation through an ALIAS of the subject (`p = h; p.swap()` / `p.pet = ...` -- needs alias tracking) and via an opaque method whose readonly verdict is unknown (`is_readonly is None`, e.g. some `@native`). **Sound fix:** register the surviving non-scalar arm bindings (and `__match_subject` for field/element sources) as loans of the subject root in the BorrowTracker for the arm's extent, and reject root mutation while they live -- a compile-time check, never a runtime copy (a copy would violate the performance goal for `str`/`BigInt`). Needs the MIR `Place`/`LoanInfo` model with arm-EXTENT-aware loans, which the current whole-function mutation facts cannot express (the deferred-check resolver is extent-blind) -- an IR-migration deliverable (see `docs/IR_DESIGN.md`). Dropped from [HIGH] to [MED] now that the verified silent shapes (scalar copy; non-scalar method-call) are closed; the remaining alias/opaque residual is rarer. Audit finding G2.
- **[LOW small, deferred: MIR] readonly is not projected onto a NESTED borrow-form tuple element on subscript.** `t[i]` where `t: readonly[tuple[..., tuple[Int32, Box], ...]]` returns the inner `tuple[Int32, Box]` without `readonly`, so a write through `t[i][1].field` is not rejected at sema. Root cause: the four subscript readonly-projection sites in `tpyc/sema/expressions.py` (tuple, container, protocol, record) all gate on `not elem.is_value_type()`, and `TupleType.is_value_type()` is unconditionally True -- so a tuple element (even one with borrow-form sub-elements) is treated as a copied value and skipped. The non-nested cases work (`readonly[tuple[Box, ...]]` projects `readonly[Box]`). Same family as the `readonly[list[tuple[...]]]` element-write entry above. **Fix shape:** unify the four gates to project readonly when the element is a borrow-form tuple (`TupleType.has_ref_elements()`), not only when `not is_value_type()`. Surfaced by /tpy-review (architecture-fit) of the readonly-tuple-projection fix.
- **[MED, deferred: MIR] General destructive move-out of aggregate members is unimplemented beyond the whole-rvalue-tuple-unpack slice.** Moving ownership *out* of an aggregate member is supported only for whole-tuple unpack of a fresh rvalue (`a, b = make_pair()` distributes each `Own[T]` element to a movable owned local -- shipped). Named-source whole-tuple unpack (`t = make_pair(); a, b = t`) is now supported (the source is moved into the temp at its last use). Still unsupported: partial element move-out (`take(pair[0])` leaving `pair[1]` live), record-field move-out (`take(rec.field)` for a `@nocopy` field), constant/dynamic-index move-out. These need per-place partial-move state tracking: representing `pair[0]` / `rec.field` as individually movable places with their own consumed flags, propagating "partially moved" up to the aggregate (so the whole value can't be used/moved once a part is), flow-sensitive merge of partial-move state across branches, use-after-partial-move diagnostics, and rejection of un-trackable dynamic-index moves -- the `Place(Local/Field/Index/...)` move-state model the borrow-tracker/MIR notes describe as future work. Reading members in place (borrow) is unaffected. Surfaced implementing the narrow whole-tuple-unpack slice (Send/Sync Phase 4 channel). **Fix shape:** add per-place move-state tracking to the borrow/move analyzer; until then multi-owner handoff routes through whole-tuple unpack or `Rc.clone()`. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug` (large; architectural).
- **[LOW small]** A generator that captures a `str` parameter as a `std::string_view` and is iterated later than its source's lifetime dangles, with no diagnostic. `re.Pattern.finditer` (now a true generator) takes `subject: str` and holds the view across suspensions; `for m in p.finditer(make_temp_string()): ...` (or storing the generator and iterating after the source string is gone) reads freed storage. Instance of the unenforced view-lifetime model (the type system doesn't tie a captured view's lifetime to the generator object). Pre-existing in kind, but the generator form widens the window vs the old eager-`list` `finditer`, which consumed the view immediately. Reachable for any generator (free function or method) that captures a `str`/view param and outlives the argument. Fix shape belongs with the broader view-lifetime work (tie captured-view provenance to the returned generator's lifetime). Surfaced by /tpy-review (safety-model) of the finditer-as-generator change.
- **[LOW small]** A generator that captures a `*args` varargs pack by value and then escapes its defining frame dangles, with no diagnostic. `_build_capture_list` (`tpyc/codegen_cpp/gen_generators.py`) captures a `tpy::varargs<...>` pack by value into the generator lambda; the pack's `indirect_` member points into the caller's `__tmp_N` stack array (`std::array<T*, N>` -- the primary `varargs<T>` template's indirect form, NOT the direct-only value specialization). `__tmp_N` lives at the caller's frame scope, so iterating the generator within that frame is sound (the common `for x in merge(...)` / `list(merge(...))`), but a generator that ESCAPES the frame -- `g = merge(a, b); return g`, or storing it in a longer-lived container -- leaves the captured pack pointing at freed stack storage. No TPy diagnostic. Same unenforced view-lifetime family as the captured-`str`-view generator entry above; the type system doesn't tie the captured pack's lifetime to the generator object. Reachable for any generator with a varargs param that outlives its call frame. Fix shape belongs with the broader view-lifetime / region work (tie captured-pack provenance to the returned generator's lifetime) -- IR-gated, same region/lifetime family as the deferred create_task escape. Surfaced by /tpy-review (safety-model) of heapq.merge's varargs generator.
- **[LOW small] (precision, not unsound)** A `@native` method emitted through the frontend-IR plugin path (`tpyc/frontend_ir/lower.py` `_route_decorators` -> `BUILTIN_LOWERING(tpy.native)`) cannot express `native_preserves_refs`. That property is set by a *separate* decorator (`@native_preserves_refs`, qname `tpy.extern.native_preserves_refs`) which is not in `CORE_DECORATORS`, so a plugin can't emit it -- `_route_decorators` rejects it as an unknown decorator. `_is_invalidating_method` (`tpyc/sema/methods.py`) reads `native_preserves_refs` only for *builtin* records; plugin-emitted natives are user-defined and take the `direct_self_mutated` fallback, so the borrow checker conservatively treats every plugin-lowered native method as invalidating iterators/borrowed refs, potentially yielding spurious invalidation diagnostics. Latent: no in-tree plugin emits native methods yet. Fix shape: add a `native_preserves_refs` entry to `CORE_DECORATORS` (or a `_NativeSpec` kwarg) and thread it onto `TpyFunction.native_preserves_refs` in lowering. Surfaced by `/tpy-review` (safety-model).
- **[LOW small] Temporary-rvalue-into-view-storage is rejected for `list`/`set` `append`/`add`/`insert` only; the sibling sinks are uncovered.** The view-lifetime fix (audit #10 item 5) rejects `list[StrView].append(make())` / `set.add` / `list.insert` of a temporary, but the same dangling-view store is still silently accepted for: `dict[StrView, ...]` / `dict[..., StrView]` literal keys/values and `d[k] = make()`, `list.extend([make()])`, list/set/dict LITERAL elements (`[make()]` typed `list[StrView]`), and `tuple`/comprehension element stores. (StrView FIELD stores are the separate Future field-lifetime feature, below.) Param/local-derived views stored into an ESCAPING container are also still accepted -- that needs container-lifetime (region) analysis, not just rvalue rejection. **Fix shape:** route every element-into-view-storage coercion through one chokepoint that rejects a temporary source (extend the `_view_source_is_temporary` check at the literal-element and dict/extend coercion sites); overlaps audit #11 (literal elements copy). Surfaced implementing audit #10 item 5. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[MED small] An explicit `s: str = <view>` annotation is silently downgraded to view storage, binding owned-copy temporary into a `std::string_view` -- dangling.** `def use(sv: StrView): s: str = sv` emits `std::string_view s = std::string(sv);` -- the `str` (owned) annotation is overridden to view storage by the pending-view machinery (`is_view_compatible_source` returns view-safe for a view-typed source, so `initialized_from_owned=False`), yet the source coercion still materializes an owned `std::string(sv)` temporary; the result is a view into a destroyed temporary. Same for `s: str = make().strip()`. Root cause: the pending-view storage decision ignores an explicit OWNED annotation (it should force owned storage when the user wrote `str`/`bytes`), and the storage-vs-coercion choice is uncoordinated. Pre-existing; surfaced probing the view-lifetime cluster (audit #10). **Fix shape:** when the local has an explicit owned (`str`/`bytes`) annotation, resolve to owned storage unconditionally (no view downgrade); keep the owned-copy coercion. Distinct from the inferred-local promotion (#10 item 3), which routes `initialized_from_owned=True` to owned storage and is unaffected. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] (precision, not unsound)** `await x` reads the awaitable's `__poll__` `is_readonly` at Phase-1 analysis time (`_analyze_await` -> `_extract_awaitable_inner`, `tpyc/sema/expressions.py`); auto-readonly is a Phase-2 fixpoint fact. A *user-defined* awaitable whose `__poll__` is only inferred `@readonly` in Phase 2 (no `@readonly` annotation, no direct mutation) reads `is_readonly=False` here, so `_mark_await_operand_mutated` over-marks the awaited operand's root -- blocking the enclosing async method's auto-readonly inference even though awaiting that awaitable doesn't actually mutate it. Over-approximation: conservative and never unsound (a method wrongly kept non-readonly, never wrongly readonly), and stdlib awaitables (`Task`/`Future`/`Event`/`SleepFuture`) all mutate in `__poll__` so they're unaffected. Fix shape: defer the await-mutation mark to Phase 2 via a call edge (mirroring `_is_self_call_deferred` for `self.method()`), or consult the Phase-2-finalized `__poll__` readonly verdict. Surfaced by /tpy-review (safety-model) of the await-self-mutation fix.
- **[LOW small] (by design, not a regression) Post-sema `replace_expr` injects AST that bypasses sema re-analysis.** `PostSemaFunctionMacroContext.replace_expr` (inherited from `FunctionMacroContext`, `tpyc/macro_api.py`) splices nodes after pass 7, so the injected nodes get no mutation-fact collection (no `call_edges` for new calls -> Phase-2 propagation can miss a mutating call -- `PostSemaFunctionMacroContext.note_param_mutated` is a manual escape hatch for the param-rooted mutating-call sub-case, but it only seeds `direct_mutated_params`, not `direct_structural_mutated_params`, so an emitted *structurally*-mutating call (append/insert/clear on a param) is still unrecorded), no borrow-check registration, no `deref_check` insertion, and no narrowing/readonly enforcement. The contract is documented (the caller must `set_expr_type` every emitted node and is responsible for safety), and this is inherent to the whole macro system (pre-sema `replace_expr`, builder-trace `emit_function`, and class macros already inject un-rechecked AST) -- a known limitation, not a defect introduced here. A macro that injects only value-type expressions (the shipped test) is safe. **Fix shape (future):** restrict the post-sema surface to value-type replacements (enforce via `type_of`/`set_expr_type`), or re-run the relevant sema checks over injected subtrees once the IR makes that cheap. Surfaced by /tpy-review (safety-model) of the post-sema function-macro phase.
